## SOL KESI

Speculative Evolution Bestiary

Dedicated to Dayne, the only world I need

<!-- image -->

There will come soft rains and the smell of the ground, And swallows circling with their shimmering sound;

And frogs in the pools singing at night, And wild plum trees in tremulous white;

Robins will wear their feathery fire, Whistling their whims on a low fence-wire;

And not one will know of the war, not one Will care at last when it is done.

Not one would mind, neither bird nor tree, If mankind perished utterly;

And Spring herself, when she woke at dawn Would scarcely know that we were gone.

Sara Teasdale 1884 - 1933

<!-- image -->

Isolated  for  eons,  Sol'Kesh  is  an  island  without  humanity,  without  a  species  to spark  flame  and  conquer.  A  primal  landscape  of  predator  and  prey,  clawing  at the  edge  of  extinction,  where  a  single  law  of  life  reigns  supreme,  evolve  or  die

Competition  breeds  adaptation  and  an  immense  variety  of  life  has  taken  root  and flourished.  From  miles  of  exposed  coral  reefs,  sunken  forests  and  fungi-lit  caves  that riddle the earth below. Wild and unique flora and fauna struggle in an evolutionary arms race, fighting for their next breath, their next meal and for their next generation to survive

Welcome to Sol'Kesh,  The  Speculative  Evolution Bestiary

<!-- image -->

## An Evolving Preface

Sol'Kesh began with a cynical hope - that every life ends, but life itself never does.

At the height of the pandemic, as countries and people failed to contain, control, and then care, it was all too easy to feel a sort of hopelessness. Within that brief moment where humanity slowed, and nature breathed out a sigh of relief - the arthopause, as they later called it, a feeling of hope I never expected began to grow. Whales sang louder, migration patterns changed, nocturnal predators did not hide from the dawn. In that moment I felt that life was larger than us, that it would be fine long after us, and I then felt there could be a world to create for my creatures.

I found there is a certain harmony in speculative evolution world building within a roleplaying setting. Creating a setting for an RPG is not the same as writing a story, it is instead about creating tools for others to use, a sandbox to build and play in. More than a land with history, this setting needed to feel like a living, breathing world, one that exists beyond the travelers that set foot upon it, and through speculative evolution's method of building how and why each plant and creature evolved to fit within the niche of their ecosystem, it did just that.

" In nature, nothing exists alone " - Rachel Carson, The Silent Spring .

Very little describes the experience of speculative evolution as much as that quote, and the core value of interconnected life - that each plant and animal plays a part in the environment is key to building a believable world for others to explore, and how each creation sparks the question: what does it eat? For every predator there is prey. If the roaming carnivore has long loping legs, then it must have developed that form to chase nimble prey, which then raises the question on how a fleet-footed prey could keep from the slavering jaws, while still grazing off the far ranging fields it strides across. Before long, a scene coalesces of low lying grassy fields under a cloudless sky, dozens of peaceful herd beasts chewing the soft blades before a snapped twig alerts them to flee from the now charging beast, its claws ripping up the loamy soil as it closes in.

While this may only be a sliver of the life that can be inspired by a possible future long after humanity, I thank you for being part of this journey and exploring the island of Sol'Kesh.

## Stepping On Silent Shores

Exploring the Island &amp; Th e Anatomy of a Stat Block

Sol'Kesh, a sanctuary for life upon a ceaseless horizon spanning ocean, inhabited by plants and animals that have had to evolve and struggle to survive isolated for millions of years. Countless species contend for a niche of their own, whether that be the relative safety of burrowing beneath the soil, outpacing any predator that stalks the grass-strewn prairies, or growing tall and rising above the canopy to unfurl broad emerald leaves and soak in the nourishing rays of the sun. Th ere is a primal balance on this island, a pristine environment with fi  ve distinct biomes hosting unique flora and fauna to discover. How they respond to the Players ( Travelers ) that step on these untouched lands depends greatly on how the Travelers treat them in kind.

Similar to our own distant history, when mankind fi  rst stepped across the Bering Strait into an unseen and untouched new world, these ancient explorers walked amongst towering megafauna that would not have known what to make of the sharpened stick wielding fur-clad bipeds. Sol'Kesh offers the same primal exploration, replete with beasts that will respond to these intruders with curiosity, fear, and hunger. With new horizons to tread, new discoveries to be made, Sol'Kesh unveils a land to explore, to claim, to hunt.

In this bestiary, a Game Master ( Keeper ) will fi   nd everything they need to help guide their storytelling within this natural setting. With a wide selection of creatures, locations, and quests, a Keeper can bring this primitive land to life unlike any other. For a Keeper, there's a multitude of ways to use the bestiary in their own games, from basing this isolated setting for their world building, adding it as new land to be explored in one of their own or existing fantasy worlds, or simply plucking choice creatures from the pages to populate their own stories.

For Travelers, while it may add to the mystery to experience these creatures for the fi  rst time, just the same way the beasts will experience the strangers within their lands, there is much to be found within these pages to enhance their experience. For a Traveler need not only slay the beasts within these pages, for there is the means to tame and befriend, craft and cook, and offers unique feats to aid them in navigating this wild land.

<!-- image -->

Th e Sol'Kesh Speculative Evolution Bestiary is 5e compatible, meaning it plays seamlessly with Dungeons &amp; Dragons , 5th Edition and would require the Dungeons and Dragons Core Rulebooks to be played correctly. While most of the rules within this book align with 5e, there are some unique features to Sol'Kesh that are highlighted below. Th ese additional features add flavor to the setting and creatures of Sol'Kesh, and their use is not mandatory in anyway.

## Traits

Keen Eyes Th e  stonecrest  sparrow  has  Advantage  on  Wisdom (Perception) Checks that rely on sight.

Flutter Flight Th e stonecrest sparrow doesn't provoke an Opportunity Attack when it flies out of an enemy's reach.

Defensive Instinct (1/Day) If it survives any attempt at being attacked. On its turn, Wisdom Saving Th row: DC 11, the stonecrest sparrow. Success: Th e  stonecrest  sparrow  Shattering  Chirp  Attack  is automatically  recharged  and  it  makes  that  attack  as  a  Bonus Action if there is an enemy creature within range.

## Creature Behavior

To  further  guide  Keepers  and  Solo  Travelers  on  the  natural demeanor of the beast, each creature has a Behavior Archetype: Aggressive, Defensive, Wary . Th is unique feature is explained on Page 256 and describes how the creatures would respond and react to an encounter with Travelers. Th is ties directly into Instinct. Instinct  represents  a  creature's  natural  reaction  when  faced with danger and desperation. When certain criteria are met, the Keeper rolls for the creature to either succeed or fail its Instinct Test. If the result is activated, it then must use its Instinct Ability before  any  other  Action.  Many  creatures  have  unique Instinct abilities that are directly related to their Behavior archetype.

## Scavenging

Each  adult  creature  found  in  the  Sol'Kesh  Bestiary  has  three Scavenging  Components,  while  the  rare  and  legendary  Apex creatures  have  six.  Not  all  Scavenged  Components  require  the Travelers to slay the creature, and these Components can be sold or used in Crafting, Preparing Meals and Hatching Eggs .  Each component features the type of item that it can be used for, the name  of  the  component,  its  cost  and  the  DC  Test  required  to Scavenge it. Th is unique feature is further explained on Page 246.

## Taming

Creatures  in  Sol'Kesh  are  more  than  just  encounters  to  slay,  a Traveler  that  befriends  or  captures  a  creature  can  eventually ally  with  it  through  persistent Taming. Th is  unique  feature  is represented by the Bond or Control stat, requiring certain levels to be obtained before they become Tamed and a life long ally of the Traveler. Th is unique feature is further explained on Page 251.

## Encountering

Th is  lore  text  further  expands  on  the  creature's  behavior  and helps  guide  the  Keeper  in  methods  in  which  the  Travelers  may encounter the creature in its natural habitat.

## Beneath The Wings Of A Mother

Navigating the Island of Sol'Kesh

Warm, salt-tinged winds rush towards the lone isle, a bastion of life after countless miles of ceaseless waves. White wings widely spread to rise along with it as the seabird glides over weaves of coral. Spirals of color, teeming with crustaceans fight over every inch of exposed coral reaching past the water, creating great strands of calcified land that sparkles in the high noon sun. For a while the bird glides over this bustling seascape, feeling it unsafe to land as even the corals themselves writhe to catch prey. She soon spots true land as the relentless waves break against stone cliffs. A vinnirex pup, its maw covered in blood from the slain scavick its kin recently caught, looks up at the white winged bird, and turns its head to follow her flight across the rising hills of woodlands to the north.

With a steady beat of wings she scales high over the dense canopy, a deeply uncomfortable feeling of danger felt when she looks down at the undulating green mass of leaves. Suddenly, sunlight glints off a stream of liquid that fires off from below, arcing thankfully too low. Her fear, a combination of instinct and deep seeded nature are justified as a green scaled ynnil glowers from the canopy it clings to; its potential meal now long out of range. For miles the seabird flies over thick forest. In the distance to the east, a loud crash of trees can be heard, one of tearing branches and falling trunks, causing small quakes disturbing birds and insects in their thousands, the seabird sees this and keeps her distance. For the white winged bird has an ancient pathway to follow, one she has never navigated herself but feels its direction ingrained in her genetic memory.

Exhaustion leeches the seabird's strength with every beat of her wings as she soars higher. Grass and dirt transition to slate gray stone beneath her, snow drifts thicken as she rises higher still over rushing herds of thick shelled insects. Though it chills her white feathers, the west bound winds push her onward, until at last, sailing over outcrops untouched by the tread of beasts for eons, the last snow covered peak breaks and she sees her ancestral destination.

Passing the last peak with a release of absolute exhilaration, she flows downward with the land, feeling the wind whip past her outstretched feathers, quickly descending into rolling hills and silver rivers. Finally, the seabird sees the distant island she had sought all this time, the place of her birth and the one she defied so many odds to return to lies before her. Nestled in the bay, like a sanctuary, rests an outcropping of stone rings steeply rising from the waters, entirely covered in hundreds of her kin. Their cries are a cacophony of warmth and welcome, an instinctual bloom of warmth that other creatures may call happiness. The brilliant blue crests from an array of males, beckon her to the nests they prepared for her and other potential mates, as the white winged bird makes her final descent. The exhausted seabird's eyes spot a nest with just the right amount of smooth stones and a male with as vibrant and strong crest as she could ever ask for. With a final beat of her wings, she settles into the finely crafted nest and clacks her beak, feeling fulfilled.

<!-- image -->

## Seasons upon the Shores

Rising from the temperate oceans north of the equator, the island of Sol'Kesh has been isolated from the main continents for eons, their landmasses far distant and unseen. Any creature born on these shores may think this is the world in its entirety and, besides long distance migratory animals, it is. These creatures, far removed from their continental origins, have drastically changed through isolation and island gigantism, creating a unique ecosystem that finds each living species competing for every niche to call their own and propagate.

The climate is often comfortably mild, warm currents bloom with life across its teeming shores. While the summer months can get blistering hot, particularly on the dry wastes of the western Grayfynn plains. The east on the other hand, commonly enjoys steady rainfall that enriches the fertile soil, though this causes many swamplands to fester along the north and south. Fall sees an incredible change of color throughout the woods that blanket the west, and up in the mountain steppes, Last Bloom's coniferous trees retain their green needles throughout the chill of winter. The cold months that follow soon slow down the arthropod megafauna and forces many herbivores to migrate away from the higher altitude lands into the warmer fields and prairies nearer the lowland shores. Though there are days of snowfall, it rarely lasts long enough to settle on the ground beyond the deep coverage seen on the central mountain range of the Splitspine mountain and Tundrus highlands, where the draffers hunt in droves. By spring, it is rare not to find a day without rain in the west, the gloom of the sky ushering in an absolute bloom of fungi that dominate the landscape with a dank vibrancy that creates a foundation for all life to grow from.

<!-- image -->

## •ISLAND OF SOL'KESH

<!-- image -->

300 MILES

<!-- image -->

Th e rhythmic sound of the waves crashing along the exposed coral that criss-crosses along the expanse of the coast greets the Traveler with a sense of serene peace as they approach the shores of Sol'Kesh. As the moon lowers over the brightening horizon, spiraling mounds of coral slowly reveal iridescent buds, expanding into dancing fronds of tendrils that bloom gold to meet the day, their gametes releasing and floating in the wind like motes of gentle dawn light. Down the coast, pillars of coral release theirs in kind, like a river in the sky, the gametes flow above the waves in a golden stream of life.

Th is fleeting moment of nature's beauty is interrupted by the sound of chitinous skittering and the taint of rot spoiling the salt laden wind. Scuttling in their thousands, tiny ravenous crustaceans crawl over to the decaying daughon, as its being pulled ashore by three chittering illithisks with their hooked forelimbs.

Interested in documenting the new exciting life, the Traveler steps closer to witness, only for the clawed scavengers to all fall eerily still at once. In a single moment of unnerving silence, their black, stalked eyes swivel in unison to the lone intruder. Discarding the rotting mammal, they claw forward for the promise of a much fresher meal. Alarmed, the Traveler quickly steps back and looks for a means for defense. Spying a red stalk of coral strand, half sunk into the sand, they quickly grasp the staff of bone-like coral and prepares to ward off the incoming chitinous horde.

Adapting to change is the only means for survival, and in no place else has this been more true than the coast. As the tide rises and falls with the steady passing of the moon above, as waters recede over eons of changing global temperatures, life has adapted to this intertidal zone unlike any other. With coral reefs transforming whole stretches of the ocean floor, spreading their colorful sanctuary for miles, the build up of their bones for generations pushed some species up past the waves to fi  nd purchase. Th ese pioneers, like skyscrapers from a bygone age of ruin, stretched into the salt sprayed air. Th ey became home to countless winged creatures, as saturated with life as below, and soon colonized the land, weaving great strands of coral to both support and encroach on the sandy beaches and cliffs they now claim as their own.

In  a  biome  entirely  governed  by  the  tide,  much  is  revealed  when  it  recedes,  revealing  wide  strands  of  coral,  pools  of  briny  water teaming with life, and the voracious scavengers that rush across the newly exposed beach to pluck at the trapped creatures before the  tide  returns.  More  so  than  the  biomes  inland,  the  strands  are  rife  with  opportunity.  No  other  creatures  embody  this  than the  cerrophol,  gharril,  and  illithisk.  Th ese  rather  devious  creatures  take  great  advantage  of  their  amphibious  nature,  of  their environment and strike  out  to  snag  prey  whenever  they  have  the  chance.  Cerrophol,  adapting  to  life  outside  the  water  for  short periods  of  time,  climb  into  isolated  tidal  pools  and  devastate  the  cornered  prey  easily.  Gharril  feast  on  the  beached  remains  of large  fi   sh  and  mammals,  accumulating  an  ossifi  ed  pearl  within  their  maw  to  spit  out  in  a  lethal  barrage  if  disturbed  from  their meal. And the illithisk, with its scything arms folded in a nearly apologetic manner, lashes out at terrestrial prey, dragging them into the salty depths to drown. Although the predators and scavengers have made this mercurial biome dangerous, at night when the  corals  bloom  to  release  their  free  floating  gametes  to  sparkle  on  the  moonlit  air,  it  is  truly  something  beautiful  to  witness.

## Traveling Along the Strands

Along stretches of coral that bridge islands, countless creatures live  on  and  under  the  surface.  Th ese  strands  harbor  a  dense ecosystem that, to an unwary Traveler, can spell disaster. As many of the corals that inhabit the Strands react differently when out of the water, some even resorting to carnivorous behavior when they  can't  rely  on  a  languid  fi  lter  feeding  existence.  Th ough treacherous, even to walk along safely, there are unique resources that can be found on the strandsfor an adventurous sort: living plates  of  easily  carved  coral,  dozens  of  venomous  and  stinging creatures to be harvested, and hundreds of unique species that make a home in this mercurial landscape.

Th e  Strands are named for the long bridges of coral that weave in  on  themselves  to  rise  above  the  waves  and  encroach  on  the shore. Traversing this type of living landscape is diffi  cult as the criss-crossing pillars are festooned with symbiotic growths, both gnarly and easy to grip, as well as slippery slime molds that can easily cause a misstep.

To  safely  explore  this  environment,  a  Traveler  must  shift  their perspective  into  seeing  this  land  as  not  split  between  land  and sea, but as one. Life has evolved to take advantage of the shifting tide,  predators  become  prey  as  waters  recede,  pulling  aquatic creatures into the suffocating air and vice versa. And so someone foreign  to  this  quickly  changing  relationship  must  be  ready  to swim and climb when necessary, to know when a plant or coral is  ready  to  be  harvested,  and  when  it  is  harvesting  in  kind.

A  nephyte  crustacean  inches  its  way across the sand, attempting to escape into the relative safety of a nearby tidal pool

## Windward

Ocean-borne winds break across the high cliffs that blow outwards onto ever smaller outcrops of land. Th ese shattered pieces of stone are far from lonely islands as coral bridge across them in web-like growths. Terrestrial corals creep up from the tidal line in evervibrant displays of color and life, teeming with crustaceans and darting fi  sh that weave around the coral strands and glowing white tentacles of eloom beneath the waves. As the sun rises to its zenith, a riotous pod of daugon can be heard over the crashing waves. Pulling their heavy, streamlined bodies onto the overlapping strands they lay out in the sun peacefully, their eyes closed and dorsal sails raised to soak in the heat before attempting another deep dive, thousands of feet beneath the sunlit surface to hunt for their favorite squid. Excited caws herald descending black beaked fi  ngulls that eagerly pick off any sand mites from the resting mammals, their loud chattering alerting a nearby cerrophol that it may get an opportunity to snatch an unwary pup.

<!-- image -->

## Shifting Lands Of Salt And Sand

Strands Biome Locations

## Astradale

A crown of stones befitting a god rises within the northern bay, shards of earth that broke from the firmament in a land shattering impact from ages past now ring this inner sea. Strands of coral have colonized these stones, their bone-like webs stretching across in  overlapping  bridges,  while  a  teeming  horde  of  crustaceans crawl in their thousands. For those willing to venture across the expanse of water from the mainland, the Astradale grows some of the most durable and supple coral plate, nearly unblemished by barnacles and rich in color. Though this is not without risk as sentry-like arkiopti, wading on the water's surface spit their lethal payload of stones at seabirds that make their home at the top of the broken spires, or any others that trespass.

## Bastion

Encircling the bay of Astradale rises a chain of islands that buffer the strong southbound headwinds. With a near constant crash of waves, the cliffs of Bastion are bleak and bereft of life, only upon the  peaks  of  the  tallest  islands  do  the  exposed  corals  flourish, raising wide frills of tentacles into the salt-tinged air to collect the particulates and algae that circulate within the sea spray. North of the cliffs, schools of tiama can be found gliding over the whitetipped waves in elegant, long finned flights, plucking fish from the sea in their low slung mouths with ease; their gracefully soaring, silver bodies, a picture of beauty as they flit through the waves, glittering in the daylight sun. Until the abrupt interruption of a dark shadow from above, an obsidian winged skyelarx plunging their taloned claws into flesh, then flying back to their aerie above the shattering waves.

## Eventide - REVISE

The calm waters of Eventide evoke a sense of peace and tranquility, fields of kelp stretch far beneath the red tinged waters as the sun sets  on  the  western  coast.  Motes  of  light  twinkle  beneath  the waves and over the serene sound of the tide, a family of vinnirex frolic  and  fight  over  the  carcass  of  a  slain  goliarch.  Though  the surface  remains  quiet,  a  monstrously  sized  prawn  searches  for prey to sate its voracious appetite beneath the waves. Fortunately for the plethora of sea grass, these predators have repelled most herbivores, allowing the flora to grow rich and wide spread. While this form of over-predation would often not be sustainable for an ecosystem, by late summer the large, ravenous crustaceans will then turn on each other, decimating their own numbers until only the  largest  and  most  ferocious  remain  to  enter  a  new  stage  in their life cycle and mate.

## Halfmoon Sands - REVISE

Stretching  out  from  the  north,  the  tide  recedes  to  reveal  miles of  smooth  sands,  leaving  pock-marked  tidal  pools  trapping  all manner of creatures in their salt water havens, the ocean breeze barely  masking  the  scent  of  exposed  sea  life.  On  these  often sunken dunes, the coral outgrowths are minimal, and roaming bands of illithisk prowl. Their swiveling stalked eyes investigating every nook for fresh prey, wary of the swarms of lanceleth that may spring from each. While this is the most ideal area for prying pearls from clams, daellash have been known to reside within the deeper pools, clamping down on prey with their wide mouths and pulling them beneath into their tunnels below. Although predators are usually the most obvious dangers in these wild lands, on the Halfmoon Sands it is the creeping, gradual rise of the tide. For a Traveler that has ventured out could find themselves quickly too far from the shore in a rising flood of water.

## Shattermouth

The archipelago of islands that form from the onrushing Shiverrun is one of the more dangerous areas in all of Sol'Kesh. The rapids collide with the crashing waves of the sea, leaving little but the most hardy of creatures to survive. Numerous fresh water rauka ward off the more aggressive flora from venturing up the river and instead plague the fractured coast. Using the numerous undersea caves and tunnels that weave through the islands, esslinter and daellash compete for the safety within these confines. Lanceleth swarms, using the spray of the water to hide their small forms, wait in ambush along every outcrop of stone to leap and pierce prey, both aquatic and terrestrial prey. While at night, when the tide drowns out all sound but the crash of the waves, the malefic slannethiss swims between the sharp outcrops of stone and coral, violently ripping esslinter from their burrows and dashing them upon the stones above the waves, their horned face covered in the viscous remains of salt and blood.

Thick  stabbing  pincers  can  quickly grab  a  morsel  of  flesh  when  a cariver  slams  into  larger  prey

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

## Stranded Dawn

A landscape apart from any other on Sol'Kesh, the Stranded Dawn is made entirely from coral strands. These bone-like bridges crisscross over each other from the ocean floor to breach the waves and encroach on the sandy islands. Incredibly rich in both flora and fauna, thousands upon thousands of birds flock to these perforated shores to feast. Gharril, cerrophol and illithisk roam the uneven surface, standing above the chittering hordes of crabs and lobsters. Just beneath the surface, spiera dart and flow around the overlapping strands of coral while goliarch claim whole swathes of reefs as their hunting grounds. Although this is one of the better grounds to find coral plate, many of the brilliant hued coral have adapted to protect themselves above the waves, from retracting their feeding tentacles when they sense the tremor of large creatures, to lashing out with neurotoxin laced spines at the slightest touch. This is a landscape on the leading edge of evolution and the competition between predator and prey is as fierce as nature can offer.

<!-- image -->

## Flora and Fauna of the Strands The Ebb And Flow Of Evolution

A single eye blinks away salty grit and a sigh bubbles up from the wet sand. Half submerged on the beach, the gharril looks to the shore waiting, while the rising tide crashes on its shell. A pack of vinnirex leap and play on the sand, two young pups fighting over a violet branch of coral that broke off from a recent storm. More bubbles escape the maw of the large snail as its patience wears thin. The sun sets further, the sky overhead spreads crimson and the waters continue to rise. With a splash, the much chewed upon coral branch lands near the awaiting cerrophol. With an instinct built on eons of success, its large eye recedes into the soft flesh of its face to look like just any outcropping of coral. With a yap and bark, one of the young canines paddle out to retrieve their toy, only for four grasping tentacle-like appendages to burst out of the water and grip the small furred mammal. With one last bark, it is pulled under, and the rising tide covers the top of the snail's twisted shell.

By the rise and fall of the tide, the creatures of the coast prowl this highly competitive stretch of habitable land that shifts wildly between dawn and dusk leaves little room for calm herbivores, and instead plays host to a constant struggle between scavengers. As the receding waves expose long stretches of coral reefs that after eons have expanded past the waves to crawl onto land like questing, outstretched fingers, many of the advantageous, amphibious creatures stalk out to claim trapped prey. With the telltale sound of a slapping fin, a fish trapped in a tidal pool calls to the chitinous horde of crustaceans and slithering cephalopod that are found teeming on nearly every stretch of sand around the island. Striding above the wave of smaller beasts, illithisk, cerrophol and gharril compete for the morsel of flesh, their struggle often leading to one of their kind being left in the sand for the scurrying scavengers to feast on instead. As this competition carries ever onward like the ebb and flow of the tide, a lone slannethiss lifts its sensory horns into the salted air, awaiting larger, more worthy prey. Out beyond the beach, wide swathes of coral border much of the island, these colorful bone-like columns are covered in urchins, mollusks and smaller corals. Like darting birds among trees, spiera shoals weave in and out of the branching coral snatching fish with ease, giving the long daellash a wide berth, with their wide mouths snapping at any passing prey. Further out still, tread arkiopti, sharped stones slicing through the air as they aim to fell a gliding tiama while a goliarch rushes upward to eviscerate the lone large crustacean. There is no peace among the flora and fauna that struggle to survive the crashing waves, and the Strands offer no easy welcoming to Travelers.

## Bruish Length - 1 ft. 8 in.

Protected by a length of fatty tissue and a stinging  needle  along  its  back,  the omnivorous fish uses its barbels to brush through sand and sediment for sea grass and snails to feast upon peacefully.

## Deep Dawn Height - 5 ft. 4 in.

Often the only source light on the seafloor, the leafy plant's bioluminescent hydrophily pollen glows an eerie yellow. Attracting all manner of fish to hunt by its illumination

## Ferastack Height - 3 ft. 2 in.

An enormous sponge that filters  saltwater  through its spire and ejects desalinated water upward. The interior is home to a rich micro  biome

## Lilyplum Height - 8 ft. 2 in.

Fruiting bodies cluster below the lilyplum's leaf in a protective cover, safe from the tumultuous ocean surf.  Herbivorous fish pluck the fruit and spread their seeds out onto the seafloor in their excrement for  the  next  generation  of  lilyplum  to  take  root, before  maturing  to  rise  to  the  surface  and  float upon the current

<!-- image -->

## Sea Bead Height - 3 ft. 5 in

Providing a salty tang flavored fruit that rises from the sea floor. Egg-shaped fruit blossom at the end of thick rich green stalks, enticing large  mouthed  fish  to  swallow  them  whole and disperse their seeds far out into the ocean

<!-- image -->

## Grinfin Length - 2 ft. 2 in.

With a build up calcium carbonate from  a  diet  of  chewing  on  coral, the fish's teeth are nearly unbreakable.  Which  can  lead  to a  rather  nasty  bite  if  this  fish  is disturbed during its feasts

<!-- image -->

Coral Plate Height - 3 ft.

Coral  plate  grows  quickly  and  is  ideal  for  carving  into  durable  and  supple nearly living material. The dome-like structure is made of layers of calcium carbonate  with  a  microscopic  thin  space  of  air  between  them,  that  can withstand damage by radiating out the blunt force energy outwards into its layers. Unfortunately for this specimen, a worm-like booril fish has chosen it as its home, and can spend years chewing through the same coral plate, slowly killing it while excavating a nest for its spawn

<!-- image -->

## Swimming Into Storm

Seasonal Effects for the Strands

The capricious nature of the sea sky, crafts the Strands as much as the coral growths that give it its namesake. While life responds to the rhythmic rise and fall of the tide with the steady adaption of evolution, the storms that batter the coast add an element of raw chaos that not only forces the flora and fauna quickly into new behaviors, but alters which predator will pursue which prey.

On the Strands, there is no escape from the wild weather that can batter the shore. The coral spires and bridges that make up Dawns Reach offer little protection from the storms and waves. To the north west, the wind picks up great billowing clouds of blinding sand off the Halfmoon Sands and, to the north east, waves pummel the outcrops of stone that shelter the inner sea. While rarely freezing over in the coldest of winters, the temperature of the water fluctuates greatly, causing untold creatures to migrate with each changing season.

## Seasonal Animal Behavior

## Spring

## Shimmering Flight

Tiama glide in great shoals during this mating season. Double any found on the Expedition Hunt and Scavenger Tables and they gain Advantage to Initiative rolls.

## Rising Hunger

Shaking off the lethargy of winter, the goliarch are ravenous. Once per day, they gain Advantage on their first round of attack rolls.

## Rising Hunger

Daellash fearlessly protect their egg-laden den tunnels. During this season, daellash have +1 AC.

Rivalla Horn Height - 1 ft. 8 in.

<!-- image -->

## Summer

Fall

## Sun Roasting Rot

The rank smell of rot wafts in the hot wind from beached creatures. Double any Scavengers Table results.

## Cooler Waters

Daugon swim further out to find colder seas and are less likely to be seen. Re-roll the first of any Hunting Expeditions that result in a daugon.

## Rising Hunger

Readying for the flocks of seabirds, arkiopti are found to be more voracious during the summer. They can perform 4 attack Actions for their multiattack instead of 3.

## Trawlauth Bacterial Blooms

As the seasons wane, trawlauth feast on dying vegetation and creatures have Disadvantage on Constitution Saving Throws against their Bacterial Fume ability.

## Shoal Migration

Spiera migrate out to the deeper ocean for prey. Re-roll the first of any Hunting Expeditions or Scavenger Tables that result in a spiera.

## Blood in the Water

Slannethiss hunt ferociously during the harvest moon. They gain Advantage in melee attack rolls against Bloodied creatures. They can use the Collapsing Coast Lair Action during this season.

## Winter

## Icebreakers

The spinning vortautilus thrive in the frigid waters. Double any vortautilus Scavenger Tables results, and they gain an additional 10 ft. to their swim speed.

## Keeping Warm

Aggressively staving off the cold to protect its urchin growth, the illithisk fights furiously. During this season, illithisk gain the ability; Multiattack The iIllithisk makes two attacks, using Scything Claw or Paralytic Bite in any combination.

## Hidden in the Sand

The cold forces gharril into a slow torpor state and they spend most of their time dug into the sand. Re-roll the first of any Hunting Expeditions or Scavenger that result in a gharril and they gain Advantage on Stealth Saving Throws.

When  the  tide  comes  in,  the terrestrial coral raises the soft fibrous polyp tufts out from  their  protective  weave so that the incoming surf pulls at gameats, sending them out to sea to grow elsewhere along the coast

## Reaping The Sea's Bounty

Expeditions upon the Strands

From the crushing depths to the salt-strewn air, competition has bred innovative adaptions for corals and flora to spread across every inch of the shifting landscape. Exposed to the sun and wind, the coral that rises from the tide tends to be hardier than their aquatic kin. Flexible bone-like plates grow along the bridging structures and can be carved away to be fashioned into a sturdy material for armor, the living plate rejuvenating itself in the salted waters of the ocean. Beyond the shore, wide swathes of kelp forests stretch along the shallows, their roots lit by the cold glow of deep dawn plants. Among these forests, seaweave grass grows in the light of those lit orbs, their dark green tough fibers though difficult to cut, make for an incredibly supple and durable material akin to boiled leather.

## Gharril

Class Gastropoda · Genus Derived Evolution Euglandina · Niche Amphibious Generalist Omnivore Preyed upon by Cerrophol, Daellash, Illithisk, Slannethiss · Preys on Daugon, Lanceleth, Scavick, Vinnirex

As the tide recedes, a large shell rises out of a tidal pool, a single orange eye peering across the receding waves, searching for the flopping desperation of stranded fi  sh. Suddenly, a tail whips salt water into the air and with loping limbs the large snail strides across the sands. Quickly, out pacing competing crustaceans, it grasps the fi  sh with its four prehensile tentacles, pulling the live prey in and grinding flesh and bone whole into its fearsome maw. Over time, gharril slowly build up a pearl of sorts in their gullet, a rolled and compressed sphere of bone and chitin from their coastal feasting. When threatened by a larger predator, they can expel this organic cannonball with devastating velocity, shattering bone and shell alike.

## Traits

Amphibious A gharril can breathe both air and water.

Coral Shelled When a gharril's lower body is buried and remains motionless,  it  is  indistinguishable  from  a  coral  outcropping.  It has advantage on Dexterity (Stealth) checks made to Hide while in the Strands biome or coastal-like environments.

Defensive Instinct (1/Day) If it survives any attempt at being attacked for the fi   rst time the gharril will attempt to bury its more vulnerable soft flesh in the sand.

On its turn, the gharril lowers its movement speed to 0 ft., loses its Vulnerability to Cold and gains +2 AC (total 17). It can choose to return to its normal state as a Bonus action.

If discovered at night, this creature will put up little fi   ght as it takes time for warmth to suffuse its limbs.

## Illthisk

Class Malacostraca · Genus Derived Evolution Nephropides · Niche Amphibious Generalist Carnivore Preyed upon by Daellash, Slannethiss, Vortautilus · Preys on Daugon, Gharril, Rauka, Vinnirex

Rising from the surf, salt water streams off odd squirming maggot-like pillars, with a strong paddle-like tail, the illithisk bursts out of the shallow tide. Scythe-like claws extended and grabs at a nearby vinnirex, a pained bark cut short by the mandibles biting deep into its fur. Within a heart beat of the flailing beast, paralyzing toxins built up over time by the symbiotic host of urchin on its back quickly silences the canine as the crustacean drags it under the waves. Although they are often the prey for larger predators, both from land and sea, this crustacean has an edge over the other diminutive scavengers that plague the beaches by the use of processing a toxin from the symbiotic urchin on its back, able to take down creatures that would otherwise crush its feeble form.

## Trawlauth

Class Malacostraca · Genus Derived Evolution Ligia · Niche Specialist Marine Algivore Preyed upon by Cerrophol, Lanceleth, Goliarch, Vinnirex

Trudging through the coastal shallows, the trawlauth looks to be merely the head of a thick trailing fog that drowns out all life behind it. When in reality, it is performing a vital service to the ocean floor by scraping off the virulent oxygen sucking algae and clearing space for new plant and coral growth. Th rough symbiotic mutualism the trawlauth is host to a colony of bacteria that resides on and under its exoskeleton. Living off the excrement of the crustacean and leaching calcium from its shell, the bacteria builds up funnels along the back of the creature. Th rough these, it vents a viscous bacterial soup that billows out into the water in thick, gill-choking clouds that protects the trawlauth from any nearby predators while spreading its bacterial spores far and wide.

Th ough near mindless, the crustacean is  often passive and slow to react, only resorting to aggression when cornered or desperate to defend itself.

## Cerrophol

Class Cephalopoda · Genus Derived Evolution Dosidicus · Niche Amphibious Generalist Carnivore Preyed upon by Daellash, Gharril, Goliarch, Illithisk, Slannethiss, Vinnirex · Preys on -

Daugon, Gharril, Illithisk

As the tide falls, exposing trapped crustaceans and fi  sh within tidal pools, the cerrophol stalks out of the surf to survey the pickings. Th ough resembling an octopus, this semi-terrestrial squid uses its two feeding tentacles to hunt above the waves. Th rough the selective pressures of evolution, they have hardened into claw-like tips for dragging its body across stone and sand, as well as cracking urchin and crab shell that it hunts for with its forward facing predatory eyes. With enough salt water held in its mantle for short trips upon the sand, the creature can make use of the often trapped prey in this alternating environment. Unfortunately for this squid, many other predators along Sol'Kesh coasts have adapted to this same tidal ecosystem strategy, and the competition to thrive is fi  erce.

## Reactions

Ink Spill (1/Day) Trigger: Th e cerrophol takes damage while out of the water. Response: It releases a 15 ft. radius puddle of slippery ink centered on its body. Th e area becomes diffi  cult terrain for 1 minute. Any creature that moves on the ink must make a DC 14 Dexterity Saving Th row or get the Prone condition. Th e ink can be washed away with at least 2 gallons of water.

Encountering Naturally inquisitive, the cerrophol has adapted to a life of changing tides and is quick to react to new creatures. If a Traveler acts as if they have no interest in eating it, the squid may follow them stealthily, attempting to sate its curiosity. A traveler that drops miscellaneous items, each more unique than the last, will eventually convince the squid to approach, its sense of self-preservation forgotten by its own sense of wonder.

On its turn, Wisdom Saving Th row: DC 12, the cerrophol. Success: Th cerrophol gains a third attack for its Multiattack action. Failure: Th e cerrophol is overcome with fear and makes a free Disengage and Dash action in any direction away from adversaries.

## Daellash

Class Actinopterygii · Genus Derived Evolution Gorgasia · Niche Generalist Marine Carnivore Preyed upon by Goliarch, Slannethiss, Spiera, Vortautilus · Preys on Cerrophol, Esslinter, Illithisk, Tiama

From deep within limestone tunnels to vast interwoven coral caverns, the daellash worms its way through tight confi  nes and hidden pathways, snapping up a multitude of crustaceans and fi  sh that crowd those submerged waterways. Extending long fi  lament gills, the daellash can weave them through the cramped spaces, allowing the large eel to breathe, all the while pushing deeper into the stony depths for new crevices to explore.Unlike its younger form, the adult exchanges group ambush strategies for a long ranged solitary hunting, ever vigilant for new nooks and crannies to corner prey. Th ese wildly successful large eels are found especially within Dawnsreach, its long powerful body so perfectly adapted to the twisted weaving coral that many believe the species originated there before spreading outwards to dominate the rest of Sol'Kesh's coastline.

## Actions

Grasping Bite Melee Attack Roll: +5 to hit, reach 10 ft., one target. Hit: 12 (2d8 + 3) Piercing damage. Wisdom Saving Th row: DC 10, target  Medium  or  smaller  creature  successfully  hit. Failure: Target  creature  has  the  Grappled  condition  (Escape  DC  12). Until this Grapple ends, at the start of the daellash's turn while it  is  Grappling  the  creature,  it  automatically  deals  7  (1d8  +  3) Piercing damage as its jaws sink into the target.

Gulping  Maw Th e  daellash  makes  one  Grasping  Bite  attack against  a  Medium  or  smaller  creature  it  is  grappling.  If  the attack hits,  the  target  creature  is  swallowed,  and  the  Grapple condition ends. Th e swallowed target is Blinded and Restrained, it  has total cover against attacks and other effects outside the daellash, and it takes 5 (2d4) Bludgeoning damage at the start of each of the daellash's turns. Th e daellash can only have one target  swallowed  at  a  time.  If  the  daellash  dies,  a  swallowed creature is no longer Restrained by it and can escape from the corpse using 5 ft. of movement exiting Prone.

Encountering A hunter that excels within tight confi  nes, the daellash is not often witnessed by those outside the narrow and claustrophobic  tunnels  that  make  up  its  hunting  grounds.  A mother daellash will use these same tight confi  nes to sequester her eggs, deep in the tight crevasses and twists of coral strands so they can hatch safely.

## Daugon

Class Mammalia · Genus Derived Evolution Suss · Niche Generalist Semi-Aquatic Omnivore Preyed upon by Daellash, Goliarch, Slannethiss, Spiera, Vinnirex · Preys on Cariver, Trawlauth, Trikautrae

Basking on the stony beaches of the Sol'Kesh coasts, the daugon is a survivor of two extreme temperature dualities. Found deep along the ocean floor, protected by a layer in thick blubber to withstand the frigid depths in which it scavenges for mollusks. By high noon, the large mammal will then rise to the surface, chewing through coastal plants while basking in the heat of the sun. When laying out on the beach, a dorsal sail arches upward, helping warm its blood to once again return to the depths. Th e striking colors of the sail's sunburst pattern is only found on males as they are used as a display towards potential mates. Th e matriarchal herd mothers often have their pick of competing males during the warm summer season and the bright colors make for a robust male to pick.

## Spiera

Class Cephalopoda · Genus Derived Evolution Todarodes · Niche Specialist Pursuit Carnivore Preyed upon by Goliarch, Slannethiss · Preys on Daellash, Daugon, Rauka, Tiama

Like the scales of a great sea serpent, sunlight glimmers off the mantles of a spiera shoal that race beneath the waves. With the use of forward facing siphuncles and numerous expelling jets, spiera squid are an agile and overpowering shoal. Launching into schools of fi   sh, grasping morsels of flesh in their fanged tentacles and continuing onward before the scent of blood attracts larger predators. When chancing upon enormous creatures like cetaceans and gigantic basking sharks, the shoal charges forward in a blinding stream of bubbles. With a sickening sequence of deeply heard thuds, dozens of squid slam their chitin-hardened mantle spears into the flesh of their prey, their long tentacles holding on tight as they feast on the quickly sinking creature before it falls too far into the black depths.

## Traits

Water Breathing Th e spiera can breathe only underwater.

Under Current Th e  spiera  has  Advantage  on  Dexterity  (Stealth) checks made while underwater.

Shoal Swarm When the spiera makes an attack, any other friendly spiera  within  5  ft.  of  it  can  use  its  Reaction  to  make  an  attack against the same target. Th e spiera executing Reaction attacks do not generate further Reaction attacks by other spiera in proximity to them.

Wary Instinct (1/Day) When startled,  the  shoal  spreads  a  billowing black cloud in unison, obscuring great swaths of the ocean.

Reaction Trigger: When a friendly spiera that it can see within 30 ft. of it releases its Ink Cloud. Response - Wisdom Saving Th row: DC 10, the spiera. Success: T he spiera uses its Ink Cloud unless it had already used it that day.

## Actions

Multiattack Th e  spiera  makes  two  attacks,  using  Spearing Mantle, Hooked Tentacles and Scissor Beak in any combination. Spearing  Mantle Melee  Attack  Roll :  +4  to  hit,  reach  5  ft.,  one target. Hit : 6 (1d8 + 2) Piercing damage. If the spiera swam 20+ ft.  straight toward the target creature immediately before the hit , the target takes an extra 9 (2d8) Piercing damage.

Hooked Tentacles Melee Attack Roll: +4  to  hit,  reach  10  ft.,  one target. Hit: 5 (1d6 + 2) Slashing damage. If the target is a Normal or smaller creature, it has the Grappled condition (Escape DC 14).  Until  this  Grapple  ends,  the  target  is  Restrained,  and  the spiera cannot use its tentacles on another target.

Scissor  Beak Melee  Weapon  Attack: +5  to  hit,  reach  5  ft.,  one target. Hit: 7 (2d4 + 2) Piercing damage. If the targeted creature is Restrained by the spiera's Hooked Tentacle attack, the spiera has Advantage on its Scissor Beak attack.

## Reactions

Ink Cloud (1/Day) Trigger: While it is underwater, and a creature the spiera can see hits it with an attack roll Response A 20 ft. radius cloud of ink spreads from the spiera. Th e  area  is  heavily obscured for 1 minute, although a signifi  cant current can disperse the ink. After releasing the ink, the spiera can use the Dash action.

Encountering In  the  open  waters  surrounding  the  coasts  of Sol'Kesh, spiera shoals are often found dashing under the waves at incredible speeds. Chasing a shoal down is nearly impossible as  they  will  weave  and  rush  around  any  pursuers.  Often,  the most effective way of catching the squid is to bait them with a large floating carcass, catching them in the act of taking morsels of flesh in their hooked beaks.

## Trikautrae

Class Echinoidea · Genus Derived Evolution Colobocentrotus · Niche Specialist Carnivore Preyed upon by Daugon, Gharril, Slannethiss, Vortautilus · Preys on Black Billed Fingulls

As the sun sets, the armored urchin slowly crawls out of the water with its long repurposed spines, perching itself on rocky outcrops to open its clam-like lid to reveal the waving luminescent tendrils within. Motes of glowing bacteria flow within the transparent skin of the tendrils as they wave back and forth in the gloom of the dusk light like a nest of worms to catch the eye of the high flying sea birds. Once sighted, they quickly descend to pluck the little wriggling lures, just as their beak nearly closes in on the glowing lures, the lid snaps shut with shocking force. Th ese tendrils are coated in a paralyzing neurotoxin that numbs the flesh of their victim and stills their thrashing struggle, while the hard calcite teeth of the urchin slowly grinds the immobilized prey to a grizzly end.

## Traits

Amphibious Th e trikautrae can breathe both air and water. Tidal Camouflage While the trikautrae remains motionless with its  shell  closed,  it  is  indistinguishable  from  the  surrounding stones.  It  has  advantage  on  Dexterity  (Stealth)  checks  made  to Hide while in the Strands biome or coastal-like environments.

## Actions

Paralyzing Clamp ( Open ) Melee Weapon Roll : +5 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. Hit :  11  (2d6  +  3)  Piercing  damage. Constitution Saving Th row: DC 14, target creature the trikautrae has successfully hit. Failure: Th e  target  creature  has  the  Paralyzed  condition  for  6 minutes. Th e Paralyzed target can repeat the Saving Th row at the end of each of its turns, ending the effect on a success.

Defensive Instinct When near death, its shell closes against intrusion. Reaction  Trigger: When  the trikautrae  is  reduced  to  0  hit points. Response - Constitution Saving Th row: DC 12, the trikautrae. Success: Th e  Trikautrae's  mouth  remains  tightly  closed  upon death, blocking the Scavenging of its Paralytic Tendril Knot and Concentrated  Digestion  Enzyme.  Any  effort  to  open  its  mouth requires a DC 17 Strength check.

## Taming Null

Maturity Age 6 Seasons

Devour  ( Open)  Strength  Saving  Th row: DC  14,  one  Medium or  smaller  creature  Grappled  by  the  trikautrae  (it  can  one creature swallowed at a time). Failure: Th e target is swallowed. A swallowed creature has the Paralyzed and Restrained conditions,  has  Total  Cover  against  attacks  and  other  effects outside  the  trikautrae,  and  takes  4  (1d6)  Acid  damage  at  the start of each of the trikautrae's turns. If the trikautrae takes 20 damage or more on a single turn from a creature inside it, the trikautrae must succeed on a DC 19 Constitution Saving Th row at the end of that turn or regurgitate the creature, which falls in a space within 5 ft. of the trikautrae and has the Prone condition. If the trikautrae dies, any swallowed creature no longer has the Restrained condition and can escape from the corpse using 5 ft. of movement, exiting Prone.

Shelled Tackle (Closed) Melee Attack Roll: +5 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. Hit: 11 (2d6 + 3) Bludgeoning damage.

## Bonus Actions

Clamping Shell Th e trikautrae can close its shell. While closed it gains +2 AC (total 16) and Resistance to Piercing and Slashing damage and it can use its (closed) Actions. In a subsequent turn, it can use a Bonus Action to Open its shell once more.

Encountering. Along  the  rocky  shores  of  the  Strands,  the trikautrae  is  often  found  nestled  among  the  stones  and  tidal pools.  Never  venturing  far  from  the  water,  it  often  comes ashore  at  night,  opening  its  wide  mouth  to  entrap  prey  with its  luminescent glowing tendrils that wave in the moonlit air. During the day, nestled among the clams and stones, it is much harder to spot.

## Tiama

Class Actinopterygii · Genus Derived Evolution Sphyraena · Niche Specialist Insectivore Preyed upon by Arkiopti, Daellash, Illithisk, Goliarch, Skyelarx

Tiama skim over the waves in great flying shoals with their elongated mouths extended over the water as they weave in and out of the exposed coral structures of the Strands, snatching the many insects that flit about the undulating colorful fronds. At three to four feet long, these creatures fall prey to many predators of both land and sea, surviving only by the coordinated quick movements of their shoal and always alert, orb-like eyes that dart back and forth, looking for the slightest sign of danger. When threatened and escape is no longer an option, the shoal will turn on the aggressor like a storm of razor sharp fi  ns, leaving any would-be-predator bleeding and sinking beneath the waves as the shoal flies past.

Disperse When the tiama shoal takes damage from an attack, it  can  momentarily  scatter  into  individual  fi  sh  as  a  Reaction, reducing  the  damage  taken  by  half.  Th e  shoal  of  tiama  then immediately reassemble.

## Vortautilus

Class Malacostraca · Genus Derived Evolution Nautilus · Niche Specialist Molluscivore Preyed upon by Goliarch, Nicalliath, Slannethiss · Preys on Illithisk, Toleepod, Trikautrae

Often heard more than seen along undersea cliffs, billowing clouds of grit obscure several vortautili that slam and grind into stone to dislodge barnacles and clams, grasping them in their black tentacles. With adaptations to survive the frigid depths and migrations to colder climes, the vortautilus' is suffused with an ethanol-like fluids that allows them to function in waters that most other aquatic life would suffer a slow torpor. Th ese cephalopod take advantage of the lack in competition during the colder seasons. When sheets of ice begin to form in the depths of winter, resonating deep cracking sounds are often heard as they spin violently into the ice sheets, creating new waterways to search for their favored prey, or, as the new year dawns, slamming in bouts of contending for mates.

Water Breathing Th e vortautilus can breathe only water, With the use of its chambered shell, it may hold its breath for 1 hour.

Curling Shell Th e vortautilus can retract or emerge from its shell as an Action. While retracted, it gains +2 bonus to AC (total 18) and cannot use the Gripping Tentacles or Spiral Vortex attacks but it may use its Th ermal Torrent and Jet Siphon.

Digging Spiral Th e  vortautilus  can  burrow  through  solid  earth and ice at a speed of 5 ft. leaving behind a 3 ft. wide tunnel.

Spinning  Shoal When  the  vortautilus  moves  and  performs  a Spiral Vortex Attack towards a target creature, any allied vortautili that are within 30 ft. and can see the target creature may use their Bonus Action to move up to their speed towards the same target creature and make a Spiral Vortex Attack as well.

Aggressive Instinct (1/Day) When an allied vortautilus is slain within eyesight, instincts of protecting its kin takes over.

Gripping Tentacles Melee Attack Roll: +5 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. Hit: 10  (2d6  +  3)  Bludgeoning  damage. Th ermal Torrent (Recharge 5-6) Th e  vortautilus  releases  a  jet of ethanol laced water in a 30 ft. line that is 1 ft. wide. Dexterity Saving Th row: DC 14, each creature in that line. Failure: 7  (2d6) Bludgeoning  damage  and  pushes  the  creature  10  ft.  away. Success: Half damage.

Jet  Siphon Trigger: A  creature  the  vortautilus  can  see  hits  it with  an  attack  roll. Response -Dexterity  Saving  Th row: DC  13, the  vortautilus. Success: Th e  attack  misses  instead  and  the vortautilus  propels  itself  10  ft.  directly  backwards  without provoking Opportunity attacks.

Encountering Found in hunting groups of several individuals, the vortautilus are often obscured in billowing clouds of sand and  debris  as  they  break  and  tear  at  the  ocean  floor  looking for clams and urchins to feast on. Naturally curious, they will approach a Traveler if they offer a soft fleshed starfi  sh for them to snatch into their many grasping tentacles, but will attack in unison if threatened.

## Arkiopti

Class Malacostraca · Genus Derived Evolution Acanthephyra · Niche Specialist Aquatic Carnivore Preyed upon by Goliarch, Spiera, Slannethiss · Preys on Black Billed Fingulls, Tiama

Floating near the surface, six small eyes rise over the waves in search of feathered prey. With paddle-like limbs the large crustacean treads water with a slow measured pace to match the rhythm of the waves so as not to stir the water around the stalks of its peeking eyes . When a flock of sea birds approach, it will carefully raise its articulated maw from the water, retching out a mucus-covered stone shard, one of many that rest within its gullet. With all six eyes tracking the bird's flight, a lethally precise hit cuts through the feathered prey, dropping it down to splash on the surface. With its prize floundering on the tide, the crustacean then awkwardly swims over to the dying bird and grasps it in its clawed forelimbs before it sinks and is lost to the many scavengers below the waves.

## Goliarch

Class Malacostraca · Genus Derived Evolution Penaeus · Niche Generalist Carnivore Preyed upon by Slannethiss · Preys on Arkiopti, Daellash, Daugon, Spiera, Taddil, Tiama

A behemoth among its meager lineage, the goliarch prowls the deep using its feeder limbs to grasp and pull anything it can into its needle-toothed maw. Often found drifting along the surface listlessly, preserving energy, only until its abyssal black eyes spot a glimmer of life in those darkened depths does it explode into ferocious motion. Dozens of paddle-shaped limbs churn the water as it barges into coral and stone alike to latch its many bladed maxilliped into a prey's flesh to feast. When the rare occurrence of a whale fall happening near the shores of Sol'Kesh, they converge upon them in fi  erce masses of savage hunger, often cannibalizing their own as much as feasting on the fallen cetacean in a mad frenzy.

The moon rests low on the darkened horizon, dawn only a sliver of light along the east. It's quiet, but for the reassuring rhythmic crashing of the surf along the outcroppings of coral that stretch across the sands like fingers pulling the island into the sea. The Traveler loved this time of the night, as if each cool breath of air carried the scent of purpose and peace.

Then a nagging thought crept in: Sol'Kesh is not peaceful, quiet only means imminent death. The Traveler stopped mid stride and listened. Wind softly sifting over the dune grass, birds in the distance singing to the barely risen sun and, between the crash of the waves, a rasping, slithering sound could be heard across the sands. With bated breath and a pounding heart, the Traveler silently steps back further inland, past waist-high dune grass and moves quickly to a rocky knoll that overlooks the seascape.

Dawn's light soon gives form to the coast and, at the receding tide, a long sinuous shape rises from the black water. Standing taller than the Traveler, a slick, nearly bipedal creature lifts its long twin pronged nose to twitch in the cool air and slowly it turns towards the Traveler. Unprepared and unwilling to face the looming threat, the Traveler eases back, keeping eyes glued to the dark stalker. The Traveler continues to slowly inch backward, only turning when the amphibious killer slithers back under the waves.

Evolved to stalk silently, little escapes this blind amphibian. A long, sensitive, dual-horned nose leads its way when in dark waters, sensing the electromagnetic pulse of nearby creatures. Their frightened heartbeats like a call to the slaughter as its long lithe body swims silently along the coral strands that twists over and under the waves. Daellash, daugon, even the voracious goliarch, stand little chance once this creature has tracked their beating hearts, blood pulsing loudly along the slannethiss' pronged horns as it closes the distance. For those believing the shore is safe from its extrasensory abilities, dozens of heat pits line the creature's head, allowing a full spectrum of thermal vision to aid this large nocturnal amphibian to dominate wide stretches of the shoreline. When rains pummel the coast, muffling the sound of its approach, it ventures further inland for warm blooded prey. Solitary creatures all, when two sense another nearby, they will politely draw back to unseen territory lines to keep from each other's presence and dwindling prey. Only in the rarest of occasions when too many of the amphibians vie for the same territory will they openly fight, slashing and slamming themselves against the other, the victor breaking off the weaker beast's sensory horns, pitying it as it flails in absolute pain and blindness.

## Strands Apex Influence

Unlike the other apex predators on this isolated island, the presence of the slannethiss brings little balance to its hunting grounds. If anything, it upends it so heavily that little more than small fish become all that remains of the animal life within the area. Many believe that the slannethiss is not native to the island, or native to an environment above ground, and that may explain why the surrounding flora and fauna have simply not had time to adjust to its malevolent presence.

This hypothesis rings true when one studies which locations the amphibian prefers to haunt. Coasts open to the sky with long stretches of  beach give this predator little cover to slink behind; instead, areas rife with caverns, narrow channels and, most favorably, the weaving of strands of coral are usually where this beast is found to nest. Many solitary slannethiss can be found in Dawn's Reach, for just that reason. With a multitude of islands, their coasts expanded further by long stretches of coral pillars that criss cross over each other to the sandy ocean floor, harboring an immense diversity of crustacean and fish that just manages to survive the ravenous predator. Further west, among the peninsula of Shatter Mouth, the waters run fast into the hollowed caves that riddle the many islands that meet the exit of the Shiverrun river. While not as plentiful as the corals to the east, the seasonal fish spawnings and the aggressive rauka that guard the river mouth provide a near endless supply of beasts for the predator to slay.

The cunning salamanders are far from unaware of their devastating impact on the ecosystem. After months of stalking the tides, either killing or frightening away any creature worth the effort to hunt soon forces them to migrate. Females will lay clusters of unfertilized eggs within deep crevices that face the ocean. These thickly gelled eggs will remain there for seasons, sometimes years, keeping damp by the surrounding water and awaiting both the fauna to return to the waters, and for a male to take up residence in the hunting ground the female had long since left behind. With electric sensors reading the subtle but telltale sign of awaiting eggs, the males will approach, fertilize and soon raise the young until they grow large enough to find their own hunting grounds. Conversely, when females chance upon another's eggs, they will quickly tear them apart and replace them with their own.

The shores of Sol'Kesh teem with life: Beaches covered in scuttling crabs, clouds of birds plucking them from the sand and schools of darting fish of every color. When a Traveler instead notices a barren absence, they must ready themselves, for they have stepped deep within the apex creature's lair. The slannethiss is no brute, it doesn't bellow its dominance to every beast within earshot, it doesn't challenge every other predator that steps into its territory, it simply stalks. Unseen until the last moment before it strikes, this stealthy hunter will track a Traveler for miles, learn their ways, their routines and attack when they least expect it. The only defense, the only way to survive, is to be a sliver more cunning than the amphibian, laying traps or, better yet, leave the coast and walk inland, far from the deadly tide.

<!-- image -->

These super-sensory narrow organs are packed with receptor cells that detect movement and subtle electric fields produced by its prey. Paired with thermal pits along its muzzle, very little escapes this apex predator

## Lair Actions

On  initiative  count  20  (losing  initiative  ties),  the  slannethiss takes  a  lair  action  to  cause  one  of  the  following  effects,  the slannethiss can't use the same effect two rounds in a row.

Slinking into Shadow Th e  slannethiss  melds  into  the  darkness. Until the start of its next turn, Ranged attack rolls against it are made with Disadvantage.

Tunnels in the Strands With innate sense of its coastal Strands home, the slannethiss disappears into a tight crevice of coral or stone. It reappears in a space it can see within 60 ft..

Choice Corals Grasping the clusters of a toxic breed of coral, the slannethiss throws it at an enemy within 30 ft. of it. Constitution Th row: DC  14,  target  creature. Failure: Target  has  the  Paralyzed condition and repeats the save at the end of each of its turns, ending  the  effect  on  itself  on  a  success.  After  3  minutes,  it succeeds automatically.

Salt and Grit Th e  slannethiss swipes its tail into the sand and water,  spraying  its  enemies. Dexterity  Saving  Th row: DC  13, each creature in a 20 ft. Cone. Failure: Target  has  the  Blinded condition for 1 minute.

Aggressive Rush Th e  slannethiss  surges  forward  in  a  straight line up to 50 ft. toward a creature it can see. If the slannethiss reaches  its  target,  it  can  immediately  make  a  Grasping  Bite attack against that creature.

[Seasonal] Collapsing Coast With a fi  rm stomp the slannethiss breaks  the  earth  and  displaces  a  section  of  the  coast.  Th e slannethiss  designates  a  10  ft.  square  area  adjacent  to  it.  Th e terrain in that area becomes Diffi  cult terrain.

## Regional Effects

Within the coastal caves along the Strands, the slannethiss makes its lair, leaving it nightly to hunt, before returning, dragging its prey to feast.

Scant  Predators Th e  slannethiss  clears  out  most  megafauna predators  that  encroach  within  6  miles  of  its  Lair,  leaving only the scraps to feast upon. Re-roll the fi  rst of any Hunting Expeditions that result in a predatory creature.

Nature  Unbalanced Th e  lack  of  larger  predators  allow  many small herbivores within 5 miles of its Lair to flourish, and over consume  the  flora.  Half  the  amount  of  discovered  Resources rounding up, from the results of a Foraging Expeditions.

Fear's  Shroud Creatures  within  4  miles  of  the  Lair  of  the slannethiss fear its nightly raids. Creatures have Disadvantage on Saving Th rows against the Frightened condition.

Savage Desperation Th e  predators that remain within 3 miles of  the  Lair,  attack  unusually  aggressively,  influenced  by  a stalking apex predator. Creatures native to the Umbrawells have Advantage on Initiative rolls.

Foolish Scavengers Nearing 2 miles to the Lair, brave or foolhardy scavick flourish off the remains of the slannethiss' kills. Double the number of any scavick results from the Scavenger Table.

Tremor  in  the  Water With  its  electric  receptors  picking  up movement in the sea. Creatures within the water, 1 mile of the Lair must make a DC 16 Dexterity (Stealth) check to not alert the slannethiss of their presence.

## Glass Brier

Small Natural Trap, Unaligned

AC 1

HP 2 (1d4)

Speed 0 ft.

Immunities Blinded, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion, Frightened, Prone

Senses None

Trap  Space The  small  clam  is  often  found  buried,  a  wide expanse  of  tendrils  spread  outwards  over  a  10  ft.  Square beneath the sand.

Countering The adhesive tendrils, when not covered in gains of sand, can be seen glistening in the light. Wisdom (Perception) Check: DC 10. Success: The Natural Trap is identified.

## Reactions

Numbing Tendrils Trigger: When a creature  steps  within  its Trap  Space. Response -Constitution  Saving  Throw: DC  16,  the creature  that  triggered  the  Trap. Failure: The  target  has  the Paralyzed  condition,  and  can  repeat  the  Saving  Throw  at  the end of each of its turns, ending the effect on itself on a success. The numbing lasts for 1 minute once out of the space and the creature can then move normally.

## Biology

Nestled  a  few  inches  beneath  the  beach,  the  clam  slowly spreads a network of clear tendrils throughout the sands, much like  mycelia.  These  tendrils  have  a  quick  acting  neurotoxin that numbs the flesh and tenses muscles tightly, causing the creatures to fall listly to the sand and slowly drown in the rising tide. Their decaying bodies nourishing the clam beds.

## Reactions

Trailing  Searing  Tentacles Trigger: When  a  creature  enters  its Trap  Space  below  the  water. Response -Dexterity  Saving  Throw: DC 16,  the  creature  that  triggered  the  Trap. Failure: 1d8  Poison damage  and  has  the  Poisoned  condition  for  1  minute,  taking  1 Poison damage at the beginning of their turn, the target can repeat the Saving Throw at the end of each of its turns, ending the effect on itself on a success.

## Biology

The havrenn is unlike any other creature and is more known for its symbiotic nature than its lethality. Comprised of a hydrozean colony  of  polyps  in  a  bowl-shaped  form  with  trailing  paralytic tendrils,  that  then  safeguards  a  unique  species  of  sea-lichen. Together this life raft floats off shore, with the lichen soaking in the sun safely, while the many trailing tendrils protect it from seabound herbivores.

<!-- image -->

## Torrum

Medium Natural Trap, Unaligned

AC 4 HP

Senses Tremorsense 10 ft. (Blind beyond this radius), passive Perception 8

Immunities Blinded, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion,

Frightened, Prone

7 (2d6)

Speed 1 ft.

Trap Space The towering starfish moves at a slow measured pace. Passively stinging any creatures that enter a space within a 5 ft. Circle.

Countering The  torrum,  with  its  obvious  height,  is  easily spotted,  so  it  is  recommended  to  use  ranged  weaponry  or weapons with Reach to cut it down.

## Reactions

Sea  Bound  Tendrils Trigger: When  a  Medium  or  smaller creature  enters  its  Trap  Space. Response -Constitution  Saving Throw: DC 12, the creature that triggered the Trap. Failure: The creature  has  the  Poisoned  condition  for  10  minutes  and  can repeat the Saving Throw at the end of each of its turns. While poisoned, the creature is compelled to swim in the ocean, and must use their movement each turn to get closer to the water, by the most direct route. If restrained or blocked, they become agitated and take 2 (1d4) Psychic damage for each minute they are prevented from moving toward the sea.

## Botany

Capitalizing on the exposed coral of the Strands, starfish soon evolved to not only rise past the tide, but grow to large sizes to  stave  off  the  predation  of  birds.  The  torrum  stands  above most, as it slowly crawls over smaller mollusks to feast, tendrils infused with a mind altering neurotoxin along its back deter predators, compelling them to keep away from the beach.

<!-- image -->

## Mawgraw

Medium Natural Trap, Unaligned

AC 3

Senses Tremorsense 5 ft. (Blind beyond this radius), passive Perception 6

Immunities Blinded, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion, Frightened, Prone

HP 7 (2d6)

Speed 0 ft.

Trap Space The carnivorous plant grows on vertical surfaces, its primary stalk able to shift slightly to allow its wide peristome to catch falling creatures within a 5 ft. Sphere.

Countering The  mawgraw  is  not  a  plant  attempting  to  hide and can be found along coastal cliffs, hoping to capture curious birds or falling terrestrial animals.

## Reactions

Gulping  Maw Trigger: When  a  Medium  or  smaller  creature enters  its  Trap  Space. Response -Dexterity  Saving  Throw: DC 14,  the  creature that triggered the Trap. Failure: The  target  is swallowed by the plant. A swallowed creature has the Blinded and Restrained conditions, has Total Cover against attacks and other effects outside the plant, and takes 7 (2d6) Acid damage at the start of each of its turns. If the plant takes 2 damage or more on a single turn from a creature inside it, the creature breaks free and falls in a space within 5 ft. (in addition to any vertical  space  to  the  ground)  of  the  plant  and  has  the  Prone condition. If the plant dies, any swallowed creature no longer has  the  Restrained  condition  and  can  escape  from  the  plant using 5 ft. of movement, exiting Prone.

## Botany

With  so  much  teeming  life  along  the  intertidal  zone  of Sol'Kesh's coasts, flora and fauna cling to every niche they can. Along the sheer rocky cliffs, amongst the barnacles and clams, some plants have taken root. The mawgraw's slight ability to move its  peristome  opening  to  capture  live  pray  allows  it  to thrive.
