<!-- image -->

## Under Fathomless Skies

The Ecology of the Farplains

The wind, a constant companion across the plains, cuts through dry grass and stone alike as the Traveler walks under a baking sun, away from the dangerous coastline. Each wary footstep scuffs the dusty soil as they shade their eyes to peer at two fast moving shapes far off in the distance. Though small and slender from this far away, the Traveler can still make out the powerful legs of the draffers pounding the earth as they rush headlong into a herd of rotund armored rutort insects. Forcing the herd to close in tightly and defend their young larvae, the two cunning mammals circle around, searching for any weakness in the impenetrable wall of chitin.

Knowing it's only a matter of time before the predators look elsewhere for an easier meal, the Traveler slowly lowered themselves to the earth, the Traveler slowly lowers to the earth, and hoping the wind doesn't betray their scent. Though the ground gently rumbles and the dust picks up from the distant migrating herd of bloomux, there's a peace to these wide open lands. Picking large seeds near the ground, the Traveler begins to study them, not only to document their part in the ecosystem, but if they would make for a handy snack when their supplies begin to run out.

A vast sky stretches across a wide expanse of earth, only the chain of distant Splitspine mountains breach the unending horizon. As clouds paint shadows over the rolling hills and flat plains, a multitude of beasts roam. The Farplains of Sol'Kesh, from the dry arid wastes of the west to the flowering fields of the east are home to vast herds of animals, from great chitinous megafauna, countless birds, fast moving bipedal predators and patient armored brutes.

On an isolated island, competition for space on the wide open vistas breeds a curious variety of creatures as their range for migration can be fairly limited, as well as the distance needed to escape predators. During the millions of years that Sol'Kesh has been separated from the continents, certain creatures, once small and unassuming, grew to larger sizes to graze and defend themselves, such as the swollen and ponderous bloomux aphid. While others adapted means to break through the tough armors of these generally slow moving herbivores - such as the quick footed axilot that's capable of shattering bone with their beaks, or the reptilian grahknash that can match any of the herd beasts, pound for pound, tackling them to the soil - the Farplains of Sol'Kesh have become a fierce land of brutes.

## A Wide and Distant Expanse

Traveling across the Farplains of Sol'Kesh is inevitable, as it makes up most of the island's landmass. The dangers of such a trek does not simply lie in managing to carry enough supplies across the parched western lands, or preparing for the torrential rains of the east, but truly the greatest danger is being seen. In a land of wide ranging hunters, any creature outside a crowded herd is targeted as easy prey. For those Travelers brave enough to face these quick footed  predators,  the  Farplains  of  Sol'Kesh  are  bountiful,  from some of the best sources of meat, extraordinary extracts from the myriad of flowers that spread across the hills, and with the right persuasion, a potential mount to ride across these far reaching lands.

Elsewhere far  from  the  island,  plains  stretch  across  continents and creatures spend whole lifetimes migrating from one end to simply reach the other. For the creatures that inhabit Sol'Kesh, these plains soon run against the coasts or the  horizondominating Splitspine mountains that rise from the center like a great dividing wall, and so many have learned to keep short and constant migrating patterns, following blooming plant growths and watering holes.

To  survive,  many  of  the  slower  herbivores  adapted  to  having thick, armored hides and roam in close-knit herds to defend their young. While the predators that hunt across the Farplains have either learned to hunt in packs, or to wait and conserve energy for opportunistic ambushes.

More so than any other biome throughout Sol'Kesh, the arthropods that evolved eons ago to a lower oxygenated atmosphere have been able to grow to monumental sizes on these plains. The rutort and bloomux that roam in Valedayn live off the rich fertile flowering lands,  wary  of  the  nocturnal  khanith  that  lie  in  wait  to  pounce on any that wander from the safety of the herd. Though not all arthropods grew large, and many use their diminutive size to their advantage, like the poisonous scorpithine posing in flower fields, their pygidium enticing hungry herbivores and pollinators. While birds still dominate the sky, two fast moving species found another  way  to  speed  across  the  plains.  Having  both  lost  their ability for flight, the axilot and hectatrex both rush with abandon. The  small  axilot  ram  their  sharp  beaks  into  carcasses  like voracious barracuda, while the more subtle hectatrex peers over the high grasses for nutritious, unguarded eggs, rushing away the moment the parents return. While these forms have evolved time and time again, there is one genus of bird that have evolved far beyond their ancient lineage and now swarm in great numbers, descending in a feathered whirlwind to any that find themselves beneath the sky darkening cloud of death.

Droplets of sweet nectar rise from the buds, the rich scent drawing in frugivore birds to eat and disperse the ilyrana seeds far and wide

Tall stalks proudly display brightly colored peppers  with  high  levels of capsaicin. This painful spice wards off most mammalian herbivores

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

## Valedayn

Rolling hills of dark loamy earth, bisected by the raging Inmere river, spread both north and south in a dizzying array of color and sweet scents. For much of the year these fertile plains are nearly entirely covered by blossoming flowers and fungal blooms. This unique landscape hosts more nectarivores than anywhere else on Sol'Kesh. Massive bloomux trundle through the flowers sucking deep of the nectars, as minute hairs along their six legs collect pollen to propagate the field's diverse flora. All the while, stealthy packs of khanith stalk the tall grasses, waiting for one of the slow and thorned beasts to foolishly diverge from their herd. Besides the abundant medicinal flowers that a Traveler may harvest, just beneath the soil, ooradew slimemold suffuses large patches of earth, pulled up from below by heavily armored Rutort, their long tusks irrigating the earth as they dig. Unaware of the many khanith waiting for them to rest their constant vigil.

<!-- image -->

## Land Of Wind And Sky

Locations of the Farplains

## Grayfynn

A dry arid expanse of hard packed earth, its gritty and thin topsoil is  blown  across  the  flat  plains  in  great  storms  of  dust,  feeding the eastern Four Moon Lakes. The plant life on the Grayfynn have adapted and proved to be hardier than most flora on the island; tough  rocky  exteriors  and  thorny  spines  protect  deep  rooted plants  that  can  withstand  both  the  long  dry  season  and  the voracious herbivores that migrate over these lands. Fortunately these same adaptations then provide rich sources of water if one knows which spiked plant is ready for harvesting.

## Aris

A wide vista of wet dunes, waves of sand mirrors the ocean that it stretches towards. While the tide can wane and wax over much of the landscape, it is the pervasive damp sand from the porous bedrock  below  that  gives  this  location  its  unique  properties. Besides some tenacious dune grass, most creatures have difficulty sustaining  themselves  on  these  sodden  sandy  plains,  yet  ocean faring  birds  will  congregate  in  their  thousands,  nesting  in safety from the general lack of terrestrial predators. Though this sanctuary can be short lived as axilot, migrating from Valedayn, rush  out  during  mating  season  to  barrel  into  nests  to  quickly trample eggs and feast on the spilled yolk. While rare, nicalliath are well suited to this environment, their chitin blades clashing over beached megafauna.

## Duskdown

The  eastern  cliffs  that  border  this  land  shroud  much  of  the morning in shadow, yet these lands grow verdant and fertile still as  the  dust  that  blows  north  from  the  Grayfynn  wastes  carries rich phosphorus to nourish the Duskdown prairies. While gentle khanaur  graze  in  the  cool  air  of  dawn's  shadow,  by  evening, the  golden  sunset  lights  the  Stoanhall  cliff  entrances,  allowing brilliant  growths  of  ivy  to  reach  deep  into  the  tunnels  where tormull larvae crawl in their numberless thousands, feasting on the wayward life that attempts to colonize their darkened pits.

## Tundrus

One  of  the  coldest  climates  outside  of  the  high  peaks  of  the Splitspine,  this  raised  plateau  of  chilled  winds  and  frost  laden earth leaves little to be desired. Draffers make good use of these heights as they are safe to rear their young while descending to the northern shores to prey on coastal daugon. When the biting wind picks up, bonestalk reeds that grow along the rivers rustle with the hollow sounds of winter.

<!-- image -->

## Meadow Fall

Clinging to the southern edge of Sol'Kesh, the Meadow Fall provides a stretch of verdant fields, enriched by rivers that bleed out to the ocean from the arid Grayfynn above. This sliver of blooming life provides more than just abundant flower fields, but a safe haven for the flocks of maedrynn that terrorize the northern wastes above. Fortunately for this idyllic coast, most of the year these hummingbirds individually flutter about from flower to flower instead, drinking plant nectar and using the thick colorful foliage to roost. Similarly to the Valedayn in the north east, arthropod mega-fauna dominate these lands. Though the sap filled bloomux is not to be found this far south, khanaur roam in great numbers along the coast, digging up the salt-blown earth in search of roots, further turning the soil for more flowers to take root. These slow moving insects keep a wary eye out for the silver glimmer of drafian silk whenever they near the sparse wealdwoods that encroach in from the Shiverrun's edge, their toxic silk stretching from distant trees to ward off many of the Grayfynn's aggressive predators that often invade during long droughts.

100 MILES

<!-- image -->

## Evolving With The Herd

Flora and Fauna of the Farplains

Wings outstretched, chilling wind flowing across orange feathers holding aloft the little round male bird. It chirps in short bursts, not as piercing as it could with its heavy resonating beak that crests its head, but with friendlier questing sounds. The stonecrest sparrow, like many of the birds and beasts of the Farplains, is migrating. The days grow shorter and the Tundrus more frigid with each passing gust of wind. The sparrows, unable to find the seeds that once grew plentiful among the low lying plants, moved west to the fertile Duskdown plains. Now among the warmer wind and grasses, the small bird felt a type of joy, an instinctual validation that they are on the right path. Chirping once again, an echoing call is heard, one tinged with curiosity and the unique tone of a female, in the thicket of rosewood trees past a sunlight hill, the bird rejoiced, for now, the world felt right.

On the Farplains, the wind lives. It rushes through the tall grass, whispers to the beasts that roam to never stop, to always move, for under the vaulted skies of these wide plains, movement is survival. On the island of Sol'Kesh, surrounded by endless ocean and divided by the horizon-shattering Splitspine mountain range, far ranging herds migrate constantly, taking advantage of new blooming growths, favorable weather and keeping one step ahead of their pursuing predators. When confined to an island, speed is not always enough to escape the tusks of a draffer or burning acid of a high flying tyrandroth, and while the quick footed tartakka have adapted successfully enough, many others, after eons of isolation have only survived through the thickness of their shell. The mega-fauna that dominate the gentler east, in roaming herds thousands strong are unlike any other. The sun glimmers off the rounded shell of rutort, the massive beetles that dig into the fertile soil, churning the earth for roots and tubers, drafian glide across the flat fields over dozens of waddling bloomux, drinking deep of the flower nectar that blooms in rolling hills across Valedayn. As the dry plains of the Grayfynn and the Tundrus highlands do not suit the chitinous beasts, tartakka rush about, outrunning the savage grahknash and ambushing argustaur. While speed and durability may secure survival for most seasons, all that may change when the sky darkens with the cacophonous cry of hummingbirds.

Thick pebbled flesh, like the rind of a fruit, protects the amphibian from the  claws  of  gorr,  while  retaining moisture,  funneling  dew  to  collect in specialized pore creases

<!-- image -->

## Nobblin Length - 2 ft. 6 in.

Converging on both turtle and mole-like traits, this toad digs within the loamy soil of the fertile Farplains with its fused claws. This amphibian can be found feasting on the grubs and larvae of the many arthropods of the flower fields

Spunmelon

Height - 9 in.

Flowers wave along the top of the tough,  ground-grown melons, open slits along their side inviting beetles to partake of the seed-filled fruit

Used to attract pollinators, the rich nectar will harden into an amber-like substance if left untouched for long Thorned Garnet Height - 8 ft. 3 in.

Agathe Height - 7 in.

An agile insect and seed eaters, this light tan  bird's  rhythmic  and  far  reaching trilling  song  is  a  constant  companion to the open skies of the Farplains as it rests in a wollowpon tree

Ivoripin

Length - 4 ft. 1 in.

The patient passerine bird clings onto bonestalk reeds as it waits for beetles to snatch with its long sharp  beak.  The  hollow  reeds clatter  hauntingly  in  the  wind, giving rise to their namesake

## Humbled By The Horizon

Seasonal Effects for the Farplains

The winds speak in these lands, rising from a gentle soothing breeze that caresses the grass and carries the heady scent of vitality, to an overwhelming tempest, darkening the skies menacingly and pulling trees from their roots. Like no other, this is a landscape of herds, constantly migrating to feast on the blossoming fields, leaving churned earth and the bodies of their weak in the their wake.

The Farplains span much of the island, and the great dividing mountain range of the Splitspine mountain account for much of its diverse environments. In the west, the arid Grayfynn with its argustaur sentinels and thorned succulents, to the central plateau of the Tundrus highlands, supporting little on its cold soil but lichens and quick-footed draffers. And lastly, thanks to the mountain's rain shadow, the east enjoys a bountiful flowering gardens found in the Valedayn with their lumbering bloomux and other large arthropods.

## Seasonal Animal Behavior

## Spring

## Raiding Axilot

Fearing the voracious murmurs of maedrynn, tyrandroth stay safe in the Wealdwoods. Re-roll the first of any Hunting Expeditions or Scavenger rolls that result in a tyrandroth.

## Blooming Fields

Bloomux drink deep from the blossoming flowers, their Fatal Burst Action becomes 30 ft. centered on the dying bloomux.

## A Sunless Sky

Maedrynn flock in great murmurs, becoming the region's Apex Predator during Spring. Encounters with its murmurs are frequent. They can use the Calling Cries during this season.

## Summer

## Fall

## Raiding Axilot

Axilot race across the roaming herds during the height of summer. Double any found on the Expedition Hunt and Scavenger Tables and they gain Advantage to Initiative rolls.

## Brazen Skies

The heat stirs the blood of the hunting grahknash during summer, they have Advantage on Strength rolls and have Advantage on Instinct rolls.

## Silken Threat

Drafian are found migrating across the fields, leaving 5 ft. wide Silken Streams. While the thin, nearly translucent strands can be difficult to see, the sun catches them brilliantly. DC 16 Wisdom (Perception) check. Failure: The Traveler comes in contact with the thread and suffers 1d6 Poison damage.

## Preparing for Winter

Preparing to feast before the colder season, khanith stalk far more actively. They gain Advantage on Dexterity (Stealth) checks.

## Gatherer's Moon

Gathering stores of nuts before the colder season, khanaur feast with abandon. They gain Disadvantage on Initiative rolls but gain +2 AC during this season.

## Dry Desperation

A lack of fresh eggs to sate the hectatrex diet leads it to raid the young of the herds. They gain the Skill Stealth +6 .

The rear-horned rogust dung  beetle  flies  into  the  air shaken  and  startled.  If  given the chance it will use its long mandibles to pinch at the suddenly attacking mammal

<!-- image -->

## Winter

## Cold Blooded Lethargy

A frigid cold grips the fields, bloomux, drafian, khanith, khanaur, rutort and tyrandroth all have Disadvantage on Initiative rolls.

## Dominating Tundra

Draffers, adapted to the colder climes can chase down prey with ease on the snow covered ground. They gain 10 ft. to their movement speed.

## Shelter from the Cold

Female Omaterssum listen keenly for the call of wayward infants on the cold fields and find many adoptive young during this season. Change their Adoptive Litter roll to 2d4 instead of 1d4 if encountered in the Farplains.

Heavy Rain · -2 to Perception (Wisdom) checks that rely on sight  beyond  30  ft.. All  Fire  damage  rolls  have  a  -3 damage penalty. All Cold damage rolls gain an additional +2   damage.  Negate  the  benefits  of  a  Long  Rest  if attempted without any cover or protection from the rain.

Maedrynn Scouring ·  Bones are found lying across vast swaths of the landscape after the passing of a maedrynn murmur.  Re-roll  any  Creature  results  from  Hunting Expeditions, the Traveler must keep the second result.

Feverish  Beauty ·  Deliris  yarrow  plants  rise  from  the fields,  causing a strange behavior in nearby fauna. This Natural-Trap will be encountered more frequently.

Ooradew  Affluence ·  Ooradew  is  found  glistening  in abundance  on  grassy  fields.  Any  Foraging  Expeditions will yield double the result for Ooradew. As it saturates the  fields,  any  Short  Rests  on  the  Farplains  adds  an additional 1d6 to the total healed from resting.

## Summer

Screaming Heat · Shrillion plants grow thick and ready to release their trapped gases. This Natural-Trap will be encountered more frequently.

Convincing Mirages ·  Visions of oases and far off locations waver  in  the  distance.  When  seen,  DC  12  Intelligence (Investigation)  or  Wisdom  (Perception)  check. Success: The Traveler understands it is false.

12 Summer's Spice · The sweet and spicy peppers thrive in the heat of the summer sun. Any Foraging Expeditions will yield double the result for Ilyrana Peppers.

## Fall

Cleansing Flames · Flint reeds expose their silvery fibers during  the  fall,  rubbing  abrasively  from  the  wind  or traversing Travelers may spark a flame. This Plant-Trap will be encountered more frequently.

Sky's Fury ·  A tumultuous heat storm breaks overhead. Roll a d20 every 1 hour when outside without shelter for every Traveler in the party. On a 1, they are struck by a lightning dealing 2d12 Lightning damage.

Storms of Dust ·  Dry  soil  and  windblown  leaves  make it  difficult  to  track  and  see  on  the  Farplains.  Creatures not native to the biome suffer Disadvantage on Tracking Wisdom (Survival) checks.

Ripe  Harvest ·  Orange  leaves  rise  from  conical  hardrind  fruit,  announcing  their  ripeness.  Any  Foraging Expeditions will yield double the result for Spunmelon.

## Winter

Ice  Trapped Fish · ±1  to  the  Range  Result  on  a  Fishing Expedition Roll.

Chilling Skies · Any creature not native to the Farplains, exposed to the  season's  extreme  cold  without  access  to fire  or  a  heat  source:  DC  5  Constitution  Saving  Throw each hour, which rises 5 for each additional hour exposed. Failure: Suffer 1 level of Exhaustion. Creatures that have Resistance  or  Immunity  to  Cold  damage  automatically succeed on the save.

Steps in the Snow · Wisdom (Survival) checks related to Tracking are made with Advantage thanks to the ease of snow drifts covering the Farplains.

The Last Harvest · With leaves nestled safely in a wooden shell,  bloomstone rises to take advantage of the lack of plants competing for space to soak in the weaker winter sunlight. Any Foraging Expeditions will yield double the result for Bloomstone.

## Seasons Of Harvest

Expeditions upon the Farplains

The Farplains stretch far across the island, ranging from the rich fertile soil of the Valedayn fields to the snow drifts of the Tundrus. Herd beasts migrating to the flora in bloom and in tandem, their pursuing predators picking off the weak fauna that fall behind. Beyond endless grasses, these lands harbor many unique resources. From the glistening jelly mounds of ooradew that eradicates infection and invigorates healing, to clattering pallid bonestalk reeds which make for incredible building material as they are pliable to bend in any direction without breaking. Besides what may grow among the fields, it is often the dessicated remains of beasts that hold the greatest riches, though only a Traveler ready to fend off the many scavengers can ever hope to claim them.

## Lhoupin

Class Mammalia · Genus Derived Evolution Apodemus · Niche Generalist Granivore Preyed upon by Hectatrex, Lanceleth, Scavick, Scorpithine, Th istlewing, Vinnirex

Grass sways gently in the evening breeze and the mouse leaps from to shadow to shadow across the dimly lit prairie in search of its favored meal, the bulbwark. Th ese stone-hard nuts can lay untouched for days as only the lhoupin are able to break their thick shell. With a staccato of rapid squeaks, the mouse uses its unique loop ears as a resonating chamber to register the perfect pitch for assessing the density of the shell and, with expert accuracy, a nearly imperceptibly high frequency squeak shatters the nut. Although this trait has been developed for the procurement of food, when in danger the lhoupin is capable of doing the same against predators, squeaking in quick succession to fi  gure out the exact pitch necessary to shatter carapace and bone alike.

Often roaming  alone,  their  shattering  squeak  is  usually  too  high  a pitch to discern, but their questing staccato of squeaks can lead a Traveler to the little rodents. Th ough extremely skittish, if one fi   nds their grassy hole burrows, piling a feast of bulbwark could potentially lure them out.

## Scorpithine

Class Arachnida · Genus Derived Evolution Mastigoproctus · Niche Specialist Ambusher Carnivore Preyed upon by Hectatrex, Scavick, Vinnierex · Preys on Axilot, Lhoupin

Dragging its long abdomen behind it in the dew of the dawn grass, the arachnid searches for a fresh bloom of flowers to prepare for the rising sun. Once settled among the grass and stalks, it will raise its back to the sky. Expanding the segments of its pygidium into a semblance of the flowers that surround it, the scorpithine then quickly migrates pigment cells within to match the color of the surrounding petals and masterfully camouflages itself in the underbrush. Patiently it will wait with an eerie stillness in the warm morning sun until an unwary pollinator will chance upon its false blossom. Unlike the flowers around it, its petals are lined with gripping serrated edges, ready to close in on the prey. And once they do, the long clawed hind legs repeatably stab the struggling prey until it ceases to move and the arachnid is then ready to feast.

## Stonecrest Sparrow

Class Aves · Genus Derived Evolution Passer · Niche Specialist Insectivore

Preyed upon by Lexati, Maedrynn, Skyelarx · Preys on Blister Grubs, Drafi   an [Young]

Among the rising peaks of the Sol'Kesh highlands, a single chirp pierces the calm morning air. For the blister grubs tunneling beneath the stone cliffs, this chirp is a deafening explosion of sound, shattering the cliff face in minute craters and exposing the crushed grubs for the little sparrow to snatch. Th ough small and skittish, these solitary birds have adapted their far reaching ability to communicate with each other over vast distances to a devastating effect when close. With a highly specialized resonating chamber within their crested beak, they can chirp tight bands of sound that can shatter stone and ironwood when next to the surface. While this can strain the delicate bones within its skull, the birds will not hesitate to use it when threatened by predators.

## Axilot

Class Aves · Genus Derived Evolution Geococcyx · Niche Specialist Scavenger Omnivore

Preyed upon by Argustaur, Draffer, Grahknash, Khanith, Maedrynn · Preys on Bloomux, Lhoupin, Tartakka

Th e quick pitter-patter of taloned feet speed over dry plains as the axilot rushes past circling scavengers. A dozen of their kind following suit, all directed at the large freshly slain carcass being torn apart by a famished predator under the arid day sun. With the sound of a sharp crack, it plunges into the fallen beast, its axe-like beak cracking through the ribcage and exposing the nutrient-rich organs for a fast snatch and hasty retreat. Its kin plunging in quick succession while the frustrated predator lashes out at the pestering flightless birds. With their precious prize held fi  rmly in razored beaks, bright scarlet drops trail behind them as they escape the hunting ground. Wolfi  ng down the meat while on the run, they take no rest as the flock continues their search to steal another meal.

## Bloomux

Class Insecta · Genus Derived Evolution Macrosiphum · Niche Specialized Nectarivore Preyed upon by Axilot, Draffer, Grahknash, Khanith, Maedrynn, Tyrandroth

Among the lush flower-fi  elds of Valedayn, slow plodding bloomux graze in great herds. Sipping nectar from the leagues of flowering fi   elds with their long prehensile proboscises, these peaceful arthropods, standing at nearly seven feet tall, want nothing more than to grow their honeywombs for their gestating pupae during the summer. When predators approach the herd, the nectarivores will turn their spiny backsides outwards to dissuade any foolish hunter that attempts to get near. Th ough this presents their precious honeywombs to the attacker, the potential  explosion  of  chitinous  spikes,  sticky  nectar  and  wriggling  pupae  often  proves  fatal  to predators, and allows the larvae to be eaten by scavengers for the next stage in their development.

## Drafi   an

Class Insecta · Genus Derived Evolution Cyclophora · Niche Arboreal Specialist Herbivore Preyed upon by Maedrynn, Skyelarx, Tyrandroth, Ynnil

After eons of pressure to hasten the vulnerable metamorphosis that transforms a caterpillar into a butterfly, one species developed wing-like membranes during its larval form and simply continued feasting. Th is form of neoteny forced the creature to forgo the usual brief life span of sexual maturity and, instead the insect now grows large and lives past their year-long lifespan, hibernating in leafy nests and reaching egg laying adulthood once growing to a foot in length. While not capable of true flight like a butterfly, the drafi  an, in its near larval state can nonetheless glide across great distances to fi  nd rich feeding grounds. Such a gentle flight would be an easy meal for most predators had the drafi  an's bodily fluids not be suffused with a neurotoxin from feasting on the toxic leaves of a drafwood tree.

A wide sail-like membrane spreads outwards  to  catch  updrafts  and allow the  drafian  to  glide  effortlessly across fields and rivers

<!-- image -->

Folding  its  wing-membranes  close to  its  body,  the  drafian  navigates along  treetops  in  a  similar  manner to its larval caterpillar origins. When needing to move quickly, it launches itself upwards and spreads the thin jelly-like skin out to glide effortlessly

Rows of spiracles adorn the sides of the soft fleshed insect. These allow the creature to grow large as it can take in vast amounts of oxygen when in flight

<!-- image -->

## Hectatrex

Class Aves · Genus Derived Evolution Sagittarius · Niche Generalist Omnivore

Preyed upon by Draffer, Grahknash, Khanith, Maedrynn, Tyrandroth · Preys on Axilot, Korruk, Scavick

<!-- image -->

A curiously feather-fringed avian head peers over tall grasses that rise from the plains like waves in an ocean. Th ese lone hunters use their height to survey the wide expanse of their domain for spotting prey scampering in the dry stalks, the prowl of larger, more fearsome predators, and the brightly colored fruit that grows on the lofty wollopon trees. With a long stride on wide clawed feet, the hectatrex bounds over the flat landscape evading predators and chasing down prey with ease, only being caught unaware in the waning hours of the setting sun as their sight is not well suited to low light conditions. Th ough often found alone, during the spring season a male and female bird will alternate protecting their young, vulnerable chicks that they conceal in grass woven warrens.

Encountering With  the  advantage  of  its  height,  the  hectatrex often  spies  approaching  creatures  and  quickly  responds  by escaping  into  the  prairies  if  they  deem  them  hostile.  Th ough skittish, these birds do have a curious streak and, if a Traveler stands still  in  the  open  fi  elds  holding  aloft  a  vibrant  wollopon berry,  they  may  turn  back  and  investigate  the  gift.  Th ough be wary as the hectatrex can quickly turn violent when threatened.

## Khanith

Class Insecta · Genus Derived Evolution Ocypus · Niche Generalist Nocturnal Carnivore Preyed upon by Argustaur, Grahknash, Maedrynn, Skyelarx · Preys on Axilot, Bloomux, Rutort, Scavick, Tartakka

When the moon rises to its apex, lithe arthropods scamper across the Farplains, their powerful articulated legs propelling them quickly in search of sleeping prey. Th ese instinctual pack animals prowl silently, coordinating their movements through the use of pheromones. Searching for burrows, lone herd beasts or wayward young, once they chance upon an easy kill they encircle the prey quickly and strike with flight-assisted leaps; their forward tarsus claws and sharp mandibles plunging deep into flesh and chitin alike. Khanith share a curious relationship with the lumbering khanaur, their shared ancestry leaving echoes of a pheromone based understanding. Th is connection goes beyond a lack of predation, as the khanith have been seen fi  ercely defending packs of khanaur from aggressors.

## Korruk

Class Mammalia · Genus Derived Evolution Orycteropus · Niche Burrowing Generalist Omnivore Preyed upon by Argustaur, Axilot, Draffer, Grahknash, Nicalliath · Preys on Gorr, Embergral, Scavick

Th ough covered in armored plates and wielding sword-like claws, the korruk looks almost apologetic in the way it carries itself. Th is mammal digs deep into the dry soil of the Grayfynn plains during the heat of the day digging for deep rooted plants and, at night, moves slowly above ground for fresher fare. Evidence of its passing is clear for most predators, a long dragging tail that often leads to a massive hole in the earth. When found underground, the rather docile looking mammal can turn surprisingly aggressive. When cornered, the korruk raises its dermal plates in a threatening display and will ferociously slash with its stone breaking claws, capable of cutting through flesh with devastating ease.

## Tartakka

Class Mammalia · Genus Derived Evolution Tapirus · Niche Generalist Herbivore Preyed upon by Argustaur, Draffer, Grahknash, Korrask, Khanith, Maedrynn, Tyrandroth

Galloping  across  dusty  sparse  grasslands,  the  fi  ercely  territorial  tartakka  roams  in  fast  moving  herds  searching  for  shallow  root vegetables to dig up with their stout claws. Capable of surviving long months of drought, they are covered in a leathery hide and can retain moisture with a thick deposit of fat that runs below their tail. Th ough smaller than the females, the males carelessly ram their knobby skulls and reinforced upper humps into each other on a regular basis in a constant reshuffling of herd hierarchy. Th is nearly manic behavior works in their favor when they are threatened by predators, both males and females charge forward in a sequence of devastating strikes, with long bounding leaps that end in a concussive barrage of meat and bone.

And though each herd only numbers in the dozens, there are a multitude that clash for fertile territory. Th ese herbivores often react  aggressively  to  any  not  of  their  kind,  and  there  is  little that can calm them once their blood is heated. Alone, a tartakka may be more susceptible to handling as it's vulnerable without its herd, but getting one alone is often more diffi  cult than its worth.

## Rutort

Class Insecta · Genus Derived Evolution Pachnoda · Niche Specialist Grazing Herbivore Preyed upon by Draffer, Grahknash, Khanith, Maedrynn, Sacrissian, Tyrandroth

Found slowly plodding through the flower-fi  elds of Valedayn, rutort are a common sight, churning up the soil and chewing the thick tubers and vegetables in the rich earth. Often roaming among the herds of similarly sized bloomux, they are known for their docile temperament and relaxed grazing, relying on their thick chitin to defend against all but the most dangerous of predators. While generally peaceful, when the young pupa that crawl alongside them are threatened, the dozens-strong herd will respond in an earth shattering stampede. Th eir simple minds leading them to rampage for hours on end until nearly exhausted and collapsing in a heap of churned earth and the crushed corpses of predators.

## Draffer

Class Mammalia · Genus Derived Evolution Proteles · Niche Generalist Pursuit Carnivore Preyed upon by Grahknash, Korrask, Maedrynn, Tyrandroth · Preys on Bloomux, Gorr, Khanaur, Rutort, Tartakka

With whooping howls, a dozen draffers break through the sparse cover on the Farplains in pursuit of prey. With cunning primal tactics, they run alongside herds of grazing beasts, forcing them into a long line as they nip and slash at their flanks. Over long miles they wear them down, the frail or young soon fall behind and the pursuing predators close in for the kill. With powerful legs they leap and slash with lateral bladed tusks, cutting deep wounds and bleeding their prey dry. As each herd beast collapses, draffer cubs pounce on the weakened animals in a frenzy as they furiously fi  ght their litter kin for the choice meats, while the adults continue the pursuit onward, leaving a trail of carcasses in their wake for the pack to feast on.

Multiattack Th e draffer makes two attacks, using Gnarl Toothed Bite or Rending Tusks in any combination.

Th e bleeding lasts for 1 minute or until the target receives healing or succeeds on a DC 12 Wisdom (Medicine) check to staunch the bleeding as an Action.

e Encountering Despite its fearsome appearance the draffer lives in  a  tight  familial  pack,  roaming  across  the  colder  Tundrus highlands caring for their young whilst on the move. Th ough a  voracious  pursuit  predator,  they  will  favor  somewhat  fresh carrion when the opportunity arises. Travelers who offer such grim gifts could pique their interest, often dispelling the initial aggressive demeanor they would display to other creatures.

## Khanaur

Class Insecta · Genus Derived Evolution Micropeplus · Niche Generalist Grazing Herbivore Preyed upon by Argustaur, Draffer, Grahknash, Maedrynn, Tyrandroth

A deep grinding sound heralds the peaceful packs of khanaur as they chew through stalks of tall grass and dig up tubers to feed their voracious appetites. Roaming in groups of several individuals, their larvae stuck on the undersides of the adult members to keep their familial packs tight and moving, the khanaur travel the Farplains in slow seasonal migrations. Grazing in close proximity to each other to ward off any nearby predators, while the larger females of their group patrol, their massive forelimbs stomping into the soil to warn any that think they can attempt to penetrate their heavily armored chitin shells. Keeping pace with their roaming herd are packs of khanith, picking off smaller predators that would otherwise prey on the larvae grubs, their presence welcomed by their larger cousins.

## Argustaur

Class Reptilia · Genus Derived Evolution Phrynosoma · Niche Generalist Pursuit Carnivore Preyed upon by Grahknash, Maedrynn · Preys on Axilot , Tartakka, Khanaur, Korruk, Gorr, Rutort, Scavick

Despite its heavy gait, the argustaur is capable of relentlessly charging down a herd of rutort and running them into the ground with its  persistent,  near  inexhaustible stamina. Th e  ground shakes under its weight as its hoof-like feet pound the earth. Th rough the evolutionary need to move quickly and yet retain a measure of armored protection, this lizard has developed segmented plating while its legs have lengthened and straightened to gallop over the dry and arid soil. When closing in on prey, it will extend its neck to twice its length in a whip-fast snapping bite, sinking its teeth into its exhausted prey. Once sated, the lizard will spend days digesting and building back up its energy reserves, half buried in the soil and looking nothing less than a mound of stone and dirt.

## Grahknash

Class - Reptilia • Genus Derived Evolution - Ouroborus • Niche - Generalist Carnivore

Preyed upon by - Maedrynn • Preys on - Axilot, Argustaur, Draffer, Gorr, Korruk, Scavick, Tartakka, Vinnirex

Prowling over the dusty rock-strewn wastes of the Grayfynn, a grahknash surveys the horizon for prey. Savagely ravenous, the creature relentlessly follows herd beasts for miles along migration trails, its sand-blasted plates camouflaging it as it slowly approaches before exploding into action, charging down the weak and young. Little deters this lizard in its natural habitat as its thick sharp plates, that cover it from snout to spiked tail, defend it from all but the most ferocious of apex predators. When not actively hunting, the grahknash can curiously be found licking stone and mud, slowly building up silica deposits in its body to harden and strengthen its plating. Th is allows its scales and hard, knobby, club-like tail to not only be a suitable bludgeon but sharpened to a razored edge.

A high pitch droning heralds death for many mega-fauna that roam the Farplains, stampeding in terror from a tyrandroth on the hunt. Th e large dragonfly, a fortunately rare sight, as the predatory insects compete viciously with themselves for territory or mating couplings that end with the male being eviscerated by the larger females. Th is fi   erce aerial hunter will often run down herds, singling out stragglers with its exothermic caustic chemical spray to blind and cripple them before descending and ripping apart flesh to feast. While this insect cannot hope to fly and survive when the murmurations of the maedrynn are at their height of savagery, the tyrandroth absolutely dominates the Farplains as a reigning apex predator for most of the year, only fearing another of its lethal kind.

<!-- image -->

The morning sky suggested the day was going to be bright and cloudless. Instead the fields, as far as the eye could see, were shrouded by a shifting mass of dark clouds high above. Curious, the Traveler sits on a stone outcropping and begins to sketch the rhythmic forms the cloud took. From so far away it looked like it was moving slowly, undulating and twisting upon itself...perhaps it was moving too fast for a cloud.

A small trilling chirp catches the Travelers attention and, on the rock next to them, a green feathered bird with a long, trailing, pink tail hops closer. Putting down their pencil they watch the tiny bird hop closer and closer before it opens its oddly jagged beak and takes a bite at their pants.

Yelping in surprise the Traveler swats at the bird, it flutters away with a high pitched trill. Feeling apologetic for the violent outburst, the Traveler returns to their book for only a moment before hearing an echoing trill from up above, and then another, and another, within mere moments the air began to vibrate with a deafening screech of trilling hundreds. Quickly looking to the dark sky, the Traveler sees the unnatural cloud spin and twist into a vortex, and as it descends closer to them, thousands upon thousands of birds begin to materialize from the cloud of darkness.

Ordinarily found near the prairies that skirt the edges of the Wealdwoods, the diminutive hummingbird flutters about from flower to flower, its green shimmering feathers as welcome as a calm and peaceful summer day. That all changes quickly when spring warms the grasses and a keening death knell is heard screeching across the open skies of the Farplains. Before long, thousands of them gather into a great murmur, darkening the sky above the flat lands, becoming a natural calamity and blocking out the sun. A single maedrynn hummingbird may be of little threat, but during the ferocious but fortunately short mating season, they become an absolute apex predator together. Countless birds descending like a tornado of serrated beaks onto any creature that is foolish enough to be out in the open daylight, ripping and flensing flesh from bone in a frantic need to fuel their rapid and voracious metabolism. Unlike most hummingbirds, the maedrynn can lay several eggs at once. With the mother unable to fly back and forth to feed so many hatchlings, they line their nests with layers of meat so that the hungry young can feast immediately. Once the fledglings have tested their wings and fluttered away from the nest, the hummingbirds return to a diet of sugary flower nectar and the multitude of insects and arachnids that the teeming Farplains can easily provide.

## Farplains Apex Influence

Many lands suffer annual storms, seasonal floods and reoccurring natural calamities. Nature adapts to the rhythm and uses these waves of destruction as a means of cleansing. The Farplains have done much the same with the maedrynn murmurs and their seasonal voracious storm of death.

While most of the Farplains' ecosystem would consider the tyrandroth as its apex predator, and for many seasons of the year it is, from the wide arid plains of the Grayfynn to the rich fields of Valedayn, the maedrynn reigns supreme as an absolute force of nature, if only for a destructive sliver of time. When the chill of winter abates and the flowers bloom in spring's heat, the maedrynn flock en mass from the outer edges of the fields and prairies, their trilling birdsong changing for something more aggressive, more screeching as they rapidly build in size and temperament. For the creatures that migrate across the plains, this begins as a pestering annoyance as solitary birds take nipping bites. But as the days of amassing hummingbirds continue, they begin to build up into great flocks, descending on the creatures in a ripping frenzy.

Triggering migrations and mobilizing the young and healthy away from the swarming carnivores, the maedrynn inadvertently clear out the weak and provoke many creatures to move to greener pastures. While this leads to the long term benefit of both the species and the land, it is hard to feel that when the swarm of birds rip through the creatures savagely. Thousands upon thousands glittering, green feathered bodies flock to the sky, their murmurs beautiful to behold in the great shifting mass, like a single body it flows across the sky like liquid, the males and females of their kind dancing and frolicking together in mesmerizing aerial dances, until they spot movement. The dark cloud above condenses into a twisting tornado, descending on the rushing prey in a storm of feathers and beaks. Akin to aerial piranha, the hummingbirds tear into the flesh of the terrestrial beasts, their serrated beaks flensing fur and hide with ease, a shrilling cacophony of chirps only matched by the screams of pain by the beast within their feathered vortex. Sated and flying off to lay their nest with strips of meat, the bones of the remains settle into the soil, left to fertilize the fields with its rich phosphorous and calcium. Once the eggs are lain and the nest properly prepared with meat, the bird can be quite friendly and curious, chirping delightfully as it flutters from flower to flower, completely dissimilar to the ravenous maelstrom it once was.

The maedrynn murmurs are truly a sight to behold, beautiful in their ferocity but, like a storm, it is best seen from afar. Travelers stepping on to the Farplains often must contend with ferocious lizards, gigantic insects and more. Choosing to journey during the brief reign of the maedrynn is a recipe for annihilation, leaving little but bones for the grass.

The maedrynn's moribeak, so named for the high pitched deathly wail that is heard when the males call to gather for their lethal spring murmuration

<!-- image -->

Most seasons they subsist on sugar-rich nectar and small arthropods. Only at the end of winter do the hummingbirds adopt a meat-based diet, ravenously  stripping  the  flesh  of  any  beast beneath the murmuration to fuel their offspring

## Lair Actions

On  initiative  count  20  (losing  initiative  ties),  the  maedrynn murmuration takes a lair action to cause one of the following effects, the swarm can't use the same effect two rounds in a row.

Fade  and  Form Th e maedrynn  murmuration  swarm  breaks apart into thousands of individual birds and reforms into a free space within 60 ft. of itself.

Darting  Death  Dealers Th e maedrynn  murmuration  swarm designates a point within 60 ft. of itself. Dexterity Saving Th row: DC 18, all creatures within 5 ft. of the selected point. Failure: 17 (5d6) Slashing damage. Success: Half damage.

Cloud of Fear Wisdom Saving Th row: DC 17, all creatures within 60 ft.  that  can  see  or  hear  the  swarm. Failure: Target creature has the  Frightened  condition  until  the  maedrynn  murmuration swarm's next turn.

Buffeting Wings Th e maedrynn murmuration swarm selects a target creature within 60 ft. of itself. Strength Saving Th row: DC 14,  target  creature. Failure: Th e  creature  is  pushed  10  ft.  in  the direction of the swarm's choice.

Distracted Feast Th e maedrynn murmuration swarm designates a point within 60 ft. of itself that hosts the remains of one of their earlier meals. Until the maedrynn murmuration swarm's next turn, 20 ft. from that point is Obscured, as the birds thickly flock and feast.

[Seasonal]  Calling  Cries Th e cries of hunger  gather  more maedrynn  to  join  the  murmur.  Th e  maedrynn  murmuration swarm gains 5 (1d10) Health Points (HP) as more birds join the swarm.

## Regional Effects

A  dark  storm  of  feathers  spreads  across  the  sky  in  dizzying patterns, beneath it for miles lay the bones of creatures too slow to escape this seasonal frenzy.

Grave  Temptations Emboldened  by  the  multitude  of  bones, scavengers  try  their  luck  on  the  outskirts,  6  miles  from  the Lair.  Double  the  amount  of  creatures  from  the  results  on  the Scavengers Table.

Lingering Hunger Sated individual maedrynn hummingbirds busying themselves with preparing their nests within 5 miles of the Lair may still single out nearby creatures for a bite of flesh. Increased likelihood to target Maedrynn Young (pg. 98).

Dreadful Flight Flying creatures within 4 miles of the Lair are overcome  with  fear  from  the  cloud  of  birds  on  the  horizon. Wisdom  Saving  Th row: DC  12,  any  creature  with  a  flying  speed attempting to move. Failure: Target creature lowers their flying speed to a minimum of 10 ft., the target repeats the save at the end of each of its turns, ending the effect on itself on a success. After 5 minutes, it succeeds automatically and doesn't need to take this test again for 1 day.

Barren Plains Little is left  of  the  many creatures that roamed these  fi   elds  but  bloody  bones.  Travelers  within  3  miles  of  the Lair  must  re-roll  the  fi  rst  of  any  creature  results  for  Hunting Expeditions.

Ceaseless Trilling Th e massive murmuration of hummingbirds shriek and call constantly and from within 2 miles of the Lair. Creatures that attempt a Long Rest must succeed on a DC 15 Constitution Saving Th row or gain one level of Exhaustion from the  fi   tful  rest.  Additionally,  creatures  have  Disadvantage  on Wisdom (Perception) checks that rely on hearing.

Watchful Cloud Within 1 mile, the frenzied eyes of thousands of maedrynn above look hungrily for the movement of any prey. Creatures out in the open, 1 mile of the Lair must make a DC 14 Dexterity (Stealth) check to not alert the maedrynn murmuration of their presence for every hour they remain outside.

Minuscule spores float across the windswept Farplains, harmless in their minute size. Only an infinitesimally rare few will chance upon the corpse of a creature to settle on and rapidly infest. This slime mold spreads across the bones, rendering the beast's corpse into a decrepit version of its living form, covered in fruiting bodies ready to propegate.

## Biology

During the fall  dry  season,  when  the  grasses  grow  brown  and brittle, silvery filaments fray at the edges of the flint reed grasses which will rub against each other in the heavy winds, sparking flame and igniting wide swathes of fields. Seeds are then dropped into the smoldering, ash-fertile soil for next year's   growth.

A high concentration of iron in the silvery fibers that fray at the edge of each leaf allow the plant to spark flame when agitated

## Biology

These  pungent,  yet  beautiful  flowers  can  be  found  dotted across  grassy  hills  and  prairies  throughout  Sol'Kesh.  Their delicate stems are susceptible to damage from grazing herds and have developed a means to keep large fauna at bay as the pollen  kicked  up  from  disturbing  them  can  induce  a  rapid acting fever. Forcing the lumbering beasts to slow down and keep away from the yellow flowers.

Minuscule  clusters  of  flowers  spread across the fields in an idyllic display of beauty. Only to cause feverish sickness for any that approach too closely

## The Sinking Horizon

Journeying to the Fenlands Biome

The humid marshland air nearly vibrates with the incessant droning of insects. With each step the Traveler takes further from the drier fields they left behind, they sink deeper into the sodden soil, the smells of loamy rot erupting to assail the senses as mud squishes and pulls. All the while, along the distant horizon, a green smudge of the Wealdwoods promises dry firm earth and cover from the oppressive sun above.

As the Traveler pushes aside stalks of cattail to step onto a knoll of mossy stone, they are met with silence from the interrupted mating croaks of amphibians. With only the sound of buzzing insects, the Traveler grips their coral walking stick tightly in their fist, way of the unnerving quiet. Mere heartbeats later, an eruption of silty water explodes along the water's edge, surprising the Traveler as a gray skinned amphibian with tentacles whipping from its open mouth hops towards them, quickly closing the distance. The Traveler sees the yellow viscid fluid splash off the tentacles of the rushing glomunder and knows they are in for a dangerous encounter. Before the Traveler can raise their staff to strike, a pink and bone white spear darts in from the unsettled water and the gray amphibian crashes hard onto the stone, a massive tongue breaching its side.

The Traveler stands stunned and unexpectedly grateful as they watch the head of a solmundis rise from the water and drag their prey back into the water, its hooked tongue stuck firm between the poor glomunder's ribs while its struggling kicks slowly lose strength. With a sigh of relief, the Traveler wipes sweat from their brow and begins looking for a sharp stone to affix onto their staff, a spear suddenly sounding like a good idea.

## The Life Blood of Sol'Kesh

## Stepping into Mire and Muck

From torrential flooded rivers, to stagnant peat bogs, countless creatures  have  adapted  to  freshwater  environments  to  make the  most  out  of  the  mercurial  existence.  Many  fish  developed rudimentary lungs or the means to leave the water when needed. Birds  migrate  in  great  numbers  to  take  advantage  of  both blossoming new life and the bleak final days of fish fighting for the last ounce of water in a drying lake bottom. Arthropods of all sorts  crawl  and  fly  in  endless  numbers,  the  humid  air  allowing them to  grow  large  quickly.  Many  mammals,  both  aquatic  and terrestrial, can be found wallowing in the same waters for untold generations, while others migrate, feasting on the fish and plants that flourish when the season is right. But no class of creature has benefited more than the amphibians that thrive in this crossroads of  aquatic  and  terrestrial  existence,  thriving  in  the  mud  unlike any other, and so it is of no surprise that amphibians have come to dominate this biome as the largest of them, reigns unopposed in the murky depths.

These  wetlands  are  unsuspectingly  lethal;  a  calm  gentle  river can transform into a raging white water rapids, a peaceful still lake with its mirror-fine surface may harbor fathomless depths and unseen predators, and the mud of endless marshlands can consume the body of any unsure of their step. For those able to find means to stay dry or traverse the water safely, they can find bountiful  fishing  spots,  amphibians  to  catch  and  thousands  of plants to harvest. Though the trip rarely takes the same path twice as the terrain can change within the span of a single rainstorm.

The Fenlands are one with the changes of weather and seasons, a Traveler must not only be aware of what flora and fauna dwell in the area, they must also know if recent rains have swollen the once calm river to a torrential rapid, whether the watering hole that supplies whole herds with life has dried, or how deep a lake might be in case a colossal predator resides within its black depths.

As these waters run through all of Sol'Kesh, the Fenlands are not always a destination to find, but a constant in every environment. A Traveler must always keep a wary eye out for what lies in the mud and deep waters, and carry a fishing rod in hand to make the best of it.

Solmundis - Young Length - 1 ft. 4 in.

Unlike the older salamanders, the solmundis young spend less time hiding in the mud, and are instead  found  swimming  and  wrestling  with  their clutch mates. This behavior, while unsafe, aids the amphibians  in  weeding  out  the  weak  and  unwary from the numerous  predators that prowl the Fenlands.

While  still  small,  the  young  solmundis'  wickedly sharp tongue is nearly adult sized. At first they are highly inaccurate, but with their formative month of  practice,  trying  to  harpoon fish and crab, those that  can  eat  their  fill,  survive  and  improve  upon their lethal aim

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

## Beggars Moor

North of the Inmere, above tall cliffs rests a gray solemn moor that stretches to the Thornfen woods. This pock-marked marsh can hardly support trees in its alkaline waters, instead growing undulating wet dross grass and bog petals that leave vast expanses of soggy grounds. Beneath these thick grasses drains a slurry of swamp water down to the Undermoor caves below, along with many discarded husks and bones to moulder in the caverns. Under these obscure waters, murrow thrive, blindly navigating the twisting sinkholes and swallowing whole any that wade into their waters. Fortunately too large for the voracious murrow, herds of baughur wallow above, munching on the drab plant life that manages to rise from the algae choked waters in this forebodingly dank expanse.

<!-- image -->

## Four Moon

Curiously  shallow  lakes  break  up  the  dry  plains  of  Sol'Kesh's western  range.  A  Traveler  can  wade  across  these  vast  expanses of  water,  rarely  ever  sinking  past  their  full  height  and  when in the center of the largest lake, only a haze of cliffs in the east reveals  more  than  just  endless  water  from  horizon  to  horizon. Both pebbleback auhk and baughur roam in substantial herds in these lands, thousands of birds, migrating from across Sol'Kesh and even from far distant lands settle in during the spring, while nicalliath  burst  from  the  shallow  silt  to  claw  and  rip  through any creatures foolish enough to step near their chitinous bladed forms.

## Shiverrun

Rapids rush down from the Splitspine mountain peaks, their ice cold  waters  giving  this  fast  flowing  river  its  namesake.  While home to the most abundant colonies of creekot clams, stepping into  these  near  lethal  currents  will  either  break  a  Traveler  over the countless sharp rocks, or drag them down to the river's delta where rauka aggressively defend the seaweed strewn rivers with their monstrously sharp tusks. Coming in from the ocean, silvette swim upstream to spawn, their armored bodies slicing through the more placid fish, yet falling prey to the lanky fennfistle that pluck them from the waters with their long needle-like teeth.

## Rootwell

Far  within  the  Tanglewood's  sea  of  trees  rests  a  sinkhole,  its surface mirroring the sky above in a perfect mirror of foreboding stillness. Within these black waters lie fathomless depths to the bedrock of the island, and hosts a large colony of apex predator nuthorghull. Well known to devastate larger fauna until they are sated and return to their long protracted hibernation. Despite its reputation, this large lake feeds the surrounding forest, attracting untold number of migrating forest dwellers to its misty edge, its clean waters a much needed respite from the endless trees.

## Mhinfell

Rushing  down  from  the  Splitspine  mountain  above,  the  white waters  of  the  Mhinfell  flow  into  the  crescent  bay  surrounding the  Astradale  coral  island.  Cascading  waterfalls,  stories  high, exemplify  this  river  with  small  ambushing  lanceleth  waiting at  the  base  of  the  waterfalls,  their  coiled  bodies  ready  to  spear any  ascending  spawning  fish,  or  feed  on  the  falling  detritus  of creatures that did not survive the journey. Herds of rauka can be found congregating at the river delta that feeds into the crescent bay,  the  mineral  rich  waters  enriching  the  brackish  sea  plants below  and  creating  a  stretch  of  fertile  territory  for  the  aquatic beasts to graze and clash over.

## Inmere

Seen  as  a  halfway  point  from  the  great  expanse  of  salt  water surrounding the coral island of Astradale to the Rhaen's rapids that lead to the ocean, this briny lake runs deep. Many of Sol'Kesh's surrounding ocean-bound aquatic creatures traverse the Inmere to bypass the northern tip of the island. Nuthorghull, with its thick rubbery flesh is able to survive the salty depths and will snatch any  creatures  that  enter  their  deep  domain,  they  nearly  meet their match with the enormous goliarch that stream past in great seasonal migrations. As such a vital path for aquatic creatures, many scavengers can be found picking choice carcasses along the rivers  edge,  with  scavick,  herragath  and  vinnirex  competing  in voracious confrontations over the freshest kills.

## Rhaen

Sheer cliffs to the north slowly bleed nutrient rich water from the marshlands above into the brackish river that stretches between the  Inmere  lake  and  the  eastern  ocean.  Larkiera,  dominate the  narrow  waterways  that  grow  thick  with  encroaching  kelp forests,  the  murky  river  allowing  them  to  hunt  for  smaller  fish and amphibians with ease. Glomunder and wrakis aggressively compete  for  their  sliver  of  muddy  sanctuary  along  the  cattail covered banks.

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

## Mirewynn

A low lying mist settles over this near endless expanse of swampy Fenlands like a wet shroud, the stink of rotten stagnant water permeates the air, while methane rich bubbles rise and pop on the scummy surface. A quiet slurping sound is heard as a solumundis drags its body through the thick mud, its long bladed tongue ready to pierce a crimson-winged herragath unawares. Although these treacherous bogs are riddled with sinkholes and clouds of incessant biting insects, the glimmering sheen of vigael can be seen on the surface's sluggish waters. Light refracts off the prismatic oil, hinting at its value, as the restorative algae is worth the miles of quagmires to reach. While the Mirewynn may look dour at first glance, it is a haven for life as many seabirds feast on the maddening amount of insects that breed in the marsh waters that border the salt of the ocean and multitude of streams that bleed from the Tanglewood to the north and the cold fresh Shiverrun river to the west. Many of these birds that take roost during the spring bring a much needed respite to the herds of baughur and pebbleback auhk, their welcomed beaks enthusiastically picking off parasites and bloodsucking bugs.

<!-- image -->

## Through Mud And Stream

Flora and Fauna of the Fenlands

Toxic drool runs down the large amphibian's chin as it splashes in the marsh, the footing would be treacherous for most, but with wide clawed feet it is perfectly suited to the muddy bottoms and quickly traverses the bug-ridden Fenlands. A pang of hunger drives the simple minded creature onward as its forward facing eyes searches for the movement of something worth catching. Hoping to spot the unmistakably gangly silhouette of a freshwater bird, or the splash of a large fish but, instead, finds a curious cluster of brown furred rodents lazily float down the river. Its eyes widen in anticipation and two long tentacles, dripping in a yellowed slime, unfurl from its mouth as it bounds forward eager to lash the simple mammals into a toxic-laden stupor. Landing in the mud, its mouth agape and ready to attack, the four gurubara look up at the aggressive glomunder with half lidded eye. They sigh in unison and simply close their eyes once more. The breeze then picks up and the predator feels a gentle wave of calm, of satisfaction. Within moments its muscles relax, its pang of hunger subsides and it gently settles into the river. Hunting is exhausting, why not just rest for a moment...

Unlike a rolling expanse of trees, or a sky piercing mountain, the fresh waters of Sol'Kesh are not a single environment unto itself, but are instead a source of life that feeds the ecosystems it runs through. Every and all animals that live within the island rely on the Fenlands to a certain extent. A herd of rutort may meander into the Fenlands as they chew through river-coast-grown brookbuds, or a shaevrynn will swim through sunken woods to snare a fish. While many will enter the Fenlands to drink or hunt, there are some creatures that are so well adapted to these wet environments that they never leave. Freshwater fish swim through miles of river or flourish in land locked lakes. The larkiera and murrow dominate the gloomy waters, adapted perfectly to navigating the silt and plant choked waterways. Able to straddle both environments, amphibians thrive in the marshes and sunken forests like no other. Solmundis and glomunder terrorize the muddy riverbanks while fennfistle use their long legs to leap across the marshes in search of delectable bird eggs and, beneath them all, deep in the muck and mud of the lake bottoms, rests the monstrously large nuthorghull salamander. Once roused, the amphibian will consume any and all creatures that call their waters home. The Fenlands are truly a boisterous landscape with the buzzing of insects, untold number of frogs croaking their ballads and the bellowing of pebbleback auhk and baughur. These two megafauna, while content to happily wallow in the mud and chew through thick marsh grasses, can get rather territorial when the two herds find themselves encroaching on the same stretch of grass. When the two herds meet in such a manner, it's not long before the Fenlands are shaking with the body slams of the large mammals and eechoing, bellowing grunts that add only more noise to this riotious biome.

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

## By Rain And Flood

Seasonal Effects for the Fenlands

The separation of earth and water blurs in the Fenlands. Thick obscuring mists rise from stagnate bogs, tumultuous rains spill from the riverbanks flooding the land, and the squelch of mud is heard with every step. The Fenlands flourish or perish with the weather, and its many fauna have adapted to co-exist with the mercurial wetlands, learning to move with the seasons.

The Fenlands are more than centralized locations on the island and can be found spread throughout the forests, mountains and prairies, in any land where fresh water reigns. Though there are areas where the Fenlands truly dominate the landscape, the wide lakes of Four Moon harbor whole ecosystems of fresh water fish in its shallow depths, the white water Rhaen will break any creature that tries to swim against its current, and the glooming Mirewynn welcomes many unsuspecting Travelers to a sinking demise in its muddy pits.

## Seasonal Animal Behavior

## Spring

## Copious Fish

Silvette swim quickly in the white river rapids. Double any found on Hunting Expeditions or Scavenger rolls and they gain Advantage to Initiative rolls.

## Nest Raiders

As the egg hunting fennfistle ready for the plentiful spring months, they gain Advantage on Dexterity Saving Throws.

## False Harmony

With a constant cacophony of mating calls during the spring, creatures roll with Disadvantage against the herragath's ability - Natural Mimicry

## Summer

Fall

## Unseen Scouring

Nuthorghul feast voraciously to prepare for the colder months. Nuthorghul gain Advantage in Strength rolls. They can use the Muddy Banks Lair Action during this season.

## Scavengers Edge

Scavick migrate to the Fenlands to feast near the watering holes. Double the amount of scavick when they are the result of any Scavenger rolls. And they gain Advantage on Initiative rolls.

## Stalking Depths

Larkiera aggressively hunt in the abundant plant growth during this season. During this season, larkiera's gain the ability: Multiattack The larkiera makes two attacks with its Piercing Teeth attack.

## Winter

## Muddy Hibernation

Solmundis and glomunder hibernate within the cold mud. Re-roll the first of any Hunting Expeditions or Scavenger rolls that result in a solmundis and glomunder, and they gain Disadvantage on Initiative rolls.

Cold Water Supremacy Baughur and rauka, capable of withstanding the cold, venture Double any baughur and rauka found on Hunting Expeditions rolls and they gain Advantage on Initiative rolls.

## Settled in the Mire

To survive the cold winter, murrow are found to be well fed and thick with tougher skin. During this season, murrow will have +1 AC (total 15).

## Sweeping Droughts

Baughur and pebbleback auhk aggressively vie for dwindling bodies of water. They have Advantage on all melee attack rolls while Bloodied.

## Tranquil Water

Abundant gurubara saturate the rivers with their calming algae. Larkiera, silvette and murrow have Disadvantage on their first melee attack roll of a combat encounter.

## Leaping Invaders

Wrakis converge on the Fenlands in greater numbers during this season to feast on drought-trapped prey. Double the amount of wrakis when they are the result of any Scavenger rolls. And they gain Advantage on Initiative rolls.

Moolmundis

Length - 1 ft. 6 in.

A distant  relative  to  the  solmundis,  the smaller  amphibian  lives  in a similar environment. It relies on its edged bottom jaw  to  deter  predators  and  its  sensitive cheek feelers to search for insects in the obscure mud. This foot-long salamander is more active than its more lethal relative and is often seen scurrying away from the squelching footfalls of Travelers

## Spring

Cacophonous Mating · All manner of birds, amphibians and insects loudly exclaim their desire to mate. Travelers gain  Advantage  on  Stealth  checks  as  the  various  calls drown out anything else across the Fenlands.

High Floods ·  Rivers  spill  over  onto  the  banks  in  tumultuous floods. Strength Saving Throw: DC 15, swimming Travelers or creatures not native to the Fenlands biome for every 30 ft.  of  swimming. Failure: Creatures are pushed 10 ft. down stream.

Crimson Waters · Blood Twine spreads across lakes and swamps,  spreading  their  red-tinged  algae profusely across the still surface. This Natural-Trap will be encountered more frequently.

Windswept Pollen ·  Brookbud blossom along the rivers and ponds, their pollen blowing through the wind. Once per day, a creature that takes a Short Rest in the Fenlands can  recover  an  additional  1d6  Hit  Points.  Any  Foraging Expeditions  will  yield  double  the  result  for  Brookbuds Leaves.

## Summer

Drowning Pits · Fenfall Fungus spread across the damp soil, obscured by the summer growth. This Natural-Trap will be encountered more frequently.

Irritating Insects · Thousands upon thousands of buzzing insects thicken the humid summer air. Dexterity Saving Throw: DC 10, any Traveler attempting to maintain Concentration. Failure: They lose their Concentration as they are distracted by swatting away the bugs.

Uncertain  Footing ·  Laststep  Lily's  unfurl  their  great leaves on the lakes and ponds, enticing prey to take a step. This Natural-Trap will be encountered more frequently.

Riverside  Yields ·  Thick  and  nutritious  Pond  Wattle plants sprout up all along the river's edge. Any Foraging Expeditions will yield double the result for Pond Wattle.

## Fall

Iridescent  Fog ·  Marsh  gases  mix  in  a  kaleidoscope  of color in the thick mists. -2 to Wisdom (Perception) checks that rely on sight beyond 30 ft..

Grasping Grasses ·  Wriggle Weed choke the river ways, tugging  and  pulling  anything  that  gets  closed  to  their questing  leaves.  This  Natural-Trap  will  be  encountered more frequently.

Fetid Water · Stagnant waters harbor festering microbes and wriggling larvae during the dry months. Constitution Saving  Throw: DC  16, any  creature  submerged  in  a natural body of stagnant water. Failure: Disadvantage on Constitution Saving Throws and any healing received is reduced by half for as long as they are in the water and for 10 minutes once outside of it.

Vigael  Saturation ·  The  green  algae  of  vigael  spreads throughout  the  waterways.  Any  creature  submerged  in a natural body of water gains Advantage on Constitution Saving  Throws.  Any  Foraging  Expeditions  will  yield double the result for Vigael.

## Winter

Frozen Paths · Cold temperatures freeze the surface of lakes and swamps, many waterways are now traversable by  foot.  Halve  the  results  of  any  Foraging  Expedition results.

Frigid Rain · -2 to Wisdom (Perception) checks that rely on sight beyond 30 ft.. All  Fire  damage  rolls  have  a -3 damage penalty. All Cold damage rolls gain an additional +3 damage. Negate the benefits of a Long Rest if attempted without any cover or protection from the cold rain.

Below Zero ·  Beneath the ice, the water runs cold. Any creature  submerged  for  a  number  of  minutes  equal  to its  Constitution  score  before  suffering  any  ill  effects. Each additional minute spent in frigid water requires the creature to succeed on a DC 10 Constitution Saving Throw or gain 1 Exhaustion level. Creatures with Resistance or Immunity to Cold damage automatically succeed on the save.

Frost  Bitten  Oysters ·  Cold  water  sweeps  through  the streams,  freezing  creekot  oysters  and  slowing  their ability to close and hide their pearl within. Any Foraging Expeditions  will  yield  double  the  result  for  Creekot Oysters.

<!-- image -->

Most of what lies beneath the waters is deep under layers of mud and silt, moldering wood criss-crossing over algae covered stone and bone. Seams of minerals and deposits of gems lay unobtainable without incredible feats of effort, but an enterprising Traveler still has reason to find value in what these waters can produce, especially the ever bountiful fish. Where the waters sit still and thick, choked with algae and unmoving for seasons, the bluish-green-liquid vigael can be found, smelling of vinegar and fresh grass, it rises to the top in thin oily layers. Nestled between river stones are creekot oysters, filtering the quick moving waters and providing a very rich meat. In the Fenlands, while a pickax and shovel may not be of much use, a Traveler willing to get their feet wet may still find their fortune.

## Ruske

Class - Xiphosura • Genus Derived Evolution - Limulus • Niche - Generalist Algeavore

Preyed upon by -

Nicalliath, Nuthorghull

A warm breeze sways drooping willow branches, stirring a slow gentle river, its wide banks home to long strands of cattails. Th ick clusters of lily pads with slow moving, wedge-shaped-shelled creatures quietly chewing, quietly chewing through them at a docile pace. Frogs and birds rest on this notably serene crustacean's hard, flat shell while it roams the fresh waters, spearing all manner of river plants and chewing through the fi  brous material with its long channel of teeth. Th ough blind, it has a highly developed sense of taste, able to detect the flora of its specialized diet and the flourishing surface algae. It is through the fi  ltering and consumption of this scum that the ruske both cleanses the water ways for plants to proliferate, and develops its own inner toxins. Th ese toxins suffuse its body, giving its meat an impalpable taste and often lethal digestion, leading this creature to live a safe, if slow life.

Peaceful Wader While the ruske remains motionless in natural bodies of water, it indistinguishable from the surrounding plant life and has Advantage on Stealth checks.

Defensive  Instinct (1/Day) When  suffering  a  fatal  blow,  its  slow, senseless reactions can keep it moving.

Reaction Trigger: When the ruske reaches 0 hit points. Response -  Constitution Saving Th row: DC 5 + damage taken from the most recent hit , unless it was a critical hit, the ruske. Success: Th e ruske drops to 1 Hit Point instead.

## Actions

Chitin Lance Melee Attack Roll: +3 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. Hit: 4 (1d6 + 1) Piercing damage. If the ruske swam at least 10+ ft. toward the target immediately before the hit, the target takes an extra 7 (2d6) Piercing damage.

Encountering A common sight in many ponds and rivers,the ruske  is  often  so  covered  in  lily  pads  and  algae  growths  that it  is  nearly  imperceptible  until  it  moves  against  the  current. Tranquil to a fault, there is little that can alarm a ruske, and it will gladly carry larger creatures on its back while it peacefully munches through swamp scum and floating leaves.

## Gurubara

Class Mammalia · Genus Derived Evolution Hydrochoerus · Niche Generalist Semi-Aquatic Herbivore Preyed upon by Korrask , Nicalliath, Nuthorghull, Wrakis

A curiously common sight in the many rivers throughout the Tanglewood, the peaceful gurabara float down the streams chewing on reed and river fruit along the sun dappled water in absolute contentment. Numerous predators, often prowling right next to the drowsy beasts, barely spare them a glance as they rush down their game trails to savage far more diffi  cult prey. While not impervious to the sudden snapping mandibles of a korrask or the wide ranging slaughter of a nicalliath, the gurubara still remain rather safe in their lackadaisical lifestyle. Th e key to this mysterious protection is the aquatic algae that proliferates within their fur and stains it a healthy green. Calming pheromones wafts from the algae, cooling tempers and the pangs of hunger of nearby predators.

While there is no effort into approaching them, refraining from the urge to lie by the rivers edge or float alongside them, becomes a feeling more diffi  cult to resist than a Traveler would expect.

## Herragath

Class Aves · Genus Derived Evolution Leptoptilos · Niche Generalist Ambush Carnivore

Preyed upon by Larkiera, Murrow, Solmundis, Slannethiss · Preys upon Finimander, Lilotoal, Moolmundis, Varrip

Standing tall with crimson feathers and a sinister beaked head, the herragath makes for a terrifying sight on the otherwise placid lake. To those that swim below the water, its red, sky-countering hue is all but unseen. Standing stock-still in the shallows, it waits for the sun to rise to its zenith when the frogs and fi  sh of the Fenlands seek shelter beneath the dark canopy of the lily pads. Dozens of specialized leaf shaped scales spread outwards along its legs, and as prey nestle in close, the herragath will spear its beak downwards, impaling and securing another meal. As such a patient hunter requires absolute stillness, the bird has learned to mimic the surrounding predators, periodically emulating the roars, grunts and calls of fi  erce beasts to keep the true hunters at a safe distance.

## Herragath

Medium Beast, Unaligned, Wary

## Traits

Natural Mimicry Th e  herragath can mimic the sounds of other creatures it has heard. A creature that hears the sounds can tell they are imitations with a successful DC 12 Wisdom (Survival) or Intelligence (Nature) check.

Wary Instinct (1/Day) When Bloodied and in melee combat. On its turn, Wisdom Saving Th row: DC 15, the herragath. Failure: Th e herragath Disengages away from combat. Th e herragath must repeat  the  Saving  Th row  on  each  of  its  turns,  escaping  further until it is passed.

## Actions

Spearing Beak Melee Attack Roll : +4 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. Hit : 6 (1d8+2) Piercing damage.

Piercing Screech Constitution Saving Th row: DC 13, each creature in  a  15-ft.  Cone.  Failure:  7  (2d6)  Th under damage and has the Deafened condition for 1 minute. Success: Half damage.

## Reactions

Distracting Call Trigger: A creature the herragath can see hits it with an attack roll. Response - Wisdom Saving Th row: DC 14, the triggering creature. Failure: Th e attack misses instead.

Encountering While  the  herragath's  blood-red  feathers  help disguise it from the blue fi  ltered view of its waterborne prey, above  the  surface  it  is  unfortunately  easy  to  spot.  Th ough searching  for  the  bird  by  the  use  of  sound  is  instead  an  all together different challenge, as it mimics the territorial calls of nearby predators, and even the cries of injured fowl to attract lesser scavengers for it to skewer on its spear-like beak.
