<!-- image -->

When an egg is lain, the female herragath will snip off her own lilyscales to wrap it  safely  and  place  the  egg  within  the riverbank's rushes. The naturally camouflaged scales keep the egg hidden and sealed from the encroaching water

Specially adapted scales spread outwards from the stork's stalk-like legs. These fleshy scales float on the water similarly enough to lily pads to deceive aquatic animals looking for shelter.

A tall multi-chambered hollow crest adorns the stork's skull, this intricate extension of its beak allows the bird to  mimic  a  vast  array  of  sounds  it collects during its lifetime

A  long, blood-red  beak  is shaped  specifically  to  stab downward  at fast moving fish and amphibians

## Solmundis

Class Amphibia · Genus Derived Evolution Speleomantes · Niche Generalist Amphibious Carnivore Preyed upon by Fennfi   stle, Nuthorghull, Sacrissian, · Preys on Glomunder, Herragath, Moolmundis

Rising to the height of a large dog, the mud covered salamander slinks through marshes and wetlands, its long sharpened tongue tucked into its odd jaw-less mouth. Th is amphibian, like its smaller cousins, needs a wet environment to survive and, because of its larger size, often rests in shallow ponds or in the muddy silt of swamps, awaiting the sounds of approaching prey. Evolving a powerful harpoon-like tongue that became too large and long to fi  t within its mouth, the bottom jaw receded to simple vestigial bones that rest beneath the jaw. When prey draws near, a whip-fast bone edged tongue rips out of the mud, stretching a full 10 feet to impale and embed its barb deeply into the unfortunate creature. Just as quickly, the salamander reels it into its awaiting needle toothed maw.

## Traits

Limited Amphibiousness Th   e solmundis breathes air through its skin, and needs to be submerged in water at least once every four hours to avoid drying out and suffocating.

Ambush Predator Th e  solmundis has Advantage on attack rolls against any creature it has Surprised.

Shallows Stealth When the solmundis remains motionless while submerged in mud, water, or a similar environmental feature, it gains Advantage on Dexterity (Stealth) checks.

Aggressive Instinct (1/Day) Th e solmundis can dart its tongue in quick succession.

Reaction Trigger: When the solmundis fails to hit with its Tongue Harpoon  Attack. Response  -  Dexterity  Saving  Th row: DC  11,  the original  target  for  the  Tongue  Harpoon  attack. Failure: 7  (2d6) Slashing damage. Success: Half damage.

## Actions

Multiattack Th e  solmundis  makes  two  attacks  with  its  Toothy Maw, Webbed Tail, and Tongue Harpoon in any combination. +3 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. 4  (1d6+1)  Piercing  damage.  If  the  target  creature  has  the Restrained condition from the solmundis' Tongue Harpoon, it

Toothy Maw Melee Attack Roll: Hit: deals an additional 3 (1d6) Piercing damage.

Webbed Tail Melee Attack Roll: +3 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. Hit: 4 (1d6+1) Bludgeoning damage.

Tongue Harpoon Ranged Attack Roll: +3 to hit, reach 10 ft., one target. Hit: 8  (2d6+1)  Piercing  damage. Dexterity Saving Th row: DC  12, one  Medium  or  smaller  creature  the  solmundis  has successfully hit with its Tongue Harpoon. Failure: Target has the Restrained condition (escape DC 12) and takes 2 (1d4) Piercing damage on each of the solmundis' turns until the harpoon is removed. Th e solmundis cannot use its Tongue Harpoon while currently grappling a creature.

## Bonus Actions

Tongue Th rash A solmundis that has successfully harpooned a target can use its Bonus Action to rip the harpoon free of the target forcefully. Th e  target  creature is  then dealt deal 3 (1d6) Slashing damage and it loses its Restrained condition.

Encountering While wary of most creatures larger than itself, if a Traveler chooses to use live fi  sh bait, wriggling about in the mud, the  solmundis  could  be  coerced  out  of  hiding.  If  these offering continues for several days, the large salamander may soon begin to approach the Traveler, its long tongue lolling out in a friendly manner, expecting a new treat.

## Baughur

Class Mammalia · Genus Derived Evolution Tapirus · Niche Generalist Semi-Aquatic Herbivore Preyed upon by Basilospri, Nicalliath, Nuthorghull, Slannethiss, Vinnirex

Wading through the thick muck and mire of the Fenlands, the baughur looks to be nothing less than a small fleshy island when breaching the water. Flies and birds orbit its thick hide as it shovels its tusked face into the thick sediment, its prehensile snout seeking out aquatic root vegetables. Often quite calm and docile when in their dozens-strong drove of close kin, the baughur will attempt to sink to the bottom of the marsh when confronted by any terrestrial creature threat. Only resorting to violence with their goring tusks when protecting their young or spotting their most hated rivals in the Fenlands, the pebbleback auhk. Often standing their ground with a display of loud splashes and deep-barreled chest grunts.

Bloodied Waters Trigger: When damage reduces the baughur to 0 Hit Points. Response - Constitution Saving Th row: DC 5 + damage taken from the most recent hit, unless it's from a Critical Hit. Success: Th e baughur drops to 1 hit point instead.

Encountering Loud  snorts  and  gargled  underwater  bellows announce the presence of a herd of baughur long before they can be seen. Upon approaching the semi aquatic mammals, they are usually quite peaceful if met with calm, quiet movements. If the smell of blood begins to permeate the surrounding area, or they hear overly loud noises, they will quickly flee to safer areas, goring and stampeding any that stand in their way. e

With nerve-dulled thick skin, the baughur's pallid flesh is sparsely covered in  hair  akin  to  a  fruit  rind.  Circulating blood  only  reaches  the  surface  of  the extremities,  such  as  the  ears  and  feet. Leading to a durable numbness to all but the most grievous of wounds

## Glomunder

Class Amphibia · Genus Derived Evolution Xenopus · Niche Generalist Amphibious Omnivore Preyed upon by Fennfi   stle, Nicalliath, Nuthorghull, Sacrissian · Preys Miremeer, Sprootle, Herragath ,

Solmundis

In the muck of the Mirewynn swamps, a dorsal fan breaches the water as a powerful tail churns the calm surface. Th e glomunder does not make for a subtle hunter, yet even so, many larger predators keep their distance unless desperate, as the large frog-like creature has a means of protection wholly unique to the swamps. When cornered by a voracious predator, it will swing its head violently, splashing a viscous saliva from its two sinuous tendrils that whip back and forth as it tries to slap and spread a debilitating neurotoxin. Once in contact with this thick yellow fluid, extremities fall numb and limp and, before long, the afflicted creature fi  nds itself sinking into the stagnant water, incapacitated and an easy meal for the amphibious glomunder.

## Larkiera

Class Actinopterygii · Genus Derived Evolution Hydrocynus · Niche Generalist Piscivore Preyed upon by Fennfi   stle, Nuthorghull, Sacrissian, Silvette · Preys upon Finimander, Frondilli, Parro, Murrow

In  the  dappled,  sullen  green  light  of  a  wide  river,  a  predator  slides  through  the  tall  water  grass  on  the  hunt.  Its  long  sleek  body swimming silently, its peculiar crescent-shaped pupils seeking movement in the vertical kelp forest, as well as discerning any variation in color that breaks through the deep greenery. Th roughout the sullen day the larkiera patrol the long rivers that stretch through the Wealdwoods, the dark of the canopy above suiting their stalking behavior. Th ough an effective hunter with its trap-like teeth and agile maneuverability, the larkiera is far from the top of the Fenland's trophic food-chain, and so it protects itself with a quickly lashing tail and dozens of dorsal spines that deter larger threats from immediately attacking it.

Unlike many creatures in the rivers, the larkiera is solely a hunter, and not a scavenger.

## Murrow

Class Actinopterygii · Genus Derived Evolution Bagarius · Niche Marine Ambush Carnivore Preyed upon by Larkiera, Nicalliath, Nuthorghull · Preys on Parro, Glomunder, Solmundis

Within the gloomy silt of the Fenlands' most stagnant waters, the blind murrow moves with the certainty of a vicious hunter. With a snout covered in barbels, sensitive to the electromagnetic pulses of the heartbeat and movement of nearby fauna, the fi  sh can sense prey with lethal ease. Rising slowly, its sullen flesh blending with the murk, the lilotoal that sits contently above the water, barely perceiving the looming threat below, cannot hop away in time as the lily pad and surrounding water is suddenly sucked into the engulfi  ng wide maw of the hungry fi  sh. Filling its gullet, the large catfi  sh sinks down, deep into to the thick mud, digesting and momentarily satisfi  ed.

Reaction Trigger: When the murrow reaches 0 hit points. Response - Constitution Saving Th row : DC 5 + damage taken from the most recent hit , unless it was a critical hit. Success: Th   e murrow drops to 1 Hit Point instead.

## Actions

Tail Slap Melee Weapon Attack: +5 to hit, reach 10 ft., one target. Hit: 13 (2d8 + 3) Bludgeoning damage.

Siphon Gulp Strength Saving Th row: DC 16, each creature in a 15 ft. Cone. Failure: If the creature is Medium or smaller, it is swallowed. A swallowed creature has the Blinded and Restrained Conditions, has  Total  Cover  against  attacks  and  other  effects  outside  the murrow, and takes  7  (2d6)  Acid  damage  at  the  start  of  each  of the murrow's turns. If the murrow takes 12 damage or more on a single turn from a creature inside it, the murrow must succeed on a DC 16 Constitution Saving Th row at the end of that turn or regurgitate all swallowed creatures, each of which falls in a space within 5 ft. of the murrow and unless underwater, has the Prone condition. If the murrow dies, any swallowed creatures no longer have  the  restrained  condition  and  can  escape  from  the  corpse using 5 ft. of movement, exiting Prone. Th e murrow can swallow up to one Medium creature or three Small creatures at a time.

Encountering Often only seen as a shadow beneath the depths of stagnate algae-choked waters, the murrow is diffi  cult to spot in murky waters. With its uncanny ability to detect the heartbeat of nearby creatures, the best method to attract the murrow is to use live thrashing bait, luring it to the surface and waiting for its engulfi  ng gulp.

Webbed feet step gently over the marsh lily pads as the lanky fennfi  stle searches for its favored meal, eggs. Th is opportunistic ovivore keeps a gold-flecked eye out for either bird eggs nested within the rushes and cattails along the shore, or for the glimmerings of the ovoid shapes of amphibian and fi  sh eggs beneath the surface. Its long sinuous neck perfectly adapted to quickly gulp down its prize into an expandable, horned gular and rush off before any protective parent or nearby predator can catch this nimble beast. With wide webbed toes and bounding jumps, it leaps across rivers and ponds. Th is amphibian is perfectly adapted to the damp environments of the Fenlands and can outpace any creature that ventures into its swampy home.

## Rauka

Class Mammalia · Genus Derived Evolution Sus · Niche Aquatic Generalist Omnivore Preyed upon by Nicalliath, Nuthorghull, Slannethiss, Vinnirex

A spray of exhaled breath reveals the tusked gray head of a rauka rising from the brackish water of a river delta. Th is territorial omnivore glares over the tumultuous water with beady eyes as its thick jowls clench, chewing the river grass it had swam below to gather. Roaming in slow moving herds of dozens of individuals, these aggressive mammals often clear out large expanses of the river mouth from any competing creatures, as most do not want to contend with the shearing tusks that twist out from their blunt heads, capable of goring and puncturing both hide and thick shell with ease. Th ough aquatic, they have been known to attack any terrestrial aggressors with a surprisingly violent charge, erupting onto the beach in short bursts, their strong forelimbs pulling their heavy bulk into a lethal range.

## Silvette

Class Actinopterygii · Genus Derived Evolution Ctenolucius · Niche Aquatic Carnivore Scavenger Preyed upon by Nicalliath, Nuthorghull · Preys on Larkiera, Murrow, Parro, Rauka, Varrip

From white rapids rise a pair of horns as a silvette races upstream following the scent of blood still fresh in the rushing waters. Th ese scavengers, armored in plates of overlapping scales reminiscent of a fi  sh from a bygone era, battle with larkiera and nicalliath for recent kills. To contend with such competition, razored protrusions along their face and hardened scales protect them while they wrestle for hunks of meat. Lacking the patience of an ambush predator, these fi  sh instead migrate great distances, attacking every fi  sh and amphibian they can smell, with in an unshaken faith in the protection of their armored scales. While this aggressive behavior has seen this species spread through much of the Fenlands, many larger predators have taken advantage of the mindless brute.

## Pebbleback Auhk

Class Mammalia · Genus Derived Evolution Connochaetes · Niche Generalist Semi-Aquatic Omnivore Preyed upon by Nicalliath, Nuthorghull, Slannethiss, Vinnirex

In small, tightly knit herds of a dozen kin, the pebbleback auhk wallows in the Fenlands, chewing marsh plants and digging up mollusks to chew on their shells for the rich calcium they require to build up their namesake's bony plates. Th ough rather slow grazing beasts, predators keep a wary distance from them, for not only does their bony overlapping osteoderms give them a measure of protection from claw and tooth, their wide hardened horns can batter any would-be hunter into a muddy red ruin. While not particularly aggressive, there's a certain obstinate movement in how they claim their wetland territories, sometimes even physically pushing baughur out of fertile swamps, relying on their armored hide to defend against any irritated, retaliatory tusked charges.

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

Wide  and  squat  limbs  wade  through  the Fenlands tirelessly, their four toed feet end in hoof-like nails that allow a strong grip in algae and mud-thick waters

<!-- image -->

Haunting bird call carries over the still gray water, reflecting the overcast sky above. A soft splash echoes, followed by another from the gentle rhythm of a paddling canoe. The Traveler rolls their shoulders, still tired from the upriver struggle, but now finally settling into the peace of the lake that opens wide before them, hemmed in by a standing thicket of trees that thickly encroach upon the shore.

Breathing a sigh of relief, they pull in the oar and settle into the canoe, getting their journal from the knapsack that they used as back support and begin to sketch their recent findings. The Fenlands, while as dangerous as any other inch of this primal land, hosts creatures unsure of what to make of the curious wooden object that floats above them so, in a rare moment, the Traveler relaxes.

Nearly nodding off in the warm afternoon, a bump shakes them awake. Gripping their journal to their chest and slowly peering over the edge, a long train of mottled, rubbery flesh rises in a sequence of rounded peaks, leaving no ripple in their wake. Each undulation bumping the canoe as the monstrously huge aquatic creature traverses its watery domain, unconcerned by what it must think is only drift wood as it hunts for a larger meal. Not wanting to dispel the creature of its misunderstanding, the Traveler quietly settles back into the canoe and patiently waits before attempting to row once more to the distant, and hopefully safer huddle of trees near the lake shore.

The Fenlands favor adaptability, and few creatures exemplify that more than amphibians, so it is of little surprise that one such as that grew to be the largest and most dominating creature in this watery ecosystem. The nuthorghull flourishes only because most other creatures would otherwise be limited by the confines of their environment. While best suited for an aquatic existence, this incredibly long tailed salamander can pull itself out onto the muddy banks to seek terrestrial prey or, just simply, find fresher more abundant bodies of water. While much larger, it fills the niche once held by crocodiles, able to hunt through the murk with the use of its catfishlike barbels, tasting the presence of nearby prey. These tactile, extrasensory organs allow the amphibian to sift through the cold mud and focus in on the trail of scents that lead to live prey, eschewing the need for vision within these dark and obscure depths. When winter approaches and the lakes become cold and stagnant, the salamanders will settle into the mud to hibernate, their long sinuous tails storing rich fats to endure long unmoving seasons. Unlike many species, the nuthorghull will breed in the late seasons and spend their wintering hibernation clutching their spawn of eggs. Nestled in clutches, both males and females protect their future offspring from the frigid water and any hungry ovivores that may want to stifle the next generation.

## Fenlands Apex Influence

The  appetites  of  apex  predators  have  a  massive  influence  on  their  surrounding  ecosystem;  even  in  their  absence,  the  impact is  noticeable.  While  many  of  the  Fenlands  megafauna  may  not  always  feel  the  hunger  of  a  nuthorghull  in  their  environment, after millions of years of island isolation, they have all adapted to be wary of the great amphibian that looms in the mud and silt. Only swimming in the deepest of lakes and widest of rivers, the nuthorghull does not claim large swaths of territory for itself like many other apex predators, and it instead grows to fit the competition it lives within, and when surrounded by many of their own species, will grow to less monumental sizes. Though Rootwell may be home to the largest of their kind, the isolation of the single lake leaves them little space to breed and it may not be long before they disappear from that black, forested lake. Some of their young attempt to breach into the many rivers that feed the large central lake, but can only go so far before the rivers narrow into rapids. Further east, shallow Four Moon has managed to create a kind of community of nuthorghull that settle in the mud, reaching out to the three smaller satellite lakes to feast and leave the larger middle lake to spawn and hibernate. To the north west, the mountain's frigid waters of the Inmere lake leave the amphibians more sluggish than their southern kin. Though their movements are more languid, they  feast  and  breed  with  ease  as  the  brackish  Astradale  bay  and  the  Rhaen  river  feeds  them  untold  numbers  of  migrating  prey. Even with their vast appetite, they are merely a hurdle to overcome for many of the fish that run these waters each breeding season.

As the nuthorghull are unrivaled in size, many of the Fenlands' other predators are found to be more stealthy than aggressive, as they could never hope to contend with the massive amphibian in being the environment's dominating species. Larkiera and murrow use the dour tones of their body to hide among the grass and mud, silvette keep to the surface and hope to pass quickly unseen in the river Rhaen. While the rest remain near the water, quick to rush away from the edge the moment the great beasts rears its wide head. Many herbivores and grazers, who would otherwise clear the lake bottom of sea weed are then left absent, leading to massive flourishing growths that enrich the surrounding forests and rivers downstream. Left unchecked, this often leads to algae blooms that cover vast stretches of the lakes in a thick, green, choking carpet.

Fortunately for many, the environments in which the nuthorghull dwell within keep it mostly contained to those vast and deep waters, as it can be quite slow and lumbering to carry its weight outside the water. For those brave enough to dive into the black depths, there is little they can do to ward off the beast. Lying in thick layers of mud along the bottom, most of its massive form rests unseen. Once it begins to move, only the stirring clouds of silt warns of its approach. Algae-choked waters leave little light to illuminate its domain and any submerged creature, be it fish or Traveler, have little hope to escape its gaping maw once their questing barbels have tasted and awakened its unending hunger.

<!-- image -->

Whisker-like  appendages  trail  off  the creature's  face,  questing  in  the  muck with their sensitive taste-receptors to track aquatic fauna

Short and stocky limbs support the amphibian's  heavy  weight  when  it drags itself out to find new bodies of water, or purse terrestrial prey

Th e nuthorghull can swallow up to one Large creature (or six Medium creatures or ten Small creatures) at a time.

## Lair Actions

On  initiative  count  20  (losing  initiative  ties),  the  nuthorghull takes  a  lair  action  to  cause  one  of  the  following  effects,  the nuthorghull can't use the same effect two rounds in a row.

Churning  Waters Th e  nuthorghull  beats  its  tail  in  the  water, Dexterity Saving Th row: DC 15, each creature within water, in a 40 ft. Cone. Failure: Target creature has Disadvantage on Dexterity rolls until the nuthorghull's next turn.

Depth  Drop Th e  nuthorghull  sinks  under  the  murky  water, which  doesn't  provoke  an  Opportunity  Attack  when  it  moves out  of  an  enemy's  reach.  Within  moments  the  nuthorghull reappears in a free area of water within 40 ft. of itself.

Retch Th e  nuthorghull violently discharges the contents of its stomach, including any creatures it has swallowed, which exit Prone within 5 ft. of the nuthorghull. Dexterity Saving Th row: DC 14, each creature in a 15 ft. Cone. Failure: 36 (8d8) Acid damage. Success: Half damage.

Surging Charge Th e nuthorghull moves its swim speed towards a hostile creature it can see. If the nuthorghull reaches its target, it  can  immediately  make  a  Cranial  Ram  attack  against  that creature.

Billowing Silt Using its long tail, the nuthorghull stirs up clouds of mud and muck. Water in a 30 ft. Emanation from the nuthorghull becomes heavily obscured until the start of the next round.

[Seasonal]  Muddy  Banks Violently  thrashing  its  body,  the nuthorghull splashes the coast with massive amounts of muddy water. Th e nuthorghull targets a 20 ft. square area of the ground, 30 ft. away from it. Dexterity Saving Th row: DC 15, each creature in that area. Failure: Target creature has the Prone condition.

## Regional Effects

Only in the deepest of lakes and widest of rivers can the massive nuthorghull salamander be found. Hiding amongst the dark silt and mud, patiently waiting to swallow any that venture too near.

Blooming Growth Evicting many herbivores, the aquatic plants within 6 miles of the Lair have had the chance to flourish. Any Foraging Expeditions will yield double the discovered result. Flourishing Darkness Algae growths thicken and spreads across the lakes within 5 miles of the Lair, darkening the depths beneath. Aquatic  creatures  native  to  the  Fenlands  have  Advantage  on Dexterity (Stealth) checks made while it is underwater.

Barren Waters Th e  nuthorghull's  insatiable  appetite  exhausts much of the fi  sh within 4 miles of the Lair. All Fishing Expedition Casts attempts are made with Disadvantage.

Serene Edge Carnivores have kept their wary distance from the lake edge and Travelers can rest without fear of predation. Short Rests taken while within 3 miles of the Lair add an additional Hit Dice to health regained.

Muddy Banks Th e large amphibian breaching the lake edge has flattened plants and turned it into a slick mud within 2 miles of its Lair. 10 ft. from the water's edge has turned muddy and costs an 5 extra ft. to move through it, creatures that Dash across the muddy banks must succeed on a DC 10 Dexterity Saving Th row or get the Prone condition.

Muck and Silt Within 1 mile of the Lair, underwater visibility is  reduced to 10 ft. due to stirred-up silt and algae. Creatures not  native  to  the  Fenlands  have  Disadvantage  on  Wisdom (Perception) checks while underwater.

## Laststep Lily

Trap  Space The  large  laststep  lily  floats  on  ponds  and  lakes surrounded by regular water lilies. The laststep lily is a circular plant taking up a 5 ft. Circle.

Countering While it  looks  like  regular  water  lilies,  its  larger size  is  a  clear  indication  of  its  species. Wisdom  (Perception) Check: DC 8. Success: The Natural Trap is identified.

Senses None

Immunities Blinded, Bludgeoning, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion, Frightened, Prone

AC 1

HP 2 (1d4)

Speed 0 ft.

## Reactions

Numbing Tendrils Trigger: When a creature  steps  within  its Trap Space. Response -Dexterity Saving Throw: DC 14, the creature that triggered the Trap. Failure: The target is pulled underwater and has the Restrained condition (escape DC 10).

## Botany

A behemoth amongst others of its kind, this carnivorous lily pad rests on the water looking to be a firm and stable step to cross rivers with ease, its thick fibrous leaves nearly raft-like in their structure. This is all but a feint, the moment a sizable animal steps up on its surface, sweat-like beads of sticky sap spread across its surface and the leaf twists itself downwards in a tight spiral, pulling its catch below. For weeks it will remain under the surface, digesting its cocooned prey slowly.

<!-- image -->

Large Natural Trap, Unaligned

AC 1

Immunities Blinded, Bludgeoning, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion, Frightened, Piercing, Prone

HP 11 (2d10)

Speed 0 ft.

Senses None

Trap Space Across a wide swath of lake or river bottom grows a small forest of wriggleweed, taking up a 10 ft. cube of space.

Countering These  thin  dark  green  leaves  are  difficult  to  see  as they flow with the water and other sea grasses. Wisdom (Perception) Check: DC 18 . Success: The Natural Trap is identified.

## Reactions

Grasping Grass Trigger: When a  creature  enters  its  Trap  Space below  the  water. Response -Dexterity  Saving  Throw: DC  12,  the creature  that  triggered  the  Trap. Failure: The  target  has  the Restrained  condition.  As  an  action,  a  creature  Restrained  by  the wriggleweed can try to escape from them, doing so with a successful DC 12 Strength (Athletics) or Dexterity (Acrobatics) check.

## Botany

The long grasping strands of wriggleweed, curling on themselves like the tentacles of a cephalopod hold fast against other plants and fish with an iron grip. Not to consume, but to simply grow and spread, soaking in as much of the sun that it can underneath the water. While not intentionally aggressive, the reactive grass has been known to drown many wading creatures, pulling them beneath the surface and into the silt and mud below.

<!-- image -->

## Fenfall Fungus

Large Natural Trap, Unaligned

AC 16 HP

Trap Space An  unassuming patch of  wet  mud,  measuring  a 10 ft. Circle disguises the thousands of mycelium strands that tunnel down 30 ft. into the soft earth.

Countering Indistinguishable from regular mud, only the lack of  plant  growth  belies  its  lethal  nature. Wisdom  (Perception) Check: DC 21 . Success: The Natural Trap is identified.

Senses None

Immunities Blinded, Bludgeoning, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion, Frightened, Piercing, Prone, Slashing

55 (10d10)

Speed 0 ft.

## Reactions

Devouring Mycelium Trigger: When a creature enters its Trap Space. Response -Strength Saving Throw: DC 16, the creature that triggered the Trap. Failure: The creature begins to sink 1d4 + 3 ft. into the fenfall fungus and has the Restrained condition. At the start of each of the creature's turns, it sinks another 1d4 ft.. As long as the creature isn't completely submerged in fenfall fungus, it can take an Action to try to escape, doing so with a successful Strength (Athletics) check DC 10 plus the number of feet the creature has sunk into the fungus. A creature that is completely submerged in the fungus has Total Cover and the Blinded condition and risks suffocation.

A  creature  can  pull  another  creature  within  its  reach  out  of the fungus by taking an action and succeeding on a Strength (Athletics) check DC 5 plus the number of feet the creature has sunk into the fungus.

Failing any attempt to escape sinks the creature another 1d4 ft.

## Biology

A slurry of mud and a submerged column of thousands upon thousands of minute criss-crossing threads of mycelium make up this fungus. The brown and green root-like structures while individually weak, are capable of pulling in unison, gripping and dragging biological matter into its depths.

Medium Natural Trap, Unaligned

AC 1 HP

7 (2d6)

Speed 0 ft.

Immunities Blinded, Bludgeoning, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion, Frightened, Piercing, Prone, Slashing

Red  Feast Trigger: When  a  creature  enters  its  Trap  Space. Response -Dexterity  Saving  Throw: DC  15,  the  creature  that triggered the Trap. Failure: 7 (2d6) Acid damage at the start of each of the triggering creatures turns for as long as the creature is within the algae Trap Space.

## Botany

Like  spilled  blood,  the  algae  sits  on  the  surface  of  stagnant ponds and lakes. Small fibers, lined with a layer of proteases and hydrolyze enzymes break down proteins and other organic compounds that come in contact with it. A raft-like mass that floats on the water absorbs the nutrients, growing and dividing smaller  progeny  as  a  form  of  asexual  reproduction  to  cover more of the water's surface.

## Trespassing Among Ancients

The Ecology of the Wealdwoods

Stepping into a clearing, a ray of sunlight pierces the leaf coverage above and alights on a bed of fern and flowers, the rich gold and greens beckon the Traveler into a sanctuary of safety. Motes of pollen dance in the faint breeze, with only the song of birds from above to break the otherwise gentle silence. Closing their eyes but for a brief moment, the Traveler lets out a long held breath, feeling a sort of peace and oneness with the land comes over them. Suddenly a twig breaks and the Traveler's eyes open with a snap to find four sets of yellow eyes staring from the shadows at the clearing's edge.

With spear held tight, feet planted firmly and the Traveler readies themselves to be charged. A few rapid heartbeats pass, then more, and the tension in their shoulders lessen as the distant eyes blink but approach no further, a sense of curiosity is felt from the staring and slowly the Traveler turns their spear tip downward into the soft loamy earth and gently approaches with an open hand outstretched.

Walking towards the wall of trees, shadowed bodies separate from the branches and beaks questioningly clatter. Four white feathered heads, tilt and turn to inspect the strange intruder in their wooded realm. Lithe forms drop from the branches and began prodding and pulling at the Traveler's cloak. Overcome with an unexpected joy, the Traveler quietly laughs at the reception, turning about to allow the avians to sate their innate curiosity. Finally breathing out a sigh of true relief, the Traveler pulls out their journal and begins to sketch the inquisitive calitross.

A primordial tension can be felt when stepping beneath the branches of the Wealdwoods, a rolling sea of trees that covers much of the eastern side of the island. Like the teeming reefs that crest the south, the woods are an ecosystem of vibrant flora and very territorial fauna. Through countless generations of fierce competition, from the fungus filled leaf litter, crawling with equal parts life and decay, to the nearly vertical living of vine choked towering tree trunks, to the sunlit canopy heights where the skies carry their own dangers.

Life has ruthlessly fought for every inch of space, and the line that separates predator and prey blurs with the slightest misstep, a beast that prowls the branches above may fall to the ground and find themselves entering a wholly alien environment, mere dozens of feet from the niche they once dominated, at the mercy of ground dwelling scavengers that quickly approach.

## Towering Life

To a Traveler, the Wealdwoods could be a near endless supply of resources in both plant and animal life to survive off of. Beyond the  simple  lumber  of  a  felled  tree,  countless  fungi  invade  the root, trunk and canopy above. Dozens of colorful specimens may supply a myriad of medicinal uses, or deadly poisons to those with the knowledge on how to make use of it, yet tread softly under these boughs. The raucous destruction of cutting down a tree or the heavy footfalls of a large party can attract undue attention.

The  Wealdwoods  are  not  only  home  to  towering  trees  and  the beasts  that  walk  beneath  them,  numerous  rivers,  streams,  and wetlands  cut  through  much  of  these  lands.  Herds  of  baughur wallow  through  the  shallows,  covered  in  mud  and  chewing through root and vine, while the fast moving rapids are home to larkiera,  their  cartilaginous  spines  shearing  through  the  water while  their  toothed  maws  snap  up  smaller  fish.  Much  can  be found by the waters edge here for a Traveler, and if fortunate, even a quite moment to put their fishing rod to use.

Scavengers  roam  along  the  ground  as  much  as  among  the branches, and a fallen corpse rarely lasts a full night. Everything from scurrying scavick,  cunning  calitross,  to  winged  basilospri will quickly strip the meat off the bones, leaving scraps that are readily consumed by the countless insects that crawl under the wet leaves and slick stones.

Carrier Beetle

Length - 3.2 in.

A long prominent horn bedecks the head of male carrier beetles. Used primarily in  territorial  and  mating disputes with other males of their species

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

Roots sink deep into the clear waters of this sunken Wealdwoods, the furthest eastern forest on Sol'Kesh. Tributaries run through these woods akin to veins and during spring there is hardly a patch of soil not under the flowing waters. Yet unlike the rotten bog woods found in the north, the running rivers present a clear, heady scent of fresh dew. Many fish and amphibians call the safety of these roottwisted depths home, as the natural cover is an ideal nursery for young, keeping them safe from the larger predators such as murrow and the dreaded nuthorghull, and while safe from some, lithe and agile fish still haunt these waters. Vicious larkiera in particular, with their sinuous bodies and powerful fins can chase down most aquatic prey, weaving around the twisting confines with ease, and when the opportunity arises, breach the still surface to snatch unsuspecting frondilli.

<!-- image -->

## Verdant Sanctuaries

Locations of the Wealdwoods

## Green Moot

Cyclopean trees stand sentinel over the fierce winds of both field and ocean. The tall trees that rise between the fields of Valedayn and the Rhaen river host a large amount of skyelarx hollow-dens. From up high in these bone strewn hollows, the small winged kits mewl as their parents range far out to the fields south of them to search out freshly slain creatures from roaming packs of draffers. Deeper within the woods where the wind cannot be felt, bands of aphistus pluck fruit and seed, ever wary of getting the attention of a roaming jhotus, that claim the forest as their own.

## Amberdale

Nearly unremarkable for its insignificant size and lack of larger predators, there is a hidden secret beneath the soil of this small forest. Just beneath the roots, a thick layer of amber rests under the  forest  floor.  A  Traveler  could  harvest  great  chunks  of  the ancient resin, if they're careful not to disturb the ricatrillisk and basilospri that keep to the canopy above.

## Thornfen

The encroaching mire to the forest's south leaves little nutritional value in the soil for the birch tree forest to grow strong. Still these woods make for a safe resting  point  for  the  seasonal  flights  of drafian  that  glide  out  from  the  Wealdwoods  to  migrate  to  the fields abroad. Aphistus enjoy the relative safety of the unpopulated forest,  nesting  their  young  in  the  underbrush,  their  avian  eyes watching for the sharpened mandibles of awaiting korrask that could violently erupt from ground.

## Aphistus Chick

Height - 1.6 ft.

The  chicks  are  quick  to  leave their nests to explore their surroundings and get comfortable walking, all the while under the close watch of their mother

Stonebarrow Beetle

Length - 1.7 in.

Squaring off against the large avian, this beetle is readying for a foolish charge

## Arborn

Nestled among the foothills and steep slopes that rise to the skybreaking  Splitspine  mountain,  these  woods  are  the  last  haven of  cover  from  the  arid  Grayfynn.  These  woods  harbor  denizens unlike  many  of  the  other  Wealdwoods,  as  the  loamy  grounds are riddled with tunnels that lead to the limestone caverns that runs  beneath  the  western  side  of  the  island.  Tottering  tormull can be found in the waning months, chewing through fallen trees and rotten stumps to carry the fermenting plant matter to their cave dwelling brood. During moonless nights, acrynn have been known to stalk these woods, leaping to the branches and pulling lexati violently from their nests.

## Last Bloom

In  the  central  mountain  ranges,  the  evergreen  pines  that  rise along  the  snow  covered  slopes  and  granite-bare  cliffs  stand firm in the fierce winds of the peaks. Pine covered branches give shelter to calitross flocks that crawl over the snow swept heights while white-furred frondilli leap from tree to tree, drinking deep of the sugary sap, keeping a cautious eye on the outskirts for any draffers willing to enter into their woods. Closer to the peaks can be found the stout twisted boughs of ironwood trees, the mineralrich soil giving their bark a slight sheen in the wane light. While a rare johtus, its shaggy fur covered in pine needles, chews through carpets of moss that blanket the boulders that lay strewn about the woods.

<!-- image -->

Mistwallow periodically release a fine fog of methane, leeched from the surrounding rotten soil and plant litter. While rarely lethal, condensed patches of mistwallow can be silently deadly

<!-- image -->

## Tanglewood

A sprawling forest of deciduous trees spreads far and wide, grown rich from the ocean-blown western winds. With consistent rains, and the wetlands to the south, the Tanglewood is a forest crisscrossed with swamps and rivers. Its most prominent lake, the Rootwell, is a deep well of black water and is one of the best locations for fishing, as many fish unseen elsewhere live within its depths; as does the behemoth of an amphibian, the nuthorghull. An apex predator in its own right, capable of breaching the surface and pulling in a drinking aphistus with a single bite of its vast maw. To the south of these woods, willow trees rise from the drowned earth. Baughur and pebbleback auhk's live in the shallow muddy waters, constantly beset by ravenous vinnirex and sacrissian. Along the western reaches where conifers grow in the mountain foothills, lexati soar through the branches hunting scavick. Further north past the Rootwell, the trees grow large, their boughs heavy with mossdown where the largest of jhotus dwell. The near endless woods capable of sustaining several of the far roaming apex predators, only their thunderous footfalls breaking the silence of this primordial sanctuary.

culinch

<!-- image -->

## Howling Beneath The Canopy

Flora and Fauna of the Wealdwoods

The skittish frondilli weaves between trunk to trunk, eyes wide, ears sensitive to the slightest snap of twig or exhaled breath of awaiting predators. Its leaf-like fur conceals it from most eyes, but not all. The basilospri glides from the branches above, alighting on each bough, the thick moss blanketing each branch muffles its landing as it tracks the fleet-footed deer for the single deadly strike. With a blink of its scarlet eye, the reptile launches itself from above, aiding the pull of gravity as it shapes itself like an arrow, throwing its talons forward and aiming for the herbivore below. A wet snap and single cry of pain breaks the silence of an otherwise quiet afternoon beneath the leaves.

Under the overarching canopy of the Wealdwoods, life has learned to grow in tiers: From the scurrying underbrush, countless detrivores and fungi clear out the rot and detritus; from minute insects to the buried and colossal korrask, waiting for the right moment to kill. Above that, among the bushes crawl burberry beetles and crested quills within the understory, attempting to escape the grasp of an aphistus from pulling the thick leaves into its hungry beak. Among the winding hunting trails, a pack of vinnirex chase a lone scavick that leaps to the low hanging branches of a tree and soon enters a new tier of life. Among the trunks and branches in the canopy, dozens of feet above the ground live untold birds, climbing reptiles and small furred mammals. Smartest among those are the calitross, their flock ever keeping a wary eye out for the bladed talons of both basilospri and skyelarx that fly from branch to branch looking to pounce. Higher still, live those rare few that can witness the sky peeling through the thick leaves of the emergent layer. Green scaled ynnil perch on thin branches awaiting the pass of birds, and lexati glide elegantly on thermals, sunlight dappling their furred wings. These tiers offer a balance in the Wealdwoods not seen in other biomes, clear distinctions of place and purpose, until of course the earth shaking tread of its apex predator tears the colossal trees to the ground, exposing large swaths of the forest floor to streaking sunlight for the first time in decades and upending the rigid structure once more.

<!-- image -->

## Sheltered By The Storm

Seasonal Effects for the Wealdwoods

When the worst of the rain hammers the canopy above, a Traveler might feel themselves protected from nature's fury as they huddle beneath the towering trees of the Wealdwoods. While safe from the strike of lightning or pelting hail, they may still be at the mercy of the changing seasons that affect the flora and fauna within. From new growths that urge migrating beasts to travel, to blooming fungi that spell death for any creature breathing in their drifting spores.

The Wealdwoods dominate the landscapes they grow upon; the Tanglewood sprawls across most of the east, spreading from the marshy Mirewynn to the heights of the Splitspine, while the west harbors isolated sanctuaries of life. Each of these Wealdwoods are home to flora and fauna that respond to the changing seasons, either from hibernating through the cold or migrating to the warmth of lowerlying forests.

## Seasonal Animal Behavior Seasonal Animal Behavior

## Spring

## Awakening Earth

Korrask stir in the thawing soil. Re-roll the first Scavenger roll if it results in anything but a korrask. Korrask gain Advantage to Initiative rolls.

## Arboreal Snipers

A cacophony of birds sing for mates and the ynill are alert and hungry. Double any found on the Hunt Expeditions and Scavenger Tables and ynnil gain Advantage to Initiative rolls. Once per day, their first Venom Spit Ranged Weapon Attack is made with Advantage.

## Nest Wardens

Aphistus protect their nests fearlessly during the spring. When Bloodied, they gain Resistance to Slashing, Bludgeoning and Piercing damage for the duration of the combat encounter.

## Summer

## Fall

## Sweltering Storm

As the humidity rises, so does the buzz of countless crested quills. Double any found on the Expedition Hunt and Scavenger Tables and crested quills gain Advantage to Initiative rolls.

## Aerial Domination

Skyelarx, benefiting from the abundant canopy, spend much of the warmer seasons within the Wealdwoods. Double any found on the Expedition Hunt and Scavenger Tables and they gain Advantage to Wisdom (Perception) checks.

## Blazing Speed

The hot weather instills a manic agility in the ricatrillisk. Ricatrillisk gain the Action: Multiattack The ricatrillisk can make two Hooked Claws attacks.

## Gods of the Woods

With summer waning, jhotus lumber through the old growth woods clearing the way for a new spring. When bloodied they gain +2 AC. They can use the Buried Decay Lair Action during this season.

## Unseen Hunters

As the leaves change to vibrant hues, the elusive shaevrynn is even more difficult to spot. Re-roll the first of any Hunting Expeditions rolls that result in a shaevrynn.

## Desperate Deer

The sap of trees trickle slowly during fall and the fennfistle become more bold to get their full. Fennfistle gain Advantage to their Wisdom Saving Throws.

## Winter

## From Frigid Heights

The feathered calitross thrive in the colder seasons. Double any found on the Expedition Hunt and Scavenger Tables and calitross gain Advantage on Dexterity (Stealth) checks.

## Dormant Canopy

Ricatrillisk, Crested Quill, and Gomitoad hibernate during this season. Re-roll the first Scavenger or Hunting Expeditions rolls if it results in a Ricatrillisk, Crested Quill, and Gomitoad.

## Easy Pickings

With their vibrant 'berries' easily seen in the bare foliage, burberry beetles are found more frequently during this season. Double any found on the Hunt Expeditions and double the amount when Scavenging Burberry components.

## The Bounty Beneath The Boughs

Expeditions within the Wealdwoods

Many Travelers would not consider the root twisted soil of a forest worth mining. Not only does the flora's grip on the earth require great effort to uproot, the fauna could make short work of most exhibitions. Those brave enough to test their strength against the old woods would find resources unlike any other found on the island. Great stores of amber can be found beneath the oldest of the forests, nestled within clay beds and under generations of trees. Mossdown hangs from boughs of trees that have stood the test of time for centuries, bringing new life to the ancient branches with sweet fruit. And though incredibly rare, the twisted ironwood trees that lay low to the earth leech the irons from the soil, providing an incredibly durable material for the crafting of weapons and armor.

## Burberry Beetle

Class Insecta · Genus Derived Evolution Pseudophyllus · Niche Generalist Herbivore

Preyed upon by Aristo Doves, Calitross, Dartiguan, Gomitoad, Maedrynn

Th e humble burberry beetle gingerly walks along the branch of a burberry tree to safely hide amongst the leaves. Th is diminutive insect, which spends most of the year camouflaged safely among the lower foliage, climbs high as the warm seasons wane. While the natural berries of the tree are found shriveled on the branch, the insect brandishes similarly shaped and vibrant orbs to entice migrating birds to dart in and consume the false fruit. Nestled within the digestive tracks of the bird, the burberry beetle larvae stay dormant throughout the winter and travel great distances within the avian migrations, waiting until the warmth of spring to then emerge in their droppings, crawling forth for a new year and new cycle of life.

Burberries Th e  red  eggs  that  stem  from  the  insect  can  be plucked off with a DC 10 Dexterity check without harming the insect. Each burberry beetle carries 1d8 Burberries. Burberries stay  fresh  for  1  Day.  You  may  only  experience  the  effects  of  1 Burberry per day. When consumed, roll a 1d20 and consult the results below for the immediate effect.

- 20 Benefi  t from the effects of a Long Rest.

Twiggy Leap Trigger: When an another creature moves with 5 ft. of the Burberry Beetle. Response: Th e burberry beetle can hop a short distance, allowing it to move up to 20 ft. in any direction. Th e  burberry  beetle  doesn't  provoke  an  Opportunity  Attack when it jumps out of an enemy's reach.

Encountering As its name implies, the burberry beetle spends the  majority  of  its  life  in  a  burberry  tree  and,  while  fi  nding the  specifi  c  plant  can  be  simple  once  a  Traveler  is  within  the Wealdwoods, discovering the hidden beetle within is a much more  diffi  cult  search.  Often,  the  best  practice  is  to  roughly shake the bush in the hope that the insect will leap out.

While  the  beetle  is  rather  small  and harmless,  the  leaves  of  the  burberry tree in which its diet entirely subsists on, builds up a toxin in its body that can be fatal to many predators

A  long  sail  of  branching  ovipositors  rises  over the  insect's  thorax,  acting  as  both  a  camouflaged mimicry of its natural home of tree leaves, and a safe method to the eggs far from the core of its body from any potentially aggressively hungry birds

## Gomitoad

Class Amphibian · Genus Derived Evolution Ansonia · Niche Generalist Arboreal Insectivore Preyed upon by Scavick · Preys on Burberry Beetle, Crested Quill

With strong lithe limbs and large elongated body, the gomitoad leaps from branch to branch in search of camouflaged leaf insects to pounce upon and feast. Living a rather fearless existence for a toad, its brightly colored body warns predators of their toxin-laden skin which works for most, except scavick, who can survive the lethal lunegrove toxin in small doses. Both male and female toads aggressively guard a single tree, wrestling with rival toads with their set of two frontal horns. Unlike most amphibians, the gomitoad has evolved to live in the canopy far from bodies of water. It keeps its body moist in rain water collected in leaves, and when the drier seasons approach, will dig divots in the tree bark to collect rain water to keep itself hydrated.

## Reactions

Lunegrove Toxin Trigger: A creature comes in contact or hits it with an attack roll. Response - Constitution Saving Th row: DC 14, the triggering creature. Failure: Target takes 3 (1d6) Poison damage and has the Poisoned condition from the Lunegrove Toxin. Th e target may repeat the save at the end of each of its turns, ending the  effect  on  itself  on  a  success.  After  5  minutes,  it  succeeds automatically.  While  Poisoned, on  its  turn,  the  creature  is compelled to attack the nearest creature, friend or foe.

Encountering Adept  at  climbing  high  in  the  canopy  above, fi   nding either a gomitoad or its eggs is a rare discovery. Although, during the rainy seasons of spring when the males compete for mating, their high pitched croaks can lead a Traveler to their colossal  treetops  above  where they launch themselves at each other for supremacy.

Gomitoad lay their clutch of eggs in leaf litter, often high in a tree-crotch. Lacking the calcium of avian and reptilian eggs, these soft-gel eggs safeguard the growing tadpole within with the use of dew-catching rents that run along the sides and top

## Frondilli

Class Mammalia · Genus Derived Evolution Cervus · Niche Specialist Gummivore Preyed upon by Basilospri, Korrask, Larkiera, Murrow, Sacrissian, Scavick, Skyelarx, Vinnirex

Skittish and shy, the frondilli is a fleeting sight in the colder timberlands of Last Bloom. With utmost caution, as its frail and small form is easy prey for many predators within the Wealdwoods, the small deer quietly approaches deciduous trees with practiced haste, scratching and digging into the bark with its wedge-like razored jaw. With deep rents cutting into the bark, it will lick its fi  ll of the thick calorie-rich flowing sap. Easily camouflaged in the dense underbrush with its leaf-like green tinged fur, cautiously listening for the slightest potential danger, its sensitive ears swiveling back and forth to hear in all directions. At the slightest snap of a twig or muffled crunch of snow, it will flee deeper into the woods, hidden away beneath concealing brush and branch.

e Encountering Wary of any and all other creatures, the frondilli will either flee long before being seen or hide in the underbrush using its natural camouflage. Outside of the mating months an adult deer will often be found grazing alone or in pairs if they are caring for young fawn. It is possible for a Traveler to lure a frondilli with the rich aroma of syrups or sap, but it will quickly escape from the slightest sign of danger.

## Calitross

Class Aves · Genus Derived Evolution Tyto · Niche Generalist Omnivore Arboreal Scavenger Preyed upon by Basilospri, Lexati, Skyelarx, Vinnirex,Ynnil · Preys on Burberry Beetles, Dartiguan, Lhoupin

In the northern wilds of Sol'Kesh, high amongst the colossal conifers of Last Bloom, the calitross silently climbs. Th is avian creature, having long lost its ability to fly, uses its long limbs to quietly stalk the boughs and cliff crags instead, gathering berries, and if lucky, scavenging recent kills. Surviving in the harsh northern climate has made this versatile omnivore one of the more intelligent creatures in Sol'Kesh, living in small flocks of a dozen family members they have a developed rich vocabulary of chirps, cries and bird song to warn each other of incoming predators, like the basilospri and lexati. Together the flock can distract larger predators to scavenge from their kill, create communal stores of nuts for the colder seasons, and protect their terrestrial fledglings for the next generation.

## Crested Quill

Class Insecta · Genus Derived Evolution Schistocerca · Niche Specialist Ambush Pack Carnivore Preyed upon by Calitross, Gomitoad, Scavick · Preys on Aristo Doves, Dartiguan, Maedrynn

Flitting through the forest understory, the crested quill displays an unusual pack hunting technique. With their spring-like legs, the small swarm synchronously launches themselves at flying birds, their sharp mandibles piercing hide and bringing down prey much larger than themselves. While descended from locusts, these insects do not amass in swarms thousands strong, but instead group together in swarms of their original spawning. Th ese spawn-kin spend most of their lives hunting together until they chance upon the spawn-kin of another, through an array of mesmerizing dances the two swarms will form and split, exchanging males to breed and propagate further swarms to patrol the Wealdwoods.

Encountering A lone crested quill and one living in a pack have two very different behaviors. When alone they act much like any other insect, mostly consisting on carrion and flitting away at the nearest disturbance. When in their spawning pack, a more cunning instinct  overtakes  them,  and  they  act  synchronously when  flitting  through  the  understory  in  search  of  a  prey  to ambush.

## Lexati

Class Mammalia · Genus Derived Evolution Leopardus · Niche Specialized Aerial Omnivore Preyed upon by Basilospri, Skyelarx, Tyrandroth, Ynnil · Preys upon -

Burberry Beetle, Dartiguan, Drafi   an,

With furred arms outstretched, the lexati glides from the mountain cliffs to skim over the wide open Wealdwoods canopy, a sunlit world untouched by the crawling competition and mayhem below. Skillfully snatching birds and large flying insects mid flight and feasting on the fruits of the highest branches. Unfortunately, a handful of predators can dispel this arboreal paradise with the gliding mammal often having to dodge the acidic streams of ynnil spittle or the claws of skyelarx and basilospri. Th ese amiable felines are often found in familial clowders playfully bounding along the tops of the trees. Th ough incapable of true flight, the lexati can rise to great heights by riding warm updrafts to nest in safer, harder to reach areas amongst communal roosts in the treetops or cliff face ledges.

## Ricatrillisk

Class Insecta · Genus Derived Evolution Extatosoma · Niche Specialist Arboreal Fungivore Preyed upon by Basilospri, Jhotus, Sacrissian, Scavick, Skyelarx

A  staccato  of  clicks  can  be  heard  in  the  canopy  above,  among  the  thick  pine  boughs  of  the  colossal  conifers  that  tower  over  the mountainous forests of Last Bloom and Amberdale. While they may look sinister in appearance, the ricatrillisk is nothing but benefi  cial to the health of the Wealdwoods. Clinging to thick trunks it climbs upwards, sniffi  ng out the rot and fungus that infects the oldest trees. Once it fi  nds a sizable growth, it clings tightly to the wide trunk, munching through the tough exterior while its own chitinous hide protects it from aerial predators. Although a solitary fungivore, after mating season, its larvae will cling to the bodies of both male and female adults, as they clamber along the heights, depositing their young onto fungal growths to eat and grow as budding pupae.

## Aphistus

Class Aves · Stem Evolution Eudyptes · Niche -

Specialist Herbivore

Preyed upon by Basilospri, Jhotus, Korrask, Nicalliath, Scavick, Skyelarx, Vinnirex

Nestled between the wide expanse of the Grayfynn and the Stoanhall cliffs, a sanctuary of colossal trees form the Arborne woodlands. Under these immense canopies, deep sonorous bird song fi  lls the air as a troop of aphistus birds rummage through the undergrowth, their long talons pulling aside heavy roots to expose the hard shelled nuts and terrestrial fruit that they crave. Th eir innate strength and ability to manipulate stubborn roots allows them to make durable nests of living woven roots, packing them with leaf litter to incubate their eggs while they venture out in search of food. Th ough generally peaceful creatures, a lifetime of pulling tough roots has bred them to be powerfully strong and when roused to defend their kin, their clawed strikes can tear through hide and shell with frightening ease.

## Basilospri

Class Reptilia · Genus Derived Evolution Anolis · Niche -

Generalist Aerial Carnivore

Preyed upon by Sacrissian, Skyelarx · Preys on Aphistus, Calitross, Frondilli, Lexati, Ricatrillisk, Vinnirex, Ynnil

With a quick snap of its webbed wings the basilospri weaves among the colossal mountain forests canopy. Th is vicious flying lizard is unsuited for long periods of flight, but with its talon tipped phalanges it can pounce and pierce creatures in an instant, while still using the long digits to climb and cling to the immense trees in which it makes its home. Preferring tree dwelling prey, the basilospri is one of the few predators that can pierce the armored carapace of a ricatrillisk. Th ese lizards are highly territorial, and will defend their hunting grounds from their own kind in aggressive aerial disputes that often leave the defeated assailant's wings tattered to plummet to the forest floor. Leaving their broken rivals to the ground dwelling scavengers of the Wealdwoods to fi  nish off.

e Encountering Deep  in  the  woodlands  among  colossal  trees, half eaten carcasses hang from high tree branches to signify the hunting grounds of basilospri. Often found in small groups of three to four individuals, these wily reptiles stare down from their  perches  watching  for  both  prey  and  interlopers.  With  a piercing cry that echoes among the trees, they will aggressively dive at any that could make for an easy meal.

## Shaevrynn

Class Mammalia · Genus Derived Evolution Felis · Niche -

Generalist Carnivore

Preyed upon by Korrask, Skyelarx, Vinnirex · Preys on Calitross, Lexati, Scavick, Solmundis, Troff Trout

Th ick clawed paws soundlessly step onto the exposed bark of a large Wealdwoods tree. A shifting blur of color and light ripples across the root, its form hard to perceive by most denizens of the woods. Hunting in the gloom of dusk, the waning sun plays tricks with shadows and light, enhancing the shaevrynn's already stealthy feline traits. Light-reflecting iridophores within the feline's quill-like fur, react automatically to the colors of the surrounding environment, while they lack any real precision, colors blend and bleed over the shifting fur like water, allowing the feline to stalk nearly invisibly. Th ese silent hunters are rarely seen and rarely caught, only their golden eyes, reflecting the light of the setting sun, betray their innate camouflage.

## Ynnil

Class Reptilia · Genus Derived Evolution Bronchocela · Niche Specialist Ranged Carnivore Preyed upon by Basilospri, Skyelarx, Scavick · Preys on Aristo Doves, Calitross, Lexati

Scampering claws dig deep into the tree bark as the large lizard climbs higher, searching for a better vantage point. Within moments, a feathered blur passes the canopy clearing, and with instinctual precision, the ynill lets loose a stream of dark venom, blinding the hapless bird and dropping it down onto the trees below. Quickly,  the ynill rushes across the branches to claim it before it falls to the floor below. Th ese ranged predators hunt alone once mature, spending most of their day basking in the sun above the canopy. When courting for prospective mates, the males can be found gripping the fragile branches of the tallest trees, spitting their stream of venom as high into the sky as possible, in an effort to impress the darker green females watching from behind the leaf cover.

## Korrask

Class Chilopoda · Genus Derived Evolution Scolopendra · Niche Specialist Ambush Carnivore Preyed upon by Jhotus · Preys on Aphistus, Calitross, Frondilli, Hectatrex, Scavick, Tormull, Vinnirex

A multitude of articulated claws dig deep under root and soil as the korrask waits unseen beneath colossal Wealdwoods trees. Tunneling beneath tree roots is nearly impossible for most creatures, yet this great centipede is able to dig itself a burrow with shovel-like claws easily  with  its  prodigious  strength.  When  near  the  surface  of  well  trod  game  trails,  it  pulls  back  its  primary  mandibles,  tendons stretched taut, and its secondary jaws pull in detritus to cover its awaiting maw. With sensitive antennae questing out on the earth, the insect awaits the slightest vibrations of an incoming creature. As the unfortunate creature steps between the mandibles, they erupt from the earth violently with a fraction of a second, slicing the prey in half, before it pulls the grisly remains into its tooth-lined gullet.

## Jhotus The Old Gods of the Wealdwoods

Leaves gently drift to the ground as birds of all colors fly above and away from the source of a rhythmic pounding that shakes the colossal trees to their roots. The Traveler grips their spear tightly, fearing some imminent natural calamity. A resounding crack and crash is heard through the woods, and the pounding stops, replaced by a hushed silence and the quickened breath of the Traveler.

Venturing onward, stepping lightly over branch and leaf, the forest around them eerily silent but for the sound of wet crunching. Staying low to the ground, the Traveler steps into a clearing that ends in a steep hill of stone and grass, shafts of sunlight twinkle with the dust and pollen that rises off the dark green foliage. It is peaceful for a moment, until, incomprehensibly, the hill turns.

Taking a step back in utter shock, a porcine head as large as the Traveler is tall, looks to peer at the startled biped. Thick mouldering tree pulp falling from yellowed teeth, a large wet nose sniffing the air, before it grumbles deeply and lowers its massive head back to chew upon the old fallen tree. Breathing out the longest, quietest sigh of relief, the Traveler gently settles onto an old stump, pulls out their journal and begins to sketch the enormous beast.

As one of the few colossal mega-fauna on Sol'Kesh, and an extreme example of island gigantism, this massive boar absolutely dominates the Wealdwoods that it calls home. Rarely do any of their kind reach these massive sizes, often dying to infection or injury before entering the eighth or ninth decade of their long life cycle,  when they grow to reach passed the tree line, their limbs creaking and eyes blinded by countless ailments. Often preceded by the sound of heavy resounding foot falls and the crash of rotten branches falling to the ground, the beast is heard long before its shaggy form is seen, slowly pushing through the trees like a natural disaster. U nhurried as it knows little can stand against its thorned hide and terrible strength. The creature's incredibly sensitive nose leads them through dozens of miles of their territory, searching for the festering remains of the dead, rich fungus clusters that riddle the roots and logs, and when famished, the soft wood of dead trees, as the beast can digest nearly anything. While this slow moving beast exudes a calm passive demeanor, when confronted by aggressive predators, like the horned sacrissian or mindlessly voracious nicalliath, it won't hesitate to prove its position as apex, and will batter the contenders to a crushed ruin with its tusks and immense size. The most legendary battles within the Wealdwoods often begin by mistake, when the jhotus inadvertently steps on a waiting korrask and has to violently rip the insect out of the earth to end the ensuing struggle. Though little can slay this beast, when they do eventually perish, their disappearance may lead to an unhealthy rise in rot and fungal decay within the Wealdwoods they called home.

## Wealdwoods Apex Influence

Balance is the key to any ecosystem, supporting something as large as the jhotus, allowing it to grow without exhausting what it could eat, means that the species must greatly benefit its environment in kind, and despite its apparent destructive habits, the jhotus does just that.

Not all Wealdwoods can support families of jhotus, though they may roam alone, they need wide swaths of territory to feast and mate to still maintain a healthy genetic diversity. The Tanglewood, like a vast sea of trees, harbors the most jhotus, with more than enough old groves and sunken swamps to offer the massive beasts the decay they hunger for. The colder climate of the Last Bloom woods, with its syrup laden trees hosts a smaller, but no less dominating group. Lastly, the Greenmoot, a young forest that offers little of the soft rotten woods that the giant swine prefers, instead many of the jhotus that call these greener forests home, will venture up to the white Rhaen rapids, dipping their heads into the cold water and feasting on sea grasses and any unfortunate fish that mistakes their gaping maws for a cave.

Their presence within a forest, while overwhelmingly destructive in appearance, is not just a protective bulwark to hold back more aggressive predators, but one of cultivation. By feasting on the oldest of trees, felling them years before weather would do the same, allows new growth to take root earlier, and rise to the sunlight above. With the ability to digest nearly anything; rotten bones, thick festering pits of fungus and befouled larvae-infested stumps, all are greedily eaten up by the voracious swine. This trophic level of detritivore is often claimed by the smallest of creatures, flies, worms and other pests, but none can match the amount of rot an adult jhotus can clear out. When a new swath of territory is claimed by a family of these beasts, within a few seasons there's a noticeable change in the forest. Impenetrable ground cover is pushed aside to leave wide hunting trails from the beasts relentless tread, and a blossoming growth of low level bushes, their healthy bright leaves attracting all manner of herbivores. And lastly, though sometimes best left unsaid, its enormous piles of excrement fertilize the often nutrient-poor soil of the forest, helping the large trees that the apex predator calls home to grow tall, further expanding the forest and sheltering more beasts within its boughs.

For those stepping into the Wealdwoods that are patrolled by families of jhotus, it is far from the tranquility of newly growing life. While generally calm, they are still beasts, and when confronted by anything that they deem a threat, or even a nuisance, they will not forgo the use of their bulk to prove their dominance. Nothing on the island, beyond the sheer cliffs of the Splitspine can halt the charge of a furious jhotus, when enraged, trees hundreds of feet high will still break from the impact of its enormous shoulders, boulders will crack, and anything else foolish enough to stand in its way. Only when its heaving breath does not catch the invader's scent any longer will it finally settle back into a peaceful journey, munching through its forest.

After  decades  of  age,  knobby  growths  of keratin collect on areas that don't see much friction.  While  generally  slowing  down  the aged  beast,  the  toughened  thick  flesh  is nearly impervious to tooth and claw

Constitution Saving Th row: DC 14, all creatures within the designated area. Failure: 21 (6d6) Necrotic damage. Success: Half damage.

## Regional Effects

Th e Wealdwoods bow to the titanic presence of a jhotus, trees shudder and fall while all but the most conniving predators and prey flee from its earth shaking footfalls.

Fleeing Wildlife Th e inhabitants of the Wealdwoods often flee from the jhotus and are exceptionally skittish. Travelers within 6  miles  of  the  Lair  have  Disadvantage  on  Wisdom  (Animal Handling) checks.

Scarce  Hunts Many creatures  steer  clear  of  apex  predator's hunting grounds, not wanting to contend with such a formidable presence. Travelers within 5 miles of the Lair must re-roll the fi   rst of any creature results for Hunting Expeditions.

Blooming  Growth Within  4  miles  of  the  Lair,  life  has  taken root from the jhotus' effect of clearing out decay. Any Foraging Expeditions will yield double the discovered result.

Scavenger's Wake Within 3 miles of the Lair, the more cunning creatures know to take advantage of the fresh feasts that may lay  in  the  remains  of  the  jhotus'  path.  Double  the  amount  of creatures from the results on the Scavengers Table.

Unfamiliar Paths Trees lay fallen, wide new paths break through the underbrush and new growth covers the old. Travelers within 2  miles  of  the  Lair  attempting  to  track  have  Disadvantage  on Wisdom (Survival) checks. In addition, at the Keepers discretion, any existing maps may become obsolete.

Tree Felling Tremors Trees lay broken and precarious in the wake of a wandering jhotus. At the Keepers discretion, heavy winds or bad luck can cause a branch to break and fall dangerously within 1 mile of the Lair. A Traveler near a tree rolls a 2d20, any result of a 1 has that tree fall. Dexterity Saving Th row: DC 12, target creature. Failure: 11 (2d10) Bludgeoning damage and the terrain becomes Diffi   cult terrain 10 ft. Sphere around the fallen tree.

## Caution Among The Trees

The Natural Traps of the Wealdwoods

## Fel Floret

Tiny Natural Trap, Unaligned

AC 1

Vulnerabilities Fire

Senses None

Immunities

Blinded, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion,

Frightened, Prone

HP 3 (1d6)

Speed 0 ft.

Trap Space While the fel floret itself is tiny, it occupies a 5 ft. Square and will Trigger when a creature enters that space.

Countermeasures The  small  and  unassuming  fell  floret  is difficult to distinguish from nearby flora and leaf litter. Wisdom (Perception) Check: DC 16. Success: The Natural Trap is identified.

## Reactions

Piercing Thorn Trigger: When a creature steps within its Trap Space. Response -Dexterity Saving Throw: DC 12, the creature that triggered the Trap. Failure: 7  (2d6)  Piercing  damage, and the target has the Restrained (escape DC 12) condition as the thorn pierces  their  flesh.  The  thorn  deals  3  (1d6)  Piercing  damage when the creature removes the thorn and no longer has the Restrained condition.

## Botany

Fell  floret  flowers  lay  in  the  underbrush,  hidden  amongst fallen leaves and beneath similarly looking plants. Often found within dimly lit forests, the flower relies on low trophic level scavengers like flesh eating flies, to carry its pollen that blooms once a trapped corpse settles on top of it.

Tiny Natural Trap, Unaligned

Immunities

Blinded, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion,

Frightened, Prone

AC 1

HP 2 (1d4)

Speed 0 ft.

Senses None

Trap  Space The  single  silvery-white  cocoon  hangs  a  dozen feet off the ground, a silken thread rises to the canopy above. While gently swinging in the breeze, glittering dust wafts off, affecting  nearby  creatures  in  a  15  ft.  Emanation  originating from the cocoon.

Countering While  minuscule,  the  light  cocoon  can  be  seen when one knows to look out for it. Wisdom (Perception) Check: DC 10. Success: The Natural Trap is identified.

Moon Madness Trigger: When a creature steps within its Trap Space. Response -Constitution Saving Throw: DC 16, the creature that triggered the Trap. The target repeats the save at the end of each of its turns, fully aware that they are being affected against their will, but unable to stop the riotous laughter.

## Biology

Highly  sought  after  for  its  silver  threads,  the  Moonspun Moth's  cocoon  is  a  rare  find  in  the  Wealdwoods.  When  the gleaming  cocoon  is  found  in  the  center  of  a  clearing,  many would-be  harvesters  unknowingly  breath  in  the  dust  that gently swirl off it.  Triggering  wild  reactions  within  the creature's hypothalamus, letting loose a piercing headache and uncontrollable laughter.

Delicately hanging in the breeze, silver dust gently wafts through the air, affecting any nearby creatures with mind-shattering  psychoactive compounds

Medium Natural Trap, Unaligned
