Darting Seeds Trigger: When a creature steps within its Trap Space. Response -Dexterity  Saving  Throw: DC  14,  the  creature that triggered the Trap. Failure: Roll  2d10+6 to determine the amount of launched seeds, 1 Piercing damage per seed.

## Botany

Twisting up tree trunks, the flowering buds of the seedspear vine  often  flourish  at  a  dozen  feet  above  ground  level.  As  a reaction to potential predators, as well as a method for seed dispersal, any disturbance in the air or strong heat source, will pivot the flowers to launch their seeds in that direction.

<!-- image -->

## Crawvine

Gargantuan Natural Trap, Unaligned

AC 5

HP 16 (3d10)

Speed 0 ft.

Vulnerabilities Fire

Immunities Blinded, Bludgeoning, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion, Frightened, Piercing, Prone

Senses None

Trap Space The crawvine spreads a wide swath of twisting vines along the ground and takes up a dense 20 ft. Square of space, with large opened leaf lobes at the center.

Countering Easily hidden vines spread beneath the underbrush and leaf litter, while the wide open dark green leaf lobes, edged with firm gripping protrusions can be seen in the center of the vine mass. Wisdom (Perception) Check: DC 10. Success: The Natural Trap is identified.

## Reactions

Voracious Craw Trigger: When a creature steps within its Trap Space. Response -Dexterity Saving Throw: DC 14, the creature that triggered  the  Trap. Failure: Target  creature  has  the  Grappled condition (escape DC 14) and is pulled 5 ft. toward the center mouth of the plant every minute. Once within the center of the Trap Space, if the creature is Medium or smaller, it is swallowed. A crawvine may only swallow one creature at a time. A swallowed creature has the Blinded and Restrained Conditions, has Total Cover  against  attacks  and  other  effects  outside  the  crawvine, and takes 3 (1d6) Acid damage at the start of each of its turns. If the crawvine dies, any swallowed creatures no longer have the restrained condition and can escape from the plant using 5 ft. of movement, exiting Prone.

## Botany

Growing above spaces that have vast unseen Umbrawells cave complexes  below  the  earth.  The  acidic  earth  leaves  the  soil nutrient  poor  and  many  plants,  such  as  the  crawvine  have evolved  to  get  their  iron  and  phosphorus  from  other,  more aggressive means.

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

## Life Flourishing Below

The Ecology of the Umbrawells

Water drips from a stalactite, an acidic tang permeates the air and thousands of crimson glowing spores reflect off the damp stone floor, lighting the way for the Traveler to venture deeper in and sate their curiosity on what may grow in these sunless depths. Looking up, the Traveler spies little furred bodies among the garden of fungus above, blind bats silently plucking mushrooms into their mouths.

With pencil in hand, the Traveler sketches in the kaleidoscope of vibrant fungi and little families of mollock. Surprised by just how illuminated the abundant life is in these depths -having expected cramped, pitch-black crevices, lifeless and forlorn - the Traveler, instead, found a kind of sickly, alien landscape of bacterial mats and fungus, but a beautiful flourishing life nonetheless. Gripping their journal closely, the Traveler breathes in the sulfurtinged air and nods to themselves. They are here to learn and explore and, so, deeper they must go.

Through the eons, climate upheavals and devastating mass extinction have nearly scoured life from the surface time and time again. With life's tenacious need to survive, many species have slowly wormed their way beneath the earth to survive. Safe from the harsh radiation of the sun and erratic changes in climate, beneath tons of stone and dirt, life used other sources of energy. Deep wells of sulphuric lakes underneath the earth gave rise to acidophilic microorganisms that have since established the building blocks of a new form of biome that thrived in the darkness, the Umbrawells. Like the crushing depths of the ocean, the extremophiles that eke out a living in this lightless biome rely on incredibly effective use of the environment's resources, conservation of energy and the guiding light of bioluminescence.

## Descending into Peril

For  those  willing  to  delve  into  the  Umbrawells,  the  effort  to discover  the  underground  dwellers  and  the  riches  within  are seldom worth the fatal risk. Through the acidic foundation that has slowly evolved and carved its own unique landscape into these depths, new unseen landscapes have taken root. For the lucky few that can traverse its endless quiet tunnels and acidic black waters, this is a land like no other. Travel deep enough, and one will step upon fields of dimly glowing mold-grass under a glittering ceiling of silken worms, surrounded by towering fungal growths spewing spores that drift down like snow. It's all too easy for one used to the sky above, to think they've stepped onto a new planet all together.

With  such  an  unfamiliar  landscape  revealing  new  niches  for animals to grow into, many evolved at a frightening pace to take advantage  of  them  first  through  adaptive  radiation:  blind  fish multiplied in sunken, unlit lakes; maddening clicks of thousands of  echolocating  beetles  rush  through  vertical  shafts;  even  sunloving lizards have managed to keep warm in the alkaline waters, once they developed the durability to withstand the acid.

Travelers that set out to study and reap the nearly alien life of the Umbrawells will find that the true danger is not the multitude of predatory creatures, but the winding tunnels that worm deeper and deeper under the earth. Within a mere hour of travel, any sense of direction can be lost as paths twist and cross upon each other. Distant tunnels glow with the promising light of the sky only to be met with more bioluminescent fungi. And worse still, the lure of curiosity, as so few have stepped within these lands: the temptation of the next tunnel bend, the glow of a crack in the wall, and a chattering call echoing from below. All too easily, a Traveler may journey deeper than the point of no return.

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

## Drowned Hollows

With the fall of the tide, great gaping cavern mouths reveal themselves, burrowing into the south western limestone cliffs of Sol'Kesh. Their sunken depths are home to all manner of sea creatures that harbor in the safety of the dark confines. Esslinter in particular make great use of the tight tunnels, erupting from crevices in a storm of violence to pull rauka and skith into their abyssal burrows, though at times they may find daellash attempting to encroach on the same claustrophobic confines. Deeper into the cliff faces, above the waterline, thick mats of silk line the wet tunnels. What may seem, at first, to be a safe and dry escape from the underwater horrors, instead offer nothing but the attention of the monstrously enormous, eight-limbed apex predator.

<!-- image -->

## Ohlroot

From the forest's edge, a Traveler would not know where to find a way into the sprawling tunnels of the Ohlroot until they chanced upon  the  massive  stumps  within  the  heart  of  the  Last  Bloom forest.  Remnants  of  colossal  sequoias  that  once  dominated  the Wealdwoods, now petrified into gaping entrances dozens of feet wide. These tunnels, once the thick roots that drank deep of the soil for miles, now hollowed out and petrified, feature a twisting web of mold-lit channels that run just under the earth, a mere dozen feet from the arboreal beasts that tread above.

With numerous entrances from the forest above, the ecosystem of  the  Ohlroot  and  Last  Bloom  blend  as  tormull  come  and  go between the safety of the dark tunnels below with their skittering larvae, and above to chew on the mulch of rotten wood. Korrask often dig great burrows that break into the tunnels, their scythelike  mandibles  poised  to  slice  trespassers  on  the  ground  while their  more  vulnerable  undersides  lay  exposed  below.  Evernumerous scavick are found in the sprawls of the Olhroot more than  anywhere  as  their  heightened  sense  of  smell  is  perfectly adapted for the shallow burrows, leaving them a safe escape from the larger predators that glide between the towering trees.

While dangerous to venture into the Olhroot, a Traveler may find quicker routes through the Wealdwoods by venturing beneath the tangle of thorn and bramble, and in the tunnels below, they must just be sure they can find their way back out.

## Thoanhall

## Undermoor

Seeping  wet  ceilings  drip  foulness  from  the  marshlands  above with twisting blue veins of azacrid glimmer with the light of the bog  bacteria  within  the  ankle-deep  tunnels  that  make  up  the Undermoor. Entrances to the sodden darkness below often look to be just wide puddles on the moors, their calm, black surface obscuring hidden depths.

Only after swimming dozens of feet in terrifying darkness would a  Traveler  find  themselves in the stifling air of the caves. Filled with fungi and insects that feast on the rich decomposed organic material that leaks from the peat bogs above, surrounded by an ecosystem that thrives off of rot. Skith skitter at this level, their long tails and articulated finned legs perfectly suited to the variety of half submerged tunnels.

## Loweth

Below the waves, cavern entrances bore into the undersea cliffs, choked  by  encroaching  coral  and  the  invading  denizens  of  the ocean. Daellash and esslinter vie for territory in these darkened depths, viciously attacking each other on sight, as both compete for the winding claustrophobic tunnels.

If a Traveler can swim past these embattled waterways, they may then find themselves in the drier, pitch black caves of cold bedrock. While devoid of many of the light-giving fungi and bacteria seem in other caverns, these deep chasms still glitter like the starry sky above  when  reflecting  the  light  of  a  Traveler's  torch.  Seams  of charcoal and rare diamonds offer a treasure trove to mine, if one can pry them loose without disturbing the blind acrynn below.

Overlooking the Grayfynn plains, these caverns rest high on the cliff faces that rise up to form the Tundrus highlands. The cavern entrances guarded by the comings and going of tormull as they descend into the Arborne forests below to chew through plant matter, before returning to their swarming brood within the caves. Tunneling dig deep under the Tundrus plain, stalactites and stalagmites crowd the tight confines as fungal growths light the labyrinthine maze with life. Bioluminescent spores dance on the still air while mollock crawl in great numbers, feasting on the plethora of mushrooms and carrying their spores along the winding tunnels in their fur to spread them further. Under these overlapping fungal growths lie a healthy layer of infernoil that clings to the stalactites, creating an ideal foundation for the underworld's flora. As bountiful and idyllic as this lit underworld may seem at first glance, long tendrils hang from the ceiling from large chitinous mollucite, patiently wait to snag any passing prey that nears their grasping viscid tongues.

<!-- image -->

Rising within the highest mountain range on Sol'Kesh, great vaults of open space reveal an underworld like no other. The softest sounds echo throughout the levels that stretch upwards. Near the peak, great breaches puncture the walls, some leading deeper into the earth while others let in the light of the day. Deep within the mountain, a kind of light illuminates the hollow core of the Splitspine mountains as massive lanternulls radiate a warming glow that lights the roaming swarms of mollock above. Slithering in the cracks beneath, krainis blindly search for the bodies of careless bats that have fallen from the heights above, their sensitive heat pits sensing the slowly cooling bodies of the small mammals. Within the spire of the largest mountain, feasting on nesting skyelarx that take shelter above the cloud line, the web-lined lairs of taurachni can be found. Though too large to hang from silken threads like smaller arachnids, their massive armored bodies are surprisingly agile as their eight chitinous limbs nimbly climb along the cavernous walls, the clatter of their hungering mandibles echoing in the vaulted within the hollow mountain.

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

## Evolving For A New World

Flora and Fauna of the Umbrawells

A soft and welcoming orange glow lights the tunnel, yet the small colony of migrating mollock, that cling to the underside of a stalactites, do not see the guiding light, and instead have traveled long following the sweet scent of the slime mold that grows on veins of azacrid. The eight adult bats with their three fur-clinging pups could feast on this mold for days before needing to move onwards, and with their goal so close, salivated eagerly. Suddenly a short, high pitched squeak resonates off the walls, the echoes reverberating back to the bats, painting the dark tunnel vividly with their echolocating senses. Turning back quickly, clicking nervously to better sense the commotion, they witness the last of their group desperately fight off a wet, gripping appendage, wrapped around its furred body. Their clicks revealing the sharp glass-like teeth of a false stalactite reeling their kin away from them. The ill-fated mollock, knowing there's now no escape, silences herself, not wanting to call more trouble for her surviving colony. Life in the Umbrawells is swift and cruel, and only the hope that her kin will survive without her, will give any solace in her last moments.

Nature will fill any niche, once the sulfur-infused caverns had begun to support a foundation of microbial life, flora and fauna soon followed. When certain species fled catastrophes above or explored new opportunities for food below, a new form of life cycle began to grow. Scum from the sulfide-based bacteria settled stone and surface water, great colonies of mustermold grew in kind, slowly breaking up the hard cold stone of the depths into a kind of deep-soil, akin to the first stirrings of life on the planet's surface billions of years earlier. As more fungal species found purchase, a secondary tropic level of small creatures found new homes, worms burrowed, arthropods scurried and before long megafauna invaded or evolved to grow large in these sunless lands.

In the tight labyrinthine confines, many creatures adapted to a crawling, lightless existence. Some relatively recognizable: The mollock bat lost its ability to fly, as well as its need to see, and the pallid-scaled krainis snake adapted in a similar way as the use of its thermal senses became more than sufficient. Found crawling beneath dark opaque waters or along stalactite strewn ceilings, opportunistic skith crustaceans listen intently for the faintest tremor to hunt down and sate their hunger. Though sustenance can be scarce in these undergrounds, there is always a stalking predator, and the acrynn fills that role expertly. With wide sonar sensitive antennae on both sides of its mandible-filled head, the large, nearly humanoid beetle stalks anything that makes the slightest of sounds.

Acrynn Grub Length - 1 ft. 2 in.

Developing its unique means of navigation early, the head of the acrynn grub nearly looks like its adult form. Blindly crawling along  the  tunnels  its  mother  left  it  in, quickly eating smaller insects to eventually molt into its lithe adult form

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

## Wickwalker Length - 4 in.

With a diet consisting of sulfur eating molds, the beetle's body is suffused with enough light producing bacteria to emit a glow of its own along long filaments on its abdomen. Lighting the darkness of their tunnels to signal to each other when food is found

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

## Seasons Without Storm

Seasonal Effects for the Umbrawells

Untouched from the wind and brutal sun above, for eons the land beneath the soil had lay sterile and unchanged, until life found a new way to flourish. As the acidophilic ecosystem grew, so did the rhythms of seasons begin to affect what was once frozen in time. Bacteria and fungus blooms wax and wane, subterranean creatures scuttle in their migrations, and seasons of feast and famine dictate the life beneath the earth, just as above.

Like a dark mirror, the Umbrawells react to the land above them: The Undermoor tunnels flood with the dark silt from the Beggar's Moor, many of its tunnels awash with the rich run off that feeds countless blind algivores. The vast vaults of the Splitspine mountains react to the altitude of the sky outside its stone walls, frost collects in the crevices, leading to runnels of fresh water that feeds the unseen black rivers below. No land is untouched by change, and the Umbrawells, now more than ever before, react to change.

## Seasonal Animal Behavior

Spring

## Hungering Youth

Tormull feast voraciously during the spring months to feed their young. Creatures have Disadvantage against the tormull's Involuntary Death Reaction.

## Rising Waters

Esslinter flourish with rising tide of the spring floods. Double any esslinter found on the Expedition Hunt and Scavenger Tables and they gain Advantage to Initiative rolls.

## Curious Generation

Newly born gorr pups explore their subterranean environment, and the parents are on high alert. Double any gorr found on the Expedition Hunt Tables and they gain Advantage to Wisdom (Perception) checks.

Summer

Fall

## Drying Depths

Icullus find it difficult to navigate the drier caverns as the water table lowers. Re-roll the first Scavenger or Hunting Expeditions rolls if it results in an Icullus

## Migrating Mollusks

Mollucite take advantage of the arid season to navigate the dry ceilings. Mollucite gain Advantage on Dexterity (Stealth) checks.

## Blooming Vaults

Fungus flourish in the warm summer months, bolstering the mollock population. Double any mollock found on the Expedition Hunt Tables and double the results found when Scavenging their Guano component.

## Preparing to Harvest

Readying for the incoming influx of creatures seeking warmth from the colder season, acrynn infest the tunnels nearer the surface to claim hunting grounds. Double any acrynn found on the Expedition Hunt Tables and they gain Advantage to Advantage to Wisdom (Perception) checks.

## Hungering for Flesh

As bacterial mats dry up in the dwindling underground streams, skith turn to more carnivorous diets. Skith attacks now score a Critical hit on a 19

or a 20.

## Spent Parenthood

By fall, many adult tormull collapse, leaving dry husks in their caverns after seasons of caring for their brood. Reroll the first of any Hunting Expeditions that result in a tormull.

## Winter

## Seeking Warmth

The colder caverns only further focuses the krainis heat vision to hunt. Krainis gain Advantage to Wisdom (Tracking) checks and Initiative rolls.

## Hostile Haven

Scavengers from all over shelter within the Umbrawells during the colder seasons. Double any creature result from the Scavenger Table.

## Welcomed Invaders

Skittering within its lair, the taurachni greets all new creatures to its web-lined lair hungrily. Taurachni gain Advantage in Dexterity Saving Throws. They can use the Stone Fall Lair Action during this season.

## Seasonal Weather Effects

11

Fog of the Forgotten · Each footfall near the white globules of  slime  mold  raises  clouds  of  spores,  fogging  the  mind. Alzabrath Natural-Traps will be encountered more frequently.

Vibrant Molds · Soft light suffuses the caverns from the many molds  that  cling  to  the  walls.  Many  areas  in  the Umbrawells  that  are  usually  obscured  in  Darkness  are instead in Dim Light.

Burning Water · The sulfuric ecosystem of the Umbrawells is particularly saturated. Any creatures not native to the Umbrawells take 1 Acid damage per minute while submerged in water.

Suffocating  Stagnation ·  Oxygen  is  thin  within  the cramped  confines. Constitution  Saving  Throw: DC  12,  for every hour for any creatures not native to the Umbrawells. Failure: Suffer one level of Exhaustion.

6 Drifting  Dust ·  Fungal  spores  cover  the  stone  floors, leaving  visible  track  marks.  Any  Tracking,  Wisdom (Survival) checks are made with Advantage.

7-8 Clean Air · Calm environment with no effects.

Gaseous  Air · Natural  gases  suffuse  the  air.  All  Fire damage  rolls  add  +3  damage. Constitution  Saving  Throw: DC 14, for every hour for any creatures not native to the Umbrawells. Failure: Suffer one level of Exhaustion.

Deafening Depths · Shriekshroom spread across the caverns, loudly reacting to any that near. Shriekshroom Natural-Traps will be encountered more frequently.

Dripping Deluge · Groundwater leaking from above drips from the ceiling. Dexterity (Acrobatics) Saving Throw: DC 12, any Traveler attempting a Dash action. Failure: They slip on the slick surface and have the Prone condition.

Veins of Blue · Azacrid Ore shines brightly in the darkened tunnels. Any Foraging Expeditions will yield double the result for Azacrid Ore.

## Summer

Blind Darkness · Billowing spores of dustmote mold float within  the  cramped  tunnels,  irritating  the  eyes  of  any that  step  through. Constitution  Saving  Throw: DC  8,  any creature not native to the Umbrawells. Failure: Creature has the Blinded condition for 1 minute.

Shifting Earth ·  The  height  of  the  summer  heat  causes tremors  under  the  earth  as  stone  expands  with  the shifting temperature. At the Keepers discretion, tunnels can cave in blocking passes, or stalactites can fall  from the tremors. Dexterity Saving Throw: DC  14,  any  creature below an unstable stalactite. Failure: Target creature takes 7 (2d6) Bludgeoning damage from the falling stone.

## Fall

Creeping  Intelligence ·  Slime  molds  spread  across  the floors, ingeniously growing towards the remains of beasts. ±1 to the Range Result on Hunting Expeditions.

Caustic Dangers ·  Burning dollops of acrid mold drip for inverted  fungal  pillars  above.  Pilth  Cluster  Natural-Traps will be encountered more frequently.

A Living Pulse ·  A  bloom  of  molds  overtakes  the  ceilings and walls and pulses in hypnotic flashes. Any creatures not native  to  the  Umbrawells  have  Disadvantage  on  ranged attack rolls.

Morning Dew ·  Globules  of  slime  resting  on  small  stalks shine with a healthy light, surrounding by industrious little dewmites. Any Foraging Expeditions will yield double the result for Dewmite Spire.

## Winter

Oppressive  Mold ·  A  black  fungal  mat  settles  onto  the cave  walls,  stifling  the  air  with  a  heavy  feeling  of  gloom. Constitution Saving Throw: DC 16, any creature not native to the Umbrawells. Failure: Creature has Disadvantage on all Charisma tests for 1 hour.

Lethal  Reproduction · Dangerous projectiles fly out, bounding off stone and bludgeoning unfortunate creatures from  the  blooming  mushrooms.  Clammuster  NaturalTraps will be encountered more frequently.

Spry  Steps · An orange cloud of mold  spores float through the air, invigorating those that breathe it in with an  unbounding  energy.  Any  creatures  not  native  to  the Umbrawells, add 10 ft. to their movement speed.

Guiding Light ·  With  an  influx  of  wintering  creatures  to spread  its  pollen,  the  glowing  stalk-like  plants  lights  the way  for  many  of  these  venturing  beasts.  Any  Foraging Expeditions will yield double the result for Lanternull.

<!-- image -->

## Unearthing What Grows In The Dark

Expeditions within the Umbrawells

To survive and flourish, life below the surface has adapted to a sulphuric bacterial foundation, creating a truly unique environment far from the light of the sun. Where lakes of acidic water have long ago leaked deeper into the earth, streaks of a blue hued azacrid can be found remaining. This oily glimmering stone retains a burn to the touch, even when melted down and forged into a cutting edge. Among the stalactites above, in the humid air that carries an abysmal stench, layers of hardened sulfur, mixed with salt and bacterial grime can be scraped off and mixed into an oil to coat leather and hide. The yellow speckled oil, infernoil, infuses a slight resistance to the acids that make up so much of what lives below. Beyond such unique materials, the tunnels and caves beneath Sol'Kesh harbor gardens of fungus and molds that glow and sparkle like the stars above, enticing Travelers to keep venturing deeper.

## Icullus

Class Diplopoda · Genus Derived Evolution Ammodesmus · Niche Generalist Omnivore Preyed upon by Krainis, Mollucite, Scavick · Preys upon Acrynn Grub, Gorr Hatchling, Mollock, Whiplith

Crawling along wet walls in the darkened caves of the Umbrawells, this large flat millipede, would look unassuming among the many other arthropods that infest the tunnels beneath the earth, had it not been for the foot-long pincers that adorn its head. With these two impressive lengths of chitin, it clasps onto climbing mollock, piercing their furred hide to inject a neurotoxin that quickly inhibits all muscle use, as the insect then drags its prey back into a lightless crevice to feast. Found in most underground systems, the icullus often breeds near the dark, acrid water that runs beneath the earth, their black eggs nestled in the warm bacterial mats that feast on the sulfur-infused environment.

Failure: Target  has  the  Paralyzed condition  and  may  repeat  the  save  at  the  end  of  each  of  its turns, ending the effect on itself on a success. After 3 minutes, it succeeds automatically.

Encountering Found near the surface of underground rivers and lakes, the icullus is a constant nuisance within the Umbrawells. Far too many Travelers have fallen senseless when stepping too close to an icullus hidden underfoot. Capturing the insects requires fresh bait, live or recently slain, as the icullus can appear in great numbers once they smell iron tinged blood in the air or water.

## Mollock

Class Mammalia · Genus Derived Evolution Hipposideros · Niche Specialist Fungivore Preyed upon by Acrynn, Icullus, Krainis, Mollucite, Scavick, Skith, Whiplith

Among  dripping  stalactites  hundreds  of  blind  mollock  crawl,  a  species  of  bat  that  through  eons  of  isolation  and  a  blossoming underground ecology, adapted to a safe and fungal diet far from the night sky they once flew through. Venturing ever deeper into the tunnels, they slowly adapted to a flightless lifestyle within the acrid sulfur-based ecosystems, navigating through a chorus of echo locating chirps and a highly sensitive nose. Diminutive in size, their survival relies on their ability to breed quickly and swarm in large numbers. When predators do snatch them from their colony, many of the older members harbor a cocktail of lethal toxins in their blood from years of feasting on a variety of poison saturated fungus, causing the hungry carnivores to sicken and die.

## Gorr

Class Reptilia · Genus Derived Evolution Uma · Niche -

Generalist Fossorial Omnivore

Preyed upon by Acrynn, Krainis, Mollucite, Scavick, Skith, Taurachni · Preys on Booralug, Undercraw, Whiplith

Rarely seen above ground, the gorr digs deep connected tunnels with its own kind, building vast communal burrows. Th ese near-blind lizards navigate the dark using two methods: Primarily sniffi  ng for fresh tubers and the musty smell of tasty booralugs, that are often found attached to the top of wet cave ceilings, eating the bacteria that permeates throughout the Umbrawells. Th e second, a much more unique method that has been adapted by the static that they build up in their scales from shuffling along iron-rich stone caverns. Sensitive sensors run along their muzzle tingle with the proximity of nearby predators. Periodically the static builds up can release a lethal discharge when the animal feels an excessive amount of stress.

## Mollucite

Class Gastropoda · Genus Derived Evolution Patella · Niche Generalist Ambush Carnivore Preyed upon by Taurachni · Preys on Acrynn, Krainis, Mollock, Scavick, Skith, Whiplith

Within the vaulted caverns of the Splitspine mountain, an errant stalactite slowly scuttles along the ceiling. When it senses the tremor of nearby prey, a hard stone-like shell opens wide, revealing a long prehensile tongue that rolls out wetly from its maw. Gently swaying back and forth, the tongue feels for the slightest movement in the stale air. When an unsuspecting creature approaches, it lashes out with unerring accuracy, slapping against the body, gripping and reeling it into the rows of gashing teeth that line its hungry maw. Th is mollusk, grown large from a carnivorous diet, infests many of the Umbrawells, from tight confi  nes to the largest caverns, slowly creeping near natural stalactites and stalagmites, and waits sometimes for weeks, before catching another careless creature.

On its  turn, Wisdom Saving Th row: DC 14,  the  mollucite. Failure: Th e mollucite gains +2 bonus to its Armor Class (total AC 18). ## Skith

Class Branchiopoda · Genus Derived Evolution Triops · Niche Generalist Amphibious Omnivore Preyed upon by Acrynn, Esslinter, Mollucite, Taurachni · Preys on Krainis, Mollock, Scavick

Scuttling in lightless tunnels and pitch black streams that crisscross the Umbrawells caverns, the skith are a true survivor in a bleak underground existence. Th is opportunist omnivore is often found scraping off the sulfur-based microbial mats that grow along the deep waterways. When sensing live prey, it quickly abandons the poor fare and crawls on the ceilings and walls in pursuit. When ready to close in, it uses its long powerful tail to launch itself into its prey. When pouncing his crustacean becomes a truly unseen horror, as its body violently slams into the creature, dozens of scything claws cutting through flesh, while it coils its chitinous tail around the body,squeezing the life out of it while it bleeds out in the oppressive darkness.

A Traveler willing to attract the crustaceans can easily pound the cavern walls to get their attention, but be warned, as there are more skith in the tunnels than  one  would  expect  could  be  contained  within  these  tight twisting confi  nes.

## Tormull

Class Insecta · Genus Derived Evolution Tuberolachnus · Niche Generalist Grazer Herbivore Preyed upon by Nicalliath, Sacrissian, Taurachni, Wrakis

Scrounging through woodland leaf litter, tormull ravenously pull rotten wood and mulched leaves into their grinding mandibles. Like its distant relative, the bloomux, the insect will fi  ll its abdomen to near bursting for the need of its spawn. When fi  lled, with its thorned, swollen abdomen dragging along the ground, it will lumber back into the subterranean pits to regurgitate the plant-matter. Not for their larvae, but for the multitude of luminescent fungal clusters that cover the lair walls, which the larvae swarm over hungrily, nearly obscuring the blue glowing fungus with their black shelled bodies. Once grown past their larval form, they enter a phase of abrupt cannibalism, leaving only a few of the ravenous aphids to ever reach their adult size and continue the cycle of fungal farming.

## Acrynn

Class - Insecta • Genus Derived Evolution - Duvalius • Niche - Specialist Carnivore

Preyed upon by - Esslinter, Mollucite, Taurachni • Preys on - Krainis, Mollock, Scavick, Skith, Tormull

A true terror of the Umbrawells, these fortunately rare insects prowl in the darkest of recesses, forgoing sight entirely with the use of their highly enhanced echolocation. Solitary hunters all, they make use of their grasping scythed arms to capture and break the large arthropods that make their home in these bleak, unlit tunnels. A threat to even their own kind as viable hunting grounds are sparse, female acrynn will take daring risks to venture into the territory of males, stabbing their hard shelled eggs into crevices along the walls of the winding tunnels, leaving their progeny for patrolling males to fertilize. Th e healthiest males will not only leave fertilize the egg, but leave the partial remains of their hunts for the soon to be feasting larvae to grow strong.

## Esslinter

Class - Actinopterygii • Genus Derived Evolution - Hoplunnis • Niche - Generalist Carnivore

Preyed upon by - Daellash, Goliarch, Nicalliath, Taurachni • Preys upon -

Acrynn, Daellash, Icullus, Scavick, Skith

Preceded by the unnerving sound of claws scratching across stone, the esslinter drags itself through the claustrophobic confi  nes within the deep roots of Sol'Kesh. Th ere are few underwater caves within the Umbrawells that do not host these stalking eels, terrorizing prey both above and below the black water. Slithering out of their wet confi  nes and dragging themselves along the ground with specialized pectoral fi  ns, they can catch their prey unawares. Clawing and constricting their long twisting bodies around the struggling creature, they pull them back into the water with their vicious claws. Along the coastal caverns, daellash and esslinter fi  ght for dominance, both attempting to fi  ll a similar niche, and before too long, perhaps one species may not survive the competition.

Encountering Preferring  the  black  depths  of  sunken  tunnels, seeking out the nests of the esslinter is not only diffi  cult, but often fatal in the tight confi  nes. A Traveler would fi  nd it easier to  lure  them  out  from  their  bodies  of  water  by  making  loud echoing noises near the water edge, as they prefer their prey alive before dragging them down into the depths.

## Krainis

Class - Reptilia • Genus Derived Evolution - Orthriophis • Niche - Generalist Carnivore Preyed upon by - Acrynn, Mollucite, Skith, Taurachni • Preys upon - Booralug, Mollock, Scavick, Whiplith

Sightless in the tight confi  nes that riddle the island, the krainis hunt within the pitch darkness for the faintest pulse of heat using highly sensitive heat pits along its head. Although an expert tracker, the blind serpent lacks accuracy for a single lethal strike, and coupled with the cramped tunnels and crevices often being too narrow for the striking motion of a regular snake bite, this subterranean dweller has evolved a unique method to administer its venom; with the use of tusk-like lateral fangs that slice instead of pierce. Th ese exposed fangs, dripping with venomous runoff, score the cave walls, marking their hunting grounds to limit the competition of other krainis, as the deepest areas of the Blackwells can often be devoid of easy prey.

## Traits

Infrared Senses Th e krainis cannot use its blindsight to perceive any creature with a body temperature less than half the temperature of its surroundings.

Sightless Senses Th   e krainis has Advantage on Wisdom (Perception) checks that rely on smell.

Sinuous Form Th e krainis has Advantage on Strength checks to escape a grapple.

## Actions

Multiattack Th e krainis makes two attacks, it can use its Lateral Fangs and Extendable Maw attacks in any combination.

Lateral Fangs Melee Attack Roll: +5 to hit, reach 5 ft., one creature. Hit: 12 (2d8 + 3) Piercing damage. Constitution Saving Th row: DC 14, creature that the krainis has successfully hit with its Lateral Fangs attack. Failure: Th e target has the Poisoned condition and suffers hallucinations, making it diffi  cult to perceive friend from foe. Th e creature has Disadvantage on all attack rolls and may repeat the save at the end of each of its turns, ending the effect on itself on a success. After 5 minutes, it succeeds automatically. Extendable  Maw Melee  Attack  Roll: +5  to  hit,  reach  5  ft.,  one target. Hit : 6 (1d4+3) Piercing damage. Strength Saving Th row: DC 10,  Small or smaller creature that the krainis has successfully hit  with  its  Extendable  Maw. Failure: Target  is  swallowed.  A swallowed creature has the Blinded and Restrained conditions, has  Total  Cover  against  attacks  and  other  effects  outside  the krainis, and takes 5 (2d4) Acid damage at the start of each of the krainis' turns. If the krainis takes 12 damage or more on a single turn from a creature inside it, the krainis must succeed on a DC 14 Constitution Saving Th row at the end of that turn or regurgitate all swallowed creatures, each of which falls in a space within 5 ft. and has the Prone condition. If the krainis dies, swallowed  creatures  no  longer  have  the  Restrained  condition and can escape from the corpse using 5 ft. of movement, exiting Prone. Th e krainis can swallow up to 2 Small creatures at a time.

Wary Instinct When Bloodied and in melee combat. On its turn, Wisdom Saving Th row: DC 16 , the krainis. Success: Th e krainis Disengages from combat immediately. Slithering into the nearest crevice or tunnel it can see, away from combat.

Encountering Living solely in the darkest pits of the subterranean  cave  systems  of  Sol'Kesh,  it  is  quite  diffi  cult  to catch this snake in its natural habitat. As it hunts through the use of smell, vibrations and heat signatures through cold stone. Creating a small fi  re or luring it with the scent of scavick fur may entice the creature to slither out from its lair.

## Taurachni What Waits in the Dark

Mirroring the unseen night sky above, pinpricks of bright indigo light scatter across the slime molds that wind downwards through cramped tunnels, lighting a path to depths unknown. The Traveler, gripping the weathered strap of their pack tightly at their shoulder once again questions whether this delve was worth the potential peril. With a conscious effort they try not to think of the untold tonnes of stone resting above their head, or the skittering sounds heard just outside the soft glow of the molds. Battling against the grip of fear, they step forward, one foot in front of the other.

In these depths, tracking the passage of time is a futility best not attempted, yet the Traveler notices changes as they descend. The usual labyrinth of passages and branching tunnels are met with tough fibrous white growths blockading entrances, further reinforcing only one path downward. Over time the white substance begins to wind entirely around the tunnels, timidly the Traveler touches it, relieved to find it firm to the touch and unlike much in this alkaline underground, not melting through their glove.

Jotting down notes in their journal, about this potential new building material, the quiet clicking of legs go unnoticed. Within the space of a heart beat the silence is shattered when an explosion of skittering legs pounds down the webbed walls, echoing with the promise of death. Before the dim lights could illuminate what was quickly approaching, the Traveler spins around and runs, not sparring a moment to regret missing the opportunity to document the creature, as absolute terror instead overwhelms them in their mad dash to escape back up to the light of day.

Through decades of unchecked growth in the hidden depths of the earth, the taurachni have grown to monumental sizes, securing miles of criss-crossing tunnels as their lair, funneling all manner of creatures into their web to feast. The taurachni prowl the caves as aggressive and unrivaled apex predators, their reinforced carapace and thick limbs allowing the creature to defend their lair against rivals arthropods that would seek to take them down. With such an excessively large size, many of the unique traits that arachnids have honed over millions have years have atrophied in this brute of a spider. Too heavy to hold itself to the ceilings, and too large to necessitate having a venomous bite to incapacitate larger prey, the arachnid instead acts like a rampaging beast, barreling into its prey, crushing and pummeling them into a broken waste and sucking up the pulped remains. In the rare instances when a nicalliath or dozens of desperate skith may attempt to slay the massive spider, the conflict can be felt shaking through the earth even those that stand on the surface above, may wonder if the earth is quaking, as the taurachni throws its considerable weight into the stone walls with jarring force. When the battle calms, and the beast sates its hunger, they enter long resting periods of torpor, awaiting the next telltale vibration of trapped creatures within their winding webbed tunnels.

## Umbrawells Apex Influence

Deep beneath the earth, life bloomed unlike ever before. Growing from a foundation of sulfur instead of sunlight, flora and fauna rose into new, almost alien manners. Those that grew strong and out competed the other creatures needed to be both versatile and ruthless, and few can match the arachnids in this regard having been already competing in an evolutionary arms race with arthropods for hundreds of millions of years. Elsewhere above ground, only the avians had ever kept arachnids from rising to the top of any food chain and, the Umbrawells, within their endless dark tunnels of burgeoning life became an opportunity like no other for the quickly adapting arachnids to rise, and eventually lead to the colossal juggernaut that is the taurachni.

While spiders inhabit countless crevices throughout each and every subterranean environment that worms through Sol'Kesh, only the deepest and widest spaces can harbor an infestation of taurachni. Larger than any other arachnid, these apex predators hunt in vast webs with only a rare few of them ever reaching adulthood. Younger and smaller taurachni venture out to shape their own nest, only to be slain by the highly competitive predators that prowl the darkness. First and foremost, the vaulted caverns that rise up the Splitspine mountains feature the most taurachni, these caverns becoming essentially rebuilt as the beasts cordon off tunnels with their thick webbing and break through others to allow their bulk to pass. Many of their kin split off and ventured east into the tunnels that lead to the Stoanhalls, nesting in cliff face caverns open to the sky, pouncing on nesting skyelarx that get too close. Further south, where even the crash of the ocean cannot be heard in the subterranean lakes that run deeply into the Drowned Hollows, through the thick application of their webbing they are able to shore up sunken tunnels and venture beneath the waterline. Here taurachni contend with esslinter and daellash, both eels having little chance to escape the armored behemoth once they worm too deeply into its nest. Taurachni are not merely a specter of death within the Umbrawells, and many creatures live within their webs, as the spider pays little attention to small pests not worth the effort of crushing. Their webbing not only provide structured layers in many vast open caverns, but also hosts bacteria, fungi and a multitude of insects that all live within its unique ecology.

Travelers set on delving into the domain of the taurachni must know that they are willingly stepping into a trap. While the webs prove to be hard as stone out on the periphery of their lair, the closer one gets funneled into the center, the more supple and sensitive the webs become, and vibrate with the step of incoming prey. With a slow metabolism, the taurachni spends much of its time feasting and digesting crushed and captured prey, but at the slightest vibration they quickly travel their lair to investigate. With a speed and silence one would not expect for a creature of its size, it rushes down pitch back paths, knowing each turn and step of its domain instinctively with only a rumbling tremor in the webbing to forewarn of its approach. If a Traveler is fortunate enough to heed these subtle warnings, they must be either willing to fight to the death, or find themselves any small crevice that the webbing failed to cover, and hope to hide from the fast approaching master of the lair.

<!-- image -->

The whiplith's slicing pincers and toxin-laden tail proves ineffective against the apex predator, and chance to flee seems unlikely

## Lair Actions

On initiative count 20 (losing initiative ties), the taurachni takes a lair action to cause one of the following effects, the taurachni can't use the same effect two rounds in a row.

Murmuring Th   reads Drumming  its  smaller  four  hind  limbs, the taurachni vibrates the thick threads that line the floor at a discomforting  low  frequency. Constitution  Saving  Th row: DC  16, each  creature  within  a  30  ft.  Emanation  originating  from  the taurachni. Failure: Creatures have Disadvantage on maintaining Concentration.

Silk-Stone Barricade (1/Day) Th e taurachni rapidly spins strands of silk into a thick stone-like barricade. Th is 6 ft. high 10 ft. wide wall  provides  cover  for  the  spider,  granting  it  Advantage  on Dexterity Saving Th rows if  it  remains  behind  it.  Th e  webbing can  be  attacked  and  destroyed.  Walls  have  an  AC  8:  HP  40: Vulnerability to Fire damage: Immunity to Piercing damage.

Echoing  Screech Th e  taurachni  rubs  its  pedipalps  furiously, creating a painful screech. Constitution Saving Th row: DC 14, each creature  in  a  60  ft.  Cone. Failure: 19  (3d12)  Th under  damage. Success: Half damage.

Pinning Tarsus Th e Taurachni targets a prone creature within 5  ft.  of  it. Strength  Saving  Th row: DC  16,  target  prone  creature. Failure: 11  (2d10)  Bludgeoning damage and the creature has the Restrained condition (escape DC 15). Success: Half damage.

Repelling Bulk Shaking fi  tfully the taurachni attempts to push away any assailants. Strength Saving Th row: DC 14, each creature within 5 ft. from the taurachni. Failure: Target creature is pushed back 10 ft. and has the Prone condition.

[Seasonal] Stone Fall Ripping specifi  c strands of webbing, the taurachni  purposefully  dislodges  sections  of  the  stone  ceiling onto  the  ground  below.  Roll  1d6  for  each  creature  in  the  lair, including  the  taurachni. Dexterity  Saving  Th row: DC  14,  each creature  that  rolled  a  1. Failure: 14  (4d6)  Bludgeoning  damage. Success: Half  damage.  Th e  shattered  stone  remains  on  the ground, creating Diffi  cult Terrain.

## Regional Effects

Deep within the Umbrawells, thick strands of silk plaster across floors and ceilings, sealing off passages and funneling prey ever closer to the awaiting arachnid.

Funneling Web Lines of webbing are found within 6 miles of the Lair, creating tunnels rife with dead-ends. Creatures have Disadvantage  on  Wisdom  (Survival)  checks  on  tracking  and navigating the tunnels, but have Advantage if they plan to travel to the center of the taurachni's lair.

Husked  Remains Crushed  carcasses  of  the  taurachni's  past victims line the walls of the tunnels within 5 miles of its Lair, attracting untold number of scavengers. Double the amount of creatures from the results on the Scavengers Table.

Silk-Stone Blocked Passages Th e taurachni purposefully blocks exiting tunnels when within 4 miles of its Lair. Large surfaces of thick silk cover from floor to ceiling, but they can be damaged. Walls  have  an  AC  8:  HP  40:  Vulnerability  to  Fire  damage: Immunity to Piercing damage.

Resonating Webs Within 3 miles of the Lair, the silk becomes more supple and emits subtle vibrations that can be sensed by creatures attuned to the vibrations of the earth. Creatures with Tremorsense have their range increased by 30 ft. while in the taurachni's domain.

Web  of  Life Despite  the  echoing  chambers  of  death,  life flourishes in the webbing within 2 miles of the Lair. Any Foraging Expeditions will yield double the discovered result.

Plucking  the  Strands Th e  taurachni  immediately  feels  the vibration  of  its  web  when  it  disturbed  by  touch  within  1  mile of its Lair. Creatures attempting to move stealthily within this radius must make a DC 18 Dexterity (Stealth) check to not rouse the  arachnid's  awareness.  Any  damage  applied  to  webbing within this radius immediately rouses the taurachni to act.

## Pilthcluster

Medium Natural Trap, Unaligned

AC 1

Senses Blindsight 30 ft. (Blind beyond this radius).

Immunities Acid, Blinded, Bludgeoning, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion, Frightened, Prone

HP 14 (4d6)

Speed 0 ft.

Trap Space Like  a  stalactite,  the  pilthcluster  hangs  from  the ceiling  and  occupies  a  5  ft.  Square  above,  dripping  when  it senses heat in the space below it.

Countermeasures Gray  like  the  stone  it  grows  from,  the pilthcluster's form can be identified with a trained eye. Wisdom (Perception) Check: DC 12. Success: The Natural Trap is identified.

## Reactions

Acrid  Drip Trigger: When  a  creature  steps  within  its  Trap Space. Response -Dexterity  Saving  Throw: DC  14,  the  creature that triggered the Trap. Failure: 7  (3d4)  Acid damage, and the viscous bacterial acid continues to burn. At the start of each of its turns, the creature takes 2 (1d4) Acid damage until it can be scraped off with an Action, or washed with a gallon of water.

## Biology

Many unfortunate creatures that felt its burn would disagree, but  the  pilthcluster  bacterial  growth  is  a  key  component of  the  subterranean  ecology.  Filtering  out  the  highly  acidic environment into rich bacterial matter that falls below it, while at  first  it  is  highly  acrid,  with  time  it  settles  into  a  soil-like substance that plants and fungus can use to take root.

<!-- image -->

Tiny Natural Trap, Unaligned

Senses Blindsight 5 ft. (Blind beyond this radius).

Immunities Blinded, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion, Frightened, Prone

AC 1

HP 2 (1d4)

Speed 0 ft.

Trap Space Unassuming in form, at only the size of a closed fist,  the  shriekshroom  grows  along  damp  walls  looking  like much of the other fungal growths. Only when within 5 ft. of it, and it feels the presence of a creature will it react.

Countering Often clustered with other mushrooms, the dark clam-like  shape  can  easily  be  hidden  amongst  more  vibrant growths. Wisdom (Perception) Check: DC 14. Success: The Natural Trap is identified.

## Reactions

Excruciating Echoes Trigger: When a creature steps within its Trap  Space. Response -Constitution  Saving  Throw: DC  14,  each creature in a 20 ft. Sphere originating from the shriekshroom. Failure: The creature has the Deafened condition and the sound echoes down the halls for 200 ft..

## Biology

With  so  many  creatures  evolved  to  feast  on  the  plentiful mushrooms  that  spread  along  the  darkened  halls  of  the Umbrawells,  the  shriekshroom  has  adapted  by  reacting  to nearby stimulus aggressively. Fibers pull back and crack open the hard clamped shell, releasing trapped carbon dioxide out of the gills in a high pitched whistle.

Tough fibers make up this mushroom's hyphae, leaving it very difficult to pry off the cavern wall, or to force upon to release the shrieking whistle

<!-- image -->

## Alzabrath

Large Natural Trap, Unaligned

AC 1 HP

2 (1d4)

Speed 0 ft.

Immunities Blinded, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion, Frightened, Piercing, Prone

Senses Tremorsense 10 ft. (Blind beyond this radius).

Trap Space Spreading across the ground in a carpet of white globules, this slime mold covers a 10 ft. Circle space.

Countermeasures Nonthreatening  in  appearance  and  easily stepped upon, alzabrath can be missed without notice. Wisdom (Perception) Check: DC 11. Success: The Natural Trap is identified.

## Reactions

Dulling  Fog Trigger: When  a  creature  steps  within  its  Trap Space. Response -Constitution Saving Throw: DC 16, the creature that triggered the Trap. Failure: For one hour the creature has Disadvantage  on  Intelligence  tests  and  must  succeed  on  a DC 13 Intelligence  check  when  attempting  to  recall  any  new information that took place within a full day.

## Botany

When faced with drought, the alzabrath slime mold halts all growth and congeals into clusters of globules waiting to burst. As  creatures  traverse  over  the  carpet,  popping  spore  clouds collect on their body while they continue through the cramped corridors,  unaware  that  the  fog  of  spores  had  clouded  their mind with forgetfulness, wandering about lost and spreading the spores.

AC 1 HP 3 (1d6)

Senses Blindsight 20 ft. (Blind beyond this radius).

Immunities Blinded, Bludgeoning, Charmed, Deafened, Exhaustion, Frightened, Piercing, Prone

Countering When in bloom, the spore cluster can be seen open and ready to launch. Wisdom (Perception) Check: DC 10. Success: The Natural Trap is identified.

## Botany

In  attempt  to  out  compete  the  variaty  of  other  fungus  that flourish  in  the  Umbrawells,  the  clammuster  makes  use  of  a condensed spore dart to fire its progeny far into the caverns.

A condensed spore cluster tip the end of the dart-like  stem.  When  it impacts  a  hard  surface, it creates a cloud of widely dispersed  spores

## Nomadic Ascension

## The Ecology of the Wayward Beasts

Sunlight, like a breath of fresh air, revitalizes the Traveler as they step out from the Umbrawells crevice and into the cold of the Splitspine mountain cliff top. With eyes closed, the Traveler savors the feeling of the wind pulling at their hair and tattered clothing, torn and singed from the acidic environment below. Breathing out a sigh of relief at surviving such a trial, they open their eyes and are rewarded with a view that suddenly feels worth the struggle.

Under sparsely clouded skies, the Traveler can see farther than they ever have since stepping onto the island. Miles of Wealdwoods spread out to the east, waves of green leaves rolling over unseen hills. Past the last tree, a vibrant riot of color encroaches on the coast, distant waves break on the Strands and even from the great distance, the Traveler can see the white specks of birds swirl about the teeming hordes of crustacean.

Sitting on the stone ground, the Traveler pulls hardtack and their journal from their pack, and begins to write and sketch of their journey down below: exotic mushrooms that lit the caverns, the skittering monstrosities that called it home and speculation notes on what was in the center of the webbed lair. As the sun sinks lower in the sky, a large body suddenly thumps onto the ground behind them. With journal clutched tightly to their chest,  they quickly spin about to find golden eyes peering at them from a large canine-like head. Staying perfectly still, the Traveler and their sudden visitor stare cautiously at each other for a few moments, before the winged beast slowly approaches on clawed wings. With its large, furred head near the Traveler's chest, the winged beast sniffs the biped. Then, with an uneasy smirk, the Traveler calmly offers the hardtack.

With a quick snap of its long snout, the mammal snatches the biscuit from their hand, and crunches happily. Taking a risk, one the Traveler would have never expected this island to allow, reaches out  and runs their fingers through the warm ruddy fur of the skyelarx's neck.

## "Maybe I don't need to do this all alone..."

For a species to survive and thrive, it means carving a niche of its own, a clear space in the web of life. While true for the vast majority of animals that have swam, flew and crawled across Sol'Kesh, they are not as likely to survive an ecological upheaval as well as a creature that can adapt to any environment it finds itself in. This rare trait of evolution, often only bestowed upon the most basal of insects or most indiscriminate of scavengers, allows highly versatile creatures to flourish everywhere.

Many wayward creatures roam across vast landscapes. Nicalliath, sacrissian and skyelarx never spend more than a night in a single area, forever on the move for fresher hunting grounds. For while they are ferocious predators in their own right, they are never fully suited to the environment's ecology, and there is always a greater predator, often an apex that would oust them from their territory.

Others simply adapt so successfully to any niche, that they are found nearly everywhere: swarms of lanceleth squeeze under every rock and branch, lethally leaping into whatever unfortunate beast steps near, packs of social vinnirex hunt across fields and beaches with ease, wrakis crawl into any crevice they can find, and scavick plague and pester every biome. These festering rodents making up the most commonly found scavenger, as well as prey.

Lastly, the omaterssum, wandering far across the island not out of significant adaptability, but out of desperation. With a dwindling rate of reproduction, the large marsupial escapes predation wherever it can, whether that be an uninhabited spire of rock off the coast, or a dark crevice in the side of a cliff. Trying to secure a last niche of their own anywhere, before it's too late.

<!-- image -->

Skuleekul

Length - 1 ft. 5 in.

Dragging  its  heavily  armored,  segmented  shell across  sand  and  mud,  the  crustacean's  strong olfactory  receptor  cavity  leads  it  to  damp  fertile environments. With a durable digestion to consume nearly anything, it sniffs out a tides mane coral in bloom, hungrily approaching the supple fibers

Porter Crab Height - 7 in.

One of the very few corals that ever move inland,  this  species  has  grown  to  hitch  a ride on the porter hermit crab, lending its stinging nematocysts to the crab's defensive as it is carried far and wide. Ranging from the coasts, to the muck of the swamps and the  black  subterranean  rivers,  the  coral sifts  through  the  water  of  wherever  the traveling crab takes it to next

Arachatta Height - 1 ft. 3 in.

The  spider  may  not  be  the  largest,  nor  the  most  deadly  of creatures that roam throughout the island. But when those eight articulated  legs  speed  across  the  Farplains,  leap  from  coral outcroppings on the Strands or scurry around tree trunks in the Wealdwoods, many creatures fear for their very lives. With a sharp blade-like edge running along the top of its abdomen, it has been known to rush under larger megafauna, slitting their stomachs from below, in a ghastly display of brutality

<!-- image -->

## Vinnirex

Class Mammalia · Genus Derived Evolution Canis · Niche -

Generalist Scavenger Omnivore

Preyed upon by Daellash, Grahknash, Scavick, Skyelarx, Slannethiss, Wrakis · Preys on Daugon, Frondilli, Scavick

Nimbly stepping from stone to stone along the surf, the receding sea reveals tidal pool locked fi  sh for the vinnirex to quickly snatch what prey they can in the crowded coasts. Rising from subterranean rivers, sharp barks echo off darkened chambers as the canines drag a skith out into the sunlight. Packs of vinnirex run along side bloomux, harrying the rotund insects for their delicious honey. Th ese far ranging canines have found their way all across the island, perfectly adapted to a wide range of environments, using their webbed paws to tread the ocean, and their thick coarse fur to survive the colder altitudes. Hunting in tight familial packs, communicating and executing strategic maneuvers, these canines can effectively surround and take down larger prey than one would expect.

If  found  alone  however,  their  instinctual  need  to  be  part  of  a group  softens  their  resolve,  and  through  patient  interactions and offerings of food, a single vinnirex could be coerced into a sort of pack kinship with a Traveler.

## Lanceleth

Class Gastropoda · Genus Derived Evolution Aplysia · Niche Specialist Amphibious Ambush Carnivore Preyed upon by Acrynn, Skith · Preys on Cariver, Cerraphol, Illithisk, Ruske,

Sensitive antennae gently wave, feeling the eddies and flow of air for disturbances. When the diminutive lanceleth begin to sense the rhythm of larger creatures, they spring into action, leaping forward in an explosion of darting slugs. With unerring accuracy they spear through flesh and chitin alike, and dig into feast. Alone they can be a painful pest, but when they swarm during the warmer seasons, they can often take down prey much larger than their size would suggest, as each successful hit spurs on more to strike in kind. As their prey stumbles from blood loss and the pain of squirming bodies of slugs in their open wounds, nearby scavengers are drawn to the scent and cries, closing in the vulnerable beast to fi  nish it off.

## Scavick

Class Mammalia · Genus Derived Evolution Rattus · Niche Generalist Scavenger Omnivore Preyed upon by Basilospri, Grahknash, Taurachni, Vinnirex · Preys on Calitross, Lhoupin, Mollock, Solmundis

Th e sweet musk of rot wafting through the Wealdwoods, the cries of pain echoing in the Umbrawells, nothing gathers the beady-eyed scavick as much as an easy meal. Like to maggots in a corpse, these rodents have managed to burrow themselves in every environment of  Sol'Kesh. Highly adaptive to their surroundings and capable of surviving on everything from spoiled meat to lichen, they have multiplied and spread profusely. Skittish when alone, they often run in mischiefs lead by the largest female. While preferring to slink under the cover of darkness to scavenge the bloody remains of another predator's hunt, when confronted or desperate, the mischief will try to surround and overwhelm the aggressor, relying on their quick reflexes and rancid quills to take down more ferocious beasts.

As a creature with  an  instinctive  ability  to  know  when  to  flee  and  when  to attack, interacting with them means playing to their instincts. If a Traveler wishes to capture one, feigning a limp may draw them out, and hopefully not too many of them.

## Skyelarx

Class Mammalia · Genus Derived Evolution Vampyrum · Niche Generalist Aerial Omnivore Preyed upon by Arkiopti, Basilospri, Sacrissian · Preys on Calitross, Lexati, Frondilli, Scavick, Vinnirex

High above, circling the skies with long outstretched webbed wings catching thermals, the enormous bat's golden eyes glint as it tracks smaller terrestrial predators stalking their own prey far below. Watching hungrily as the vinnirex pack charges in tandem, executing their kill masterfully. Th e canines joyfully tear into their meal, but for a moment until a shadow soon casts over them. With wings pulled back, the skyelarx plummets, howling wildly. Within a heartbeat before hitting the earth, its wings snap out and lethally sharp talons plunge into the freshly killed carcass, claiming it as its own. Staring at the pack of vinnirex with its sinister golden eyes, it leaps upward, beating its wings and rises back into the air before the stunned canines can take back their prize.

## Wrakis

Class - Insecta • Genus Derived Evolution - Curicta • Niche - Generalist Carnivore

Preyed upon by - Acrynn, Basilospri, Sacrissian, Skyelarx • Preys on - Gharril, Murrow, Scavick, Solmundis, Vinnirex

Quietly slipping under the lily pad covered surface, water slides of its slick, dark red carapace as the wrakis stealthy approaches its prey. A long fluted extension of its abdomen continues taking in oxygen as it waits unmoving for a moment to strike. When a large murrow swims by unaware, scythe-like forelimbs slash out to grip the large fi  sh as it plunges its proboscis deep into the scaled body, unleashing a torrent of digestive fluids to quickly dissolve the unfortunate fi  sh's interior into a fleshy pulp. At six feet long, the large insect is far from the apex predator within any given region, but with its ability to hunt anywhere from the dark confi  nes of the Umbrawells to beneath the roots of the Wealdwoods, to the coastal Strands, this arthropod can be found waiting to strike from any dark crevice.

Needling Proboscis Constitution Saving Th row: DC 14, one creature the wrakis is grappling. Failure: 10 (2d6 + 3) Acid damage. Failure by 5 or more: Th e target takes an additional 3 (1d6) Acid damage at the start of its turn for 1 minute.

## Reactions

Jittery Evasion (3/Day) Trigger: When an enemy the wrakis can see makes a ranged attack roll against it and before damage is dealt. Response: Th e  wrakis can jump up to half its movement, potentially avoiding the attack by moving out of the line of sight or the attack's range.

Encountering Infesting nearly every environment  on the island, a Traveler is most likely going to chance upon the red insect in damp, dark environments. Th ere is little that garner this  creature's  attention  more  than  a  heap  of  scavenged  offal to  guard.  While  these  remains  would  be  a  tasty  meal  for  the arthropod, lying in wait to slay any other scavengers that would approach the potential meal, makes for an even fi  ner catch.

## Sacrissian

Class - Reptilia • Genus Derived Evolution - Rhynchoedura • Niche - Generalist Pursuit Carnivore Preyed upon by - Grahknash, Jhotus, Korrask, Nicalliath • Preys on - Baughur, Calitross, Frondilli, Rauka, Tartakka

With a clack of its razor-sharp beak, the sacrissian weaves around tree trunks in pursuit of its favored prey, frondilli, chasing the spry deer into an open grove. Th e nimble mammal is no match for the lithe reptile as it closes the distance and slams its horned crest into the fleeing prey, launching it to crash into a tree, broken. Th ere are few places in Sol'Kesh free from the terror of a sacrissian, stalking across the Farplains with impunity, gracefully swimming through both fresh and salt water, and in the colder seasons, wintering in the caverns of the Umbrawells. With a widely dispersed hunting range, its rare to see two within miles of each other as their beaked cries warn others of their kind to keep their distance, lest they are ready to challenge for territory, or to mate.

Th ese  reptiles  see  most creatures, including their own, as either prey or challengers and respond aggressively to any that get to near.

## Nicalliath

Class Malacostraca · Genus Derived Evolution Patella · Niche Generalist Ambush Carnivore

Preyed upon by Jhotus, Korrask, Nuthorghull, Maedrynn, Slannethiss, Taurachni · Preys on Scavick, Vinnirex

Dozens of loud taps hit wet stone in quick succession as a sinuously segmented crustacean rushes across the rocky coast under the gray light of rain laden clouds. Long antennae whip through the salty air, catching the musty scent of a gharril clutch on the cliffs above. Now sensing easy prey, the crustacean speeds up the near vertical outcropping, closing in quickly as its multitude of pronged claws fi  nd easy purchase on the stone. Without an uttered sound it crests the cliff face and strikes in an explosion of violence. Eviscerated salty gore and broken shell stream down the stone as the nicalliath feasts. Th ese far ranging predators dominate most environments on the island, to such a point that they must constantly stay on the move, lest their hunting grounds grow devoid of prey.

## Omaterssum

Class - Mammalia • Genus Derived Evolution - Didelphis • Niche - Generalist Scavenger Omnivore Preyed upon by - Korrask, Slannethiss, Tyrandroth • Preys upon - Cerrophol, Murrow, Scavick, Tartakka, Vinnirex

A series of small chirps and barks are heard as a sleek creature warily steps out from the quiet woods upon a snow covered glade. Clinging to its gray fur are an assortment of young, unrelated to the giant marsupial, yet she protectively guards them all the same. While widely spread across the island, they are a dwindling species, recent generations have been suffering from a low birth rate. To quell their matronly instincts, some female omaterssum will be seen adopting the young of different wayward creatures, those who lost their herd or fallen from their nest. While the females are seen to be fi  ercely protective, the males on the other hand will go out of their way to slaughter any young not of their kin, further dividing the two sexes and fracturing the chances for the omaterssum to survive.
