## Expeditions into the Wilds

Encounters and Gathering Components

There are four methods to collect resources in the wilds of Sol'Kesh and a Traveler does not need to master all to survive. Although, knowing when each method is needed is key to making the most of the island's bounty.

If a Traveler wants to test their luck and venture out into the wilds to hunt for a specific beast or component, they may go out on an Expedition. Expedition and Scavenging Tables can be found in each biome, providing a range of species to encounter, be it a beneficial plant or a voracious predator. Although apex predators dominate these biomes, their discovery and potential encounter has more preparation involved, and are not found in Expeditions.

## Knowledge and Skill

Those  attuned  to  the  ways  of  the  wilds,  studied  dusty  tomes detailing flora and fauna, or simply asked the right people, may have an advantage on finding what they are looking for. Many of these advantages are at the discretion of the Keeper, to see if they fit within the campaign setting and are possible for the Traveler to perform.

By having any of the given skills or knowledge below, a Traveler may  add  or  subtract  from  their  result  after  rolling  on  the Expedition tables and select a different result within the range. A roll of a natural 1 is always considered a Critical Failure, while a 20 is a Critical Success, and both cannot be adjusted in any way.

Example: A Traveler has rolled a 12 on the Strands Foraging Expedition table. They have a 2+ in the Nature Skill as well as a +1 from asking a local fisherman, giving a total of range ±3. This allows the Traveler to adjust their result of their dice roll between 8 and 15 to pick the flora of their choice.

| Expedition Modifiers BonusCriteria             | Modification to RangeResult     |
|------------------------------------------------|---------------------------------|
| Skill - Intelligence (Nature)                  | ± Skill Modifier                |
| Skill-Wisdom (Survival)                        | ± Skill Modifier                |
| Advice or rumor from an NPC(optional)          | ± 1                             |
| Completed Field Journal Studies                | ± 1                             |
| Personally has slain the biome's apex predator | ± 2                             |
| Carrying the appropriate gear                  | ± Modifier found listed on gear |

## Time Spent on an Expedition

Going on an Expedition, be it foraging for fungi, hunting for a predator or the quiet patience of fishing takes time.

The suggested time below is for the Keeper's discretion and may help establish the distance a Traveler may need to journey and the time spent in going on an Expedition.

| TimeSpentonExpeditions        | TimeSpentonExpeditions   |
|-------------------------------|--------------------------|
| Expedition Type               | Day[s] Spent             |
| Foraging                      | 1/2 Day                  |
| Setting and Waiting for Traps | 1 Day                    |
| Fishing                       | 1/4 Day                  |
| Hunting                       | 1 Day                    |

## Seeking Better Results

If  a  Traveler is looking for a different result after their first roll on any given Expedition Table they can choose to push their luck and attempt to roll again, unless they had rolled a Critical failure in their first attempt.

This  second  attempt  adds  again  to  the Time  Spent ,  and  is considered adding risk to the Expedition and often attracts all matter of creatures within the wilds.

Once they perform a second roll, they must accept those results, and  unless  the  second  attempt  rolled  a  Critical  success,  the Traveler must then immediately roll on the Scavenger Encounter Table . It is then at the Keeper's discretion on whether the Traveler encounters the Scavenging creature right away, or within a play session in which the Traveler goes on their Expedition.

<!-- image -->

The  most  important  and  often  overlooked  method  to  survive, is  foraging.  Life  surrounds a Traveler, from up above the great eastern Wealdwoods  canopies to glowing caverns beneath the  parched  soil  in  the  western  arid  Farplains.  Throughout  an adventure,  a  Traveler  can  collect  Components,  whether  this  is during the quieter times when setting up camp, or keeping an eye on choice materials while on the move mid journey. While some rarer Components may warrant a quest on their own, many can simply be found while in the midst of adventures, it only takes a mindful Traveler to keep an eye out for them.

Foraging for flora and materials requires a Traveler to roll on the biome's Foraging Expedition Table ,  adding  any  modifiers  that may improve the selection range. Once a selection is made, the Traveler rolls a Wisdom (Survival) check, the DC being determined by  both  the  rarity  and  the  danger  involved  in  foraging  for  the Component. Upon success, they gain the Foraged Component.

<!-- image -->

## Setting Snares

Patience and planning are seldom spoken of in tales of heroism, but  it  is  those  qualities  that  can  often  garner  the  best  results. Though often not done during hunting pursuits or quests that require haste, a Traveler that takes the time to set a snare to circle back to, or place outside their camp before retiring for the night can often find an easy catch awaiting them.

A Traveler must first announce their plan to place a Snare, once placed, a snare takes time to capture its prey. This period of time is generally half a day, but is ultimately determined by the Keeper's discretion. For example this can take place over a Long Rest, or the Travelers can place their trap and return to it at another time.

In  order  to  see  the  results  of  a  snare,  a  Traveler  must  first  roll on  the  biome's Snare  Expedition  Table ,  adding  any  modifiers that  may  improve  the  selection  range.  Once  a  snared  prey  is determined,  the  Traveler  rolls  a  Wisdom  (Survival)  check,  the DC  being  determined  by  creatures  fortitude  in  being  slain  by the snare. Failing to make this check often means the creature escaped,  but  in  some  cases,  creatures  listed  with Vengeance , will result in an Encounter with the creature immediately after the failure. While a Critical failure results in the snare breaking irreparably.

Returning to a snare after more than 2 days since setting it will also result in a broken snare, as the animal either escaped, or was scavenged by nearby beasts.

Skryekull

Length - 5 ft. 9 in.

Dozens of tail pleopods churn water as the long crustacean navigates the winding  sunken  tunnels  of  the  Umbrawells  in  search  of  phosstalks.  The gently glowing translucent fruit signaling all aquatic frugivores that it is ripe to tear into and spread its seeds throughout the dark waterways

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

## Fishing

What can be both a peaceful past time on a warm afternoon or a desperate hope for a meal to cook over a camp fire, fishing is a tried and true method to capture what lives in the river and sea without testing one's ability to swim.

Sea life is abundant  throughout  Sol'Kesh  as  its an island surrounded by a near endless ocean, riddled with rivers, ponds, lakes and wetlands. Fishing spots can be found in each biome to a degree.

The  creatures  that  swim  and  crawl  beneath  the  water's  surface have a rich diverse ecosystem, as predator and prey vie for survival just as they would on the surface. With even some more cunning beasts seeing a Traveler's lure not as food, but a sign that it may lead to larger prey waiting just above the water. In both fresh and salt water, many of these predators are large enough to capsize a  boat  and  swallow  the  occupants  whole,  and  so  many  failed attempts  at  Fishing  may  result  in  an  encounter  with  the  beast itself for any results that include Vengeance listed.

## Fishing Guidelines

Fishing takes more than patience, as certain gear is needed to be crafted. If one is able to find the right materials, fishing rods could be built to better one's chances (pg. 260). In addition, if a Traveler is looking for a specific creature, traveling to the area they reside in, a Fishing Spot, will help the ability to capture it immensely.

- Fishing initially requires a Traveler to roll on the biome's Fishing Expedition  Table ,  adding  any  modifiers  that  may  improve  the selection range to select a creature.

## Fishing Expedition Modifiers

Fishing Feat · ±1 to the range result Weather Bonus · ±1 to the range result Crafted Fishing Rod · ±1 to the range result

Crafted Biome Specific Fishing Lure • ±2 to the range result

-  With  a  smooth  motion  and  an  eye  for  distance,  the  Traveler can cast  their  fishing  line  and  roll  1d20  on  the  biome's Fishing Expedition Table.
- Success: After a brief moment the creature that results in the 1d20 roll catches the bait and the Traveler must make a Strength (Athletics)  check  against  the  creature's  fishing  difficulty  (DC) found in the specific biome's Fishing Expedition Table to Reel the creature out of the water. Passing the Strength check results in the Traveler obtaining the creature. A caught creature is exhausted and  will  not  be  responsive  outside  the  water,  the  Traveler  may elect to instantly slay the creature at this point. Creature's slain this way do not give any XP.
- Failure: Failing to reel the creature in will result in one of three consequences. A Critical failure will result in the breaking of the fishing  rod  and  a  missed  opportunity  at  capturing  a  creature. A  failure  to  reel  will  result  in  the  Traveler  finding  nothing  and needing to try again. And lastly, in some cases the creature will fight back, a failed result with a creature listed with Vengeance will lead to an Encounter with the creature immediately. Vengeance Encounters do result in XP for a successful combat.

<!-- image -->

## Th   e Primal Hunt

Breath rasping between clenched teeth, blood rushing through veins, knuckles white with weapon gripped tight, the thrill of the hunt can be a savagely thrilling thing. It can also be a silent death, quietly stalking the tracks of beasts, pulse slowed while patiently waiting  for  the  single  heart-piercing  strike.  On  the  island  of Sol'Kesh where the rule of predator and prey is law, hunting is often the only means to survive. Unlike the other Expeditions, hunting always ends in battle, but the value of their scavenged Components is well worth it.

Many  times  a  Traveler  will  fi  nd  themselves  requested  to  hunt and kill a specifi  c creature, and tracking down a beast is more than  simply  stepping  into  their  habitat.  Gathering  knowledge from  surveying  the  land,  conversing  with  those  familiar  with the animal or researching how best to make use of the intricate ecosystem of predators and prey, are all necessary in fi  nding the quarry they seek.

To go out into the wilds and hunt, a Traveler must fi  rst roll on the  biome's Hunting  Expedition  Table , adding  any  modifi  ers that may improve their selection range. Once a prey is selected, either  an  Encounter  begins  immediately  as  the  Traveler  has successfully  cornered  their  prey,  a  quest  begins  that  may  end with the discovery of the selected prey, or simply the prey has been slain and the Traveler can reap the benefi  ts, it is entirely up to the Keeper's discretion. A Critical failure often results in the Traveler and their party failing to fi  nd any hint or tracks of a suitable beast. Although a wicked Keeper may have them instead get hunted in kind, perhaps by selecting a larger and more vicious creature that stalks the biome.

## Hunting Expedition Modifi  ers

Have encountered the creature in the past · ±1 to the range result Have gathered written or NPC given knowledge · ±1 to the range result Have slain the biome's Apex predator in the past  · ±1 to the range result Fully completed a Journal entry for the creature · ±2 to the range result

## Apex Hunting

Tracking and slaying a region's apex predator is no ordinary hunt, and one that cannot be taken lightly. Th e apex is not a creature one comes across unexpected, as its presence affects large swaths of th region it uses as its hunting grounds and lair, and often has a massive influence on the natural ecology of its entire biome.

Th ough often few in number, an apex predator is not a mythical beast. And their voracious appetite and size force them to distance themselves from each others as to not over hunt the area. It is only in the rare occurrences of territorial disputes or at the height of mating season that a Traveler could come across more than a singular apex predator at once, and it is highly recommended to put as much distance as possible between oneself and those rare battles or couplings, as both events can be quite cataclysmic.

Blind Helm

Length - 9 ft.

Floating about in the Umbrawells' pitch black underwater rivers  and  lakes,  the  jellyfish  survives  with  a  durable exumbrella,  covering  its  organs  as  it  bumps  into  stone blindly.  Long  stinging  tentacles  trail  behind  it,  warding off any predators as it filter feeds on acidophile plankton

<!-- image -->

## Voracious Scavengers Abound

Th e Travelers of Sol'Kesh are not the only ones stalking the wilds in search of meat and for every stalking hunter there are dozens of scavengers following in their wake. With the soaring skyelarx above, its golden eyes tracking a pursuing predator to steal from. Swarming scavick  infest  every  biome  that  they  can  claw  into. Even the deadly sacrissian, renowned for its savage predation is intelligent enough to know that when the scent of death is on the air, it can lead to an effortless meal.

- Th e Scavengers  Table ,  is  not  only  to  be  used  as  a  means  of punishment for daring Travelers, but may also be a tool to help the Keeper create a living breathing world and can be used in each biome as a means to add a variety of beasts to every encounter. For in Sol'Kesh's evolutionary arms race, no meat goes wasted, and no step in the wilds goes unheard.

<!-- image -->

## A Primal Feast

Preparing Meals

When a Traveler ventures out to the untamed wilds, whether that is trudging along in the Strands' tide, or crawling through the Umbrawells' acid-stained tunnels that carve through the island like grubs through tree bark, there are certain survival needs that must always be met: warmth, shelter, clean water and food. For those living in the comfort of civilization, it is easy to forget the fortune of such needs being so easily satisfi  ed. To those alone on a dark windswept fi  eld with nothing but the stars above and the distant howls of predators as company, there is very little that would comfort them more than a simple tent and warm meal.

When a Traveler prepares a meal, they must choose which ingredients (pg. 276) they would like to use from the Scavenged Components that they have on hand. Th ey must have access to generally clean water, an open flame, a Cooking Kit (pg. 260) or adequate cooking utensils.

- Preparing a meal takes either 1 hour of uninterrupted time, or it could be performed as part of a Short or Long Rest.
- When a Traveler has all they need to begin a Meal Preparation roll, they must succeed on a Wisdom or Intelligence check: DC 10 + 3 for each ingredient added beyond the fi  rst. If a nearby allied creature that is capable of cooking helps (an intelligent humanoid for an example), the Traveler can add +1 to their roll. If a Traveler has prepared this meal before, they can also add +1 to the result.
- When a Traveler prepares a meal, they can make enough to feed a number of Medium creatures equal to 1 + their Profi   ciency Bonus . A  prepared  meal  then  permanently  uses  up  all  the  Scavenged components needed for the recipe.
- Prepared food must be eaten within 1 day of being made to gain the benefi  ts, unless its preserved through the use of salt or cold temperatures.  It  is  at  the  Keeper's  discretion  on  what  kinds  of food can be preserved in this manner. A meal that is left for 3 days without proper preservation becomes a Poisoned Meal .

Example: With a thick daellash Steak Slice and a spicy Ilyrana Pepper, a level 8 Rogue Traveler uses these two ingredients to prepare a meal and must succeed on a DC 13. With a Cooking Kit on hand, they roll a Meal Preparation  check  of  11  +  3  for  their  Profi  ciency  Bonus,  +  1  for  having prepared the same meal before, and Succeed on an 15. Now with the meal made they can eat it and gain the added benefi  ts of +1d6 Temporary Hit Points from the Steak Slice and Advantage on Intimidation (Charisma) rolls for 1 hour.

Daellash Elver

Length - 2 ft. 3 in.

Clustered  with  its  hatchling  kin,  the  young  daellash burrow their long bodies into the soft sand, patiently waiting to snatch prey in a coordinated effort

- Th ere are no restrictions to what ingredients a Traveler uses. In fact, it is encouraged for them to try and experiment and name their own recipes. Whether the Traveler knows the benefi  ts of an ingredient is up to the Keepers discretion.
-  Benefi  ts  from  new  added  ingredients  are  accumulative.  Any ingredients  that  give  the  same  exact  benefi  t  are  not,  except Temporary Hit Points.
-  Th e lasting duration of the meal's benefi  ts differ based on the result of the Meal Preparation roll.
- Rolled more than +3 the target DC: Th e benefi  ts last for 2 hours · Rolled more than +6 the target DC: Th e benefi  ts last for 3 hours · Critical Success: Th e benefi  ts last for 1 day
-  Failing  the  Meal  Preparation  roll  results  in  the  time  and Scavenged  Components  to  be  lost.  A  Critical  Failure  results  in a Poisoned Meal .  Any  creature  that  consumes  a  Poisoned  Meal must succeed on a DC 16 Constitution Saving Th row or take 2d6
- Poison damage.

<!-- image -->

## Harvesting from the Land &amp; Creating a Maker's Kit Crafting for Survival

Travelers exploring the primal wilds of Sol'Kesh journey into lands that have never known the step of an intelligent being for eons, to deal with such a natural setting, the many of amenities and supplies needed to survive must be crafted in the field, and by their own hands. Crafting in Sol'Kesh does not rely on villages, merchants or a bustling economy, but instead on the Travelers Knowledge , Components , and Time to create what they want from the bounties of the natural environment and creatures within.

## Knowledge

The  flora  and  fauna  of  Sol'Kesh  have  adapted  unique  forms  to thrive within their ecological niches. Unless a Traveler wants to test  the  toxicity  of  every  mushroom  they  come  across  the  hard (and  potentially  lethal)  way,  many  of  these  natural  Resources require  deep,  careful  study  to  discover  their  potential  use.  A Traveler may gain this wisdom either through discovering written texts or recipes, discussing with knowledgeable NPCs or studying it  themselves.  A  Wisdom  (Survival)  or  Intelligence  (Knowledge) check can ascertain what kind of items may be created with any given  Component.  For  Scavenged  Components,  the  DC  is  the same as the Scavenging DC found on the creature's stat block. While foraged, fished or trapped Components are set to DC 10.

## Components

Sol'Kesh's  crafted  items  are  unique,  rather  primitive,  never seen  before  amalgamations  of  gathered  resources  and  animalr remains.  The  Components  needed  per  item  are  listed  on  their Crafting Guide (pg. 260). These Components can be found in a multitude of ways befitting the Keeper's story, but an ambitious and self-reliant Traveler may obtain what they need by Hunting, Foraging, Setting Snares , Scavenging and Fishing .

## Scavenging Guidelines

Upon  slaying or chancing  upon  the  corpse of a creature, every  Traveler  present  will  have  an  opportunity  to Scavenge it  for Components ,  this  can  take  the  form  of  a  Survival  check when  harvesting  directly  from  the  body,  or  a  Nature  check when  searching  nearby  for  eggs  or  other  cast  off  items.  It  is recommended that the Keeper describe the components that may be Scavenged from the carcass without mentioning the Difficulty Class (DC) of the roll.

-  A  Keeper should mention what components are available as a Traveler must mention which Component they wish to Scavenge before attempting the roll.
- The corpse of each creature or defeated swarm may be attempted to be Scavenged for a total of 3 times , while apex creatures are a total of 6 , regardless of which Traveler has attempted it.
-  Creatures  found one hour after  their  death  will  not  yield  any Consumable  Components.  A  Traveler  may  ascertain  the  age of  a  corpse  by  doing  a  DC  14  Wisdom  (Perception)  or  Wisdom (Survival) check.
- Failures to Scavenge are not to be taken likely, as the spilled blood and time spent harvesting within the wilds of Sol'Kesh may attract carrion. If attempts to Scavenge a creature result in two failures in a row, the Keeper must then roll on the Scavengers Table of the specific biome that the Travelers are in, and if any creature results are rolled, immediately begin a combat encounter.

## Time

From a hastily chipped arrowhead to a finely crafted set of armor, creation takes time.

Time spent crafting can take the form of either:

- Focused Crafting - Actions such as hammering copper, carving bone, anything that takes active effort to perform is considered Focused Crafting as this leaves little else to do but the task at hand.
- Patient Crafting - Once prepared, many items take time before they are ready to be used. Brews ferment, water-logged coral dries, glue dries and many others simply take days to set. A Traveler can craft during their rests over many days of adventuring, hideaway their  items  as  it  readies,  or  spend  time  during  downtime  to prepare many long term items.

## Component Examples

Hatching - 4 Days Crevice Roe

Consumable Steak Slice

There are seven key elements to consider when using Crafting.

- Maker's  Kits  have  a  set  of Supply Charges that  are  expended through  use.  When  a  Maker's  Kit  has  zero  Supply  Charges  it cannot be used, but it is not destroyed, it is simple empty.
- Whether success or failure, the time needed for crafting is spent.

<!-- image -->

## The Maker's Kit

Self  sufficiency  is  key  to  survival,  and  the  Maker's  Kit  allows  a Traveler to craft far away from the trappings of civilization. While not  of  infinite  use,  the  Makers'  Kit  accounts  for  the  mundane resources  needed  to  craft  the  unique  items  of  Sol'Kesh.  No Maker's Kit looks alike, whether it is a leather satchel filled to the brim with odds and ends, or a sturdy  wooden cabinet  with  an organized set of tools, the Maker's Kit is truly a representation of the Travelers own sense of style and organization.

## Creating or Resupplying a Maker's Kit

The wilds of Sol'Kesh are nothing if not bountiful, and all that a Traveler needs to create or resupply a Maker's Kit is available with the right amount of time and cunning.

-  A Traveler may spend 1 day foraging in any biome, alone or in a party to craft their own Maker's Kit or replenish their current Maker's Kits Supply Charges .
-  The wilds are never safe when rummaging for supplies, at the end of the day, the Keeper rolls on the Scavengers Table of  the specific biome that the Travelers are in. If any creature results are rolled, immediately begin a combat encounter.
- If the Travelers survive the encounter, they then roll a 1d8, adding their  Wisdom  (Survival)  Modifier,  this  result  is  the  amount  of Supply  Charges they  receive  for  their  Maker's  Kit.  A  Maker's Kit  has  a  max  amount  of  Supply  Charges  equal  to  4  times  the Traveler's Proficiency Bonus.
-  Maker's  Kits  and  their  use  are  personal  to  a  Traveler,  and  its recommended that they may not be shared among other Traveler's and cannot be sold.

<!-- image -->

Heartberry Brew

- If an item requires multiple days of crafter, whether Focused or Patient, the days need not be consecutive.
- The following Tools are readily available within the Maker's kit to create any items that require:

Artisan Tools

Alchemist's Supplies

Brewer's Supplies Carpenter's Tools Cobbler's Tools Glassblower's Tools Leatherworker's Tools Potter's Tools Smith's Tools Weaver's Tools

Woodcarver's Tools

- While  the  Tools  needed  to  craft  are  automatically  supplied, the Proficiency to use the specific Tools are still required for the Traveler  to  know.  Having  the  specific  Tool  Proficiency  adds  a bonus to the Crafting roll.
- Failing to reach the DC of the crafted item will result in the time needed to craft being spent. Failing a second time will result in the Components being destroyed.
- When a Traveler is ready to craft, they roll a 1d20 and consult the chart below for added modifiers to their roll.

The viscous drink of thick flesh chunks and egg juice tastes better than it looks at first glance. W hen you drink this potion, for  1  day  it  grants  an  immediate  2d10  Temporary  Hit  Points  above  your maximum HP. In addition, it grants +2 to your Constitution and Advantage on all Constitution Saving Throws. While you feel fortified, there's a noticeable feeling of lethargy and for the duration of the potion's effect, it imposes Disadvantage on Initiative rolls.

## Journaling a Traveler's Discoveries

For Intrepid Explores &amp; Researchers

The  island  of  Sol'Kesh,  with  its  coral-strewn  shores  and  single  dominating  mountain  range  is  a  land  untouched  and  unknown. Encouraging Travelers to set their footprint upon it to explore its depths and discover all the beauty and danger that this primal land has to offer.

Researching the flora and fauna that thrive within these lands is not only for bookish scholars, understanding the perils one may endure while stepping into the thick mire of a mile wide swamp is vastly more safe when one knows which creatures lurk beneath the murky waters, and how to avoid them. Travelers that keep notes on their discoveries, who share their findings with others, and strive to understand the life that surrounds them often have a way of staying alive long enough to benefit from their research.

A Traveler's Journal can take on many forms: A loose bunch of scribbled notes - more mud and blood than ink, a hard-bound tome full of illustrations and meticulous script, or carved pictograms in the soft underside of argustaur shell. What is important is that the Travelers note their findings and learn from them. In some unfortunate cases, these dirt soiled papers are all that's left in a mouldering pile of bone and traveling gear. While an ignoble end for the writer, another Traveler chancing upon these notes would not only be warned of the dangers that await them, but can build off of them to learn more about the creatures that dwell nearby.

Writing a Creature Journal Entry is more than a narrative activity for a Traveler to take part in, writing and finishing creature Journal Entries impart benefits in dealing with the subject creature in both aggressive and peaceful ways. Traveler's Journal's can be completed in all manner of ways, this could be a shared endeavor between a party of Travelers, a singular quest of knowledge, an accumulation of notes found in dusty libraries or inscribed from the ramblings of madmen. If a Traveler wants to write a creature Journal Entry, they should discuss their goals with their Keeper, and together work out the best method to complete it. The most important aspect to keep in mind, is that this is about exploration and potentially benefiting from their discoveries.

| Journal Entry Completion   | Challenge                                                                                                     | Roll                                     | Accumulative Benefit                                                                                        |
|----------------------------|---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|------------------------------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
|                            | Initial discovery of the creature's existence, either through personal contact or second hand knowledge       | -                                        | Can begin creating the Journal Entry                                                                        |
|                            | Spending 1 hour, actively observing the creature in its natural habitat or examining its corpse               | Perception check (Wis) DC10+Creature'sCR | Advantage on Stealth checks against the creature                                                            |
|                            | Spending 1 hour actively sketching or documentinganin-depthreportonthe creature within the Traveler's journal | Nature check (Int) DC12 + Creature'sCR   | Advantage on Survival checks towards the creature that include actions such as setting Traps and Tracking   |
|                            | Encounteringincombatorperforming a successful Animal Handling check                                           | -                                        | Advantage on Intimidation and Charisma checks against the creature type                                     |
|                            | Defeating the creature in combat or fully befriending the creature                                            | -                                        | Advantage on Animal Handling checks and Scavenging rolls against the creature                               |
|                            | Upon completing all of the above: Spending 1 hour writing or drawing to complete the creature's journal page  | Nature check (Int) DC14 + Creature'sCR   | Unlockscreature Taming .Addedbonus for Hunting, Fishing and Trapping expeditions that focus on the creature |

## Additional Traveler's Journal Completion Notes

Besides  the  first  and  last  Journal  Completion  steps,  all  other Journal Completion steps can be completed in any order.

If a Traveler fails to complete a step, by either failing to succeed the DC roll or a Journal Completion challenge, they may attempt again, but only with a new encounter of the same creature type.

A Traveler will automatically complete a Journal Entry on a species if it successfully hatches and raises a creature to maturity.

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

<!-- image -->

## Taming the Primal Spirit

Caring for Eggs &amp; Raising Beasts

What better way for a Traveler to learn about the life around them, then to take an active part in raising the creatures that make up its diverse ecosystem. While many beasts are live-born, or discovered as a juvenile and raised through either Bounding or Control. Those that lay eggs, such as arthropods, amphibians, avians, lizards, fish, and more, can allow a Traveler with the means to hatch them, a life long companion, and in some instances create a true familial bond.

## Hunting For Eggs

Unlike Scavenging, searching for a creature's unborn young does not require the death of the creature. Simply being in an area that is known to host the animal and their kin, is enough for a Traveler to attempt an Intelligence (Nature) check, the DC being entirely unique to each creature and found on their Statblock.

Once an egg is found, being plucked from its natural environment can result in a premature death for the young if not taken care of quickly. Waterborne eggs from the Strands or Fenlands must be  kept  within  a  container  of  salt  or  fresh  water  immediately, lest they expire within 10 minutes of exposure. Furthermore, any discovered eggs must be placed in a biome specific incubator or it will spoil and kill the young within.

Failing  to  find  an  egg  does  not  result  in  any  risks.  Although  a Keeper may want to consider introducing an encounter from its protective  parents,  whether  or  not  an  egg  is  found  if  the  story warrants it.

## Hatching and Caring

The work is far from done once the creature emerges from its egg. The  time  it  takes  for  young  to  hatch  and  reach  maturity  varies from species to species, and is found listed on their Statblock as Seasons (4 seasons per year). When the creature reaches maturity it  will  already  be  considered  Tamed.  In  addition,  such  handson experience allows a Traveler to fully complete that creature's Journal Entry.

This  time  spent  nuturing  the  creature  can  be  considered  a downtime  activity,  or  simply  having  the  creature  along  with them, if a safe and secure method of transport is possible. This is quite unique per creature and so it is up to the Keeper's discretion on what is possible.

## Crafting Incubators

From the minuscule daellash roe to the heavy rock-hard argustaur egg, each animal has particular methods to hatch and raise their young. While imperfect to capture each need, an incubator that is tailored to the biome in which the creature was found is a reliable enough  method  to  hatch  an  egg  safely.  Incubators  are  often roughly 3 ft. long, 1 ft. wide and 1 ft. high. They may only incubate one egg at a time, and can be used indefinitely once crafted.

| DC                   | Rarity               | Crafting Time   | Tool Proficiency   | Components Needed   |
|----------------------|----------------------|-----------------|--------------------|---------------------|
| 14                   | Common               | Focused 1 Day   | Woodcarver's Tools | Seaweave Bundle     |
| Strands • Tidal Tank | Strands • Tidal Tank |                 | Supplies Used 2    | Supplies Used 2     |

An open drift wood case filled with salt water, a bed of sandy dirt along the bottom helps root strands of seaweave that both filter and aerate the water for the continued health of the egg that rests within.

| 10                         | Common                     | Weaver's Tools   | Bone Stalk Reeds   |
|----------------------------|----------------------------|------------------|--------------------|
| Farplains • Snug Incubator | Farplains • Snug Incubator | Supplies Used 1  | Supplies Used 1    |

A woven basket of bone stalks, packed with clay to absorb the heat of the sun. Cover the bottom in dry grass and allow the egg to stay warm and toasty. Keep it outdoors, unless it begins to rain.

| 13 Common             | Focused 1 Day Potter's Tools Vial of Vigael   |
|-----------------------|-----------------------------------------------|
| Fenlands • Cold Basin | Supplies Used 2                               |

A kiln-fired clay vessel, filled with cold fresh water, layered with a thick slop of mud and a healthy dousing of vigael oil. It is recommend to push the Fenland the eggs deeply within the mud.

| 10                                 | Common                             | Focused 1 Day                      | Woodcarver's Tools   | Dropmoss Bundle   |
|------------------------------------|------------------------------------|------------------------------------|----------------------|-------------------|
| Wealdwoods • Leaf Litter Terrarium | Wealdwoods • Leaf Litter Terrarium | Wealdwoods • Leaf Litter Terrarium | Supplies Used 1      | Supplies Used 1   |

A  hollowed  out  log,  capped  on  each  end  with  a  wooden  plug  and  filled  with dropmoss. Lay the egg onto the soft moss and layer dry leaves on top until the egg is fully covered and nestled within.

| 13 Common Focused         | 2 Days          | Mason's Tools   |
|---------------------------|-----------------|-----------------|
| Umbrawells • Stone Casket | Supplies Used 2 | Supplies Used 2 |

Carve a cavity within a chunk of stone and brush a thin layer of infernoil so that the natural sulphuric fumes may suffuse the tightly confined space. Place an egg into the cold stone and tightly close the lid, allowing the darkness to envelope the growing life form.

<!-- image -->

The  cloying  scent  of  sea  salt  wafts  heavily  from  the  driftwood  crate, incubating a large goliarch egg that rests upon a bed of sand. While the seaweave naturally oxygenate the water, a Traveler should make sure to trim the plants from merging too much and blocking the sunlight from alighting on the budding crustacean.

In the wilds of Sol'Kesh, whether it is the darkened tunnels of the Umbrawells or the wide open vistas of the Farplains, the creatures that roam are often coming across a Traveler for the first time in their ancestral memory. With instincts unable to process such an novel encounter, Travelers are regularly met with curiosity as much as they are met with hostility. While the temperament and natural behavior of many creatures differ wildly, like the keenly observant calitross, to the near mindless rot eater tormull, most creatures are capable of a form of familiarity and training. Though the means to do so must be tailored to the type of beast./.

<!-- image -->

## Taming Guidelines

There are certain prerequisites and rules to successfully Tame the wild creatures of Sol'Kesh. As well as bonuses to fill the segments.

-  A  Traveler  must  know  as  much  as  they  can  before  entirely trusting the animal, and so must Fully Complete a Journal Entry on the creature type before attempting to tame one of its kind.
-  Not  all  creatures  are  tamed  the  same  way,  each  creature  will state whether they are open to Bond or Control in their statblock. For example, a grahknash is stated as Control 8, meaning it has a Taming Gauge of 8 segments.
-  A  creature's  Taming  Gauge  segment  length  is  determined by  their  Bond/Control  amount  (to  a  minimum  of  1)  +  their Intelligence stat number.
-  Taming  a  creature  is  an  intense  activity  and  attempting  to complete each segment takes a full active day to perform.
- A creature can be tamed by two methods, determined by their Taming Stat on their statblock. To complete a Bond Segment a Traveler  must  succeed  in  an  Animal  Handling  Check,  while  a Control Segment requires a successful Intimidation Check. It is important to tame a creature in a similar fashion. For example, if a creature has any completed segments in Bond, and the traveler performs a Control action, this contradictory taming method will erase any existing progress in the Bond segments.
- When a creature is successful Tamed, it can then be used as a mount, a reliable ally, and understands basic spoken commands. There  is  no  limit  to  how  many  creatures  a  Traveler  can  tame.
-  It  is  important to note that Control is not a form of abuse or punishment  towards  the  creatures,  but  instead  means  that the Traveler is asserting themselves as a leader from a place of personal strength and force of will.

<!-- image -->

## Taming Example

In the above chart, a Traveler is attempting to tame a grahknash, though  fierce,  this  creature  is  open  to  both  Bond  and  Control methods. As the grahknash is a CR 6 creature with an intelligence of  2,  this  gives  it  a  total  Taming  Value  of  8.  Once  all  segments are  complete  the  creature  will  then  be  the  ally  of  the  traveler permanently.

| BondBonuses                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Segment Additions   |   Segment Additions | Control Bonuses                                                                                                                                                                     |
|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|---------------------|---------------------|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| Offering the creature its favored food                                                                                                                                                                                   |                     |                  +1 | The Traveler is a size larger than the creature they are taming                                                                                                                     |
| Attempting a contested Charisma (Persuasion) check with the creature to prove you mean no harm                                                                                                                           |                     |                  +1 | Attempting a contested Strength check with the creature in melee without the use of lethal force                                                                                    |
| Familiarizing the creature to your presence. A Traveler rolls an additional Charisma (Animal Handling) check, if they fail, the creature responds aggressively and lowers the Bond Segment Gauge by 1, to a minimum of 0 |                     |                  +1 | Prove your dominance. A Traveler rolls an additional Charisma (Intimidation) check, if they fail, the creature resists and lowers the Control Segment Gauge by 1, to a minimum of 0 |

<!-- image -->

## Evolving Your Companion

Playing, Guiding and Riding a Tamed Creature

## Becoming the Beast

Once a creature is Tamed it becomes a permanent ally. Whether this  relationship  is  one  of  friendship  or  domination  is  entirely up to the Traveler and how they choose to treat the creatures and world around them.

While the  creatures  do  not  understand  any  language,  they  will follow  simple  commands  from  their  Traveler,  either  verbally or  through gestures, and with time, the creatures will begin to understand the directives of their Traveler like second nature.

The  choice  of  whether  or  not  the  Traveler  or  the  Keeper  fully controls the Tamed creature as a Playable Character of their own, is up to a discussion with the Keeper and who they think is best suited to be responsible roleplaying the creature.

<!-- image -->

## Riding Tamed Creatures

To ride the winds above the summits, to skim over white foaming waves, to strike as one in the heat of the hunt. There is no greater form of kinship with a beast than riding together.

There  are  certain  guidelines  into  riding  a  Tamed  creature  and prerequisites to be met before one can do so.

-  Only  creatures  that  have  been  Tamed  can  be  ridden  and  the creature must be a size larger than the Traveler.
-  Mounting  a  creature  costs  10  ft.  of  the  Traveler's  movement speed, while mounted, a Traveler cannot move, but can use 10 ft. of  its  movement  to  Dismount.  A  creature  with  0  ft.  movement cannot Mount or Dismount.
-  If  an  effect  moves  the  Traveler  and  their  mount,  or  they  get the  Prone  condition  while  mounted,  they  can  be  forcefully dismounted.  The  Traveler  must  succeed  on  a  DC  10  Dexterity Saving  Throw  or  fall  off  the  mount,  landing  Prone  in  a  space within 5 ft. of it.
- In order for a Traveler to fully control a Tamed creature during the chaos of combat, at the Initiative roll step, the Traveler must roll  a  Animal  Handling  check  of  DC  10  +  the  creatures  CR  to  a minimum of 1. If the Traveler fails, the creature will resort to its base Feral Instincts as either Aggressive , Defensive , or Wary as is listed on their statblock. The Traveler can attempt to control their beast as again as an Action on their turn.

Bonuses may be added to this Animal Handling Check

- Add 1 to the roll for every Tamed level of the Creature · Add 1 to the roll if the Tamed Creature has a crafted saddle
- Both the Traveler and the Mounted Tamed Creature act during the same initiative order, and the Traveler may choose which of them would take Actions first. A Traveler or Tamed creature must complete all their own Actions before the next can act.

## A Guiding Hand

A Tamed Creature that has survived the perils of a Traveler's life may gain experience in a similar manner to a Playable Character. For  each  level  gained  from  accumulated  Experience  Points,  the Tamed Creature rolls on the Creature Augmentations table. If a creature rolls for an Augment that aligns closely with one it may have naturally, or it rolls a previous Augment, it may re-roll.

| +1 TamedCreature Advancement   | +1 TamedCreature Advancement   | +1 TamedCreature Advancement   |
|--------------------------------|--------------------------------|--------------------------------|
| Experience Points              | Level                          | Ability Score Improvement      |
| 0 xp                           | 1                              | 0                              |
| CRx200xp                       | 2                              | +1                             |
| CRx400xp                       | 3                              | +1                             |
| CRx800xp                       | 4                              | +1                             |
| CRx1600 xp                     | 5                              | +2                             |

Example:  A  CR  6  Grahknash  with  a  Taming  Level  of  1  would  need  to accumulate 1200 xp before reaching a Taming Level of 2.

Level 2 = 1200 xp (CR6 x 200)

-  Tamed  Creatures  have  an  amount  of  Hit  Dice  equal  to  their level. With dice that corresponds to their creature size: Small 1d6, Medium 1d8, Large 1d10, Huge 1d12, Gargantuan 1d20.
- If a Tamed Creature drops to 0 hit points, they are considered unconscious and are treated in the same manner as a Traveler in regards to Death Saving Throws and Stabilizing.
-  Reviving  a  creature,  though  unnatural,  is  possible  with  the Keepers discretion on the means to do so. If a cost is required, than it is recommend as a cost of 1000 GP x the CR Level (to  a minimum of 1) of the creature.

## Feral Instincts

Although there is a level of trust afforded to a Tamed creature, as control slips, their bestial nature takes over and they fall back to  their  instinctual  behaviors.  While  not  hostile  to  the  Traveler or their allies, all control is lost until combat is complete or the Keeper deems it calm enough to retain control. On the creature's statblock, they will be labeled as one of three Feral Instincts.

Aggressive On its  turn,  the  creature  moves  towards  an  enemy, attempts to attack and will not Disengage willingly from combat. If mounted, the Traveler may guide the creature towards enemies but may not force the creature to Disengage from combat.

Defensive The creature hunkers down or is to dim-witted to act. On its turn, the creature will refuse to move or attack, but can adopt any defensive abilities that it may have. If mounted, it will refuse to move but can fight back if attacked.

Wary On its turn, the creature attempts to flee from any enemies. If  in  combat,  the  creature  will  take  the  Disengage  action,  and move  as  far  as  possible  from  any  aggressors.  If  mounted,  the Traveler can guide the creature's direction of its flight, as long as its putting more distance between itself and its enemies.

| Creature Augmentations   | Creature Augmentations   | Creature Augmentations                                                                                                                                                                                                             |
|--------------------------|--------------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|

| 2                        | Venerable                | Increase one ability score by 2                                                                                                                                                                                                    |

## Rise of an Alpha

While the eternal mutations of evolution are imperceivable in a life time, there are certain individuals, that stand taller, run faster and lead the packs to a richer season. In the wilds, it is not long before an alpha of the pack will rear its head, or the strongest hunter slaughters its competition, and so this advantage a creature may have over its kin can be represented by an Augmentation .

To add to the uniqueness of an encounter, a Keeper can bestow upon a individual or excessively powerful breed of creatures an Alpha Augment by using the list above. There is not limit to the amount Augmentations a creature may have, as it is the Keeper's choice and story to tell.

<!-- image -->

## The Path into the Wilds

Unique Background &amp; Feats

## Traveler Background

Ever since you could take your first steps, you heeded the call to wander. Each distant horizon only promising more to explore. Nothing and no one could hold you back and you have learned to sustain yourself in the primal wilds, living off the land and thriving. You are a Traveler, and home is the welcoming crackle of a warm fire under the stars.

Ability Scores:

Constitution, Dexterity, Wisdom

Feat: Primal Scavenger

Skill Proficiencies:

Perception and Survival

Tool Proficiency:

Maker's Kit (pg. 247)

Equipment: Fishing Rod, Explorer's Pack, Hand Axe, Iron Pot, Quarterstaff, Tent, Traveler's Clothes

## Sol'Kesh Feats

Skilled Scaler · General Feat (Prerequisite: Level 4+, Strength 10+) Ability  Score  Increase Increase  your  Strength  score  by  1,  to  a maximum of 20.

## Origin Feats

Primal Scavenger

Skilled  Harvester You  roll  with  Advantage  when  Scavenging  a creature for Components (pg.246).

Weathered You  have  Advantage  against  any  weather  related hazard Saving Throws.

## General Feats

Wild Kin · General Feat (Prerequisite: Level 4+, Wisdom 13+) Ability  Score  Increase Increase  your  Wisdom  score  by  1,  to  a maximum of 20.

Kin  Points You  have  access  to  Kin  Points  which  you  can  spend to  gain  Advantage  on  Wisdom  (Animal  Handling)  checks.  Your maximum  amount  of  Kin  Points  are  equal  to  your  Wisdom Modifier  and  you  regain  your  expended  Kin  Points  when  you finish a Long Rest.

Feral Trust · General Feat (Prerequisite: Level 4+, Charisma 12+) Ability  Score  Increase Increase  your  Charisma  score  by  1,  to  a maximum of 20.

Natural Rider You have Advantage on checks made to control or guide a mount.

Protective You  can  use  your  Reaction to redirect an attack that targets your mount to yourself instead, as long as you are within 5 ft. of your mount.

Wayward · General Feat (Prerequisite: Level 4+, Dexterity 13+) Ability  Score  Increase Increase  your  Dexterity  score  by  1,  to  a maximum of 20.

Bound to the Soil Choose one type of biome: Coastal (Strands), Desert/Fields/Mountains (Farplains), Forest/Jungle (Wealdwoods), Swamp/Wetland (Fenlands), or Underdark/ Subterranean (Umbrawells). You gain Proficiency in the Stealth skill of the chosen biome, and you can move at normal speed while moving stealthily.

Skilled  Cook Add  +  1  Bonus  Modifier  to  the  Meal  Preparation  roll. (pg. 245)

Strong Grip You gain a climb speed equal to your walking speed. If you already have a climb speed, increase it by 10 ft..

Wall  Leap As  a  Bonus  Action,  if  you  are  adjacent  to  a  vertical surface (such as a wall or cliff face), you can leap off the surface and jump up to half your movement in any direction. This movement does not provoke Opportunity attacks.

Hunters Sense · General Feat (Prerequisite: Level 4+,Wisdom 10+) Beast  Sense You  have  Advantage  on  Wisdom  (Survival)  checks related  to  Tracking,  and  can  recognize  a  creature's  scent  and distinguish  it  from  others  once  you've  interacted  with  it  or  its tracks in the past.

On The Trail Beasts have Disadvantage on Stealth checks made to hide from you, if you've tracked them within the last hour.

Unyielding · General Feat (Prerequisite: Level 4+, Constitution 10+) Ironwood  Stance When  you  take  Bludgeoning,  Piercing,  or Slashing damage, you can use your Reaction to reduce the damage by your Proficiency bonus.

Protector When an ally within 5 ft. takes damage, you can use your Reaction to move up to 5 ft. toward them without provoking Opportunity attacks.

Living  off  the  Land Add  ±1 to  the  range  result for  Hunting, Foraging, Setting Snares and Fishing Expeditions.

Steady Hand Automatically fill 1 Tamed Segment when beginning to Tame a creature.

Storytelling is the key to role playing games and a Traveler can explore their own story without the need of a Keeper to tell it. The following pages provide methods to explore the island of Sol'Kesh in Solo Play. Either by adventuring alone into the wilds, creating NPC animals and allies to accompany you, or to simply add a narrative goal to pursue. In addition, these tools can also provide a Keeper with more storytelling tools to tell a story for their adventuring Travelers.

## Journaling

The heart of Sol'Kesh is exploration, to discover uncharted lands and observe beasts in their natural habitat. A Traveler is more than just an adventurer, they are explorers, researchers, pioneers and more. As a Traveler experiences Solo Play, it is recommended that they keep a journal of their adventures. Not only does it include the benefits of the Journal Feature (pg. 248) it helps solidify the events  that  they  experience,  to  note  the  progress  they  made  in discovering new life, and help map out their exploration of the island itself.

## Journal Example

Third day of Spring's waning moon -

That old tattered recipe book I found in that beached ship wasn't wrong, when gathering dropmoss, bring a ladder. I spent the better part of the morning swatting flies that flew up from the Mirewynn the moment I left that blessed dry patch of dirt that was my campsite. Like a wall of green, the Tanglewood greeted me, and while the early morning sky was already bright with sunshine, all of the optimism that comes with that came to a grinding halt as I stepped into that gloom under the trees. I think I even startled a frondilli when I stepped on a branch a little too loudly...All I saw was a green furred tail disappear into the bush. After what felt like hours of walking, I finally spied that telltale gray moss hanging heavy of the boughs dozens of feet above me. With an audible sigh that I soon regretted once I remembered there's more than frondilli in these woods, I got to climbing. Thankfully my master's lessons in looping rope around the trunk still held merit, and before long I was high into the canopy and starting to remember the feeling of sunlight on my skin. That moment of peace was quickly forgotten as the tree began to shake. I could suddenly feel a steady deep thud coming from the distance and immediately worried that I was in the path of the Tanglewood's resident apex predator, an old grizzled jhotus. All the birds flying off away past the source was a warning I was not too foolish to ignore. Quickly cutting off a healthy bundle of the moss I descended with less grace than I want to admit, and immediately ran out of those woods, not wanting to take my chances with the terrifyingly huge jhotus catching my scent.

## Expeditions and Environments

Within the pages of this book can be found the means to explore a  myriad of encounters and daily quests. A Traveler can choose to  begin  their  adventure  by  selecting  from  the Job  Board (pg. 257). Can choose an area on the Island Map (pg. 9) or roll on any of the five Biome's Expedition Tables, to either Forage , Hunt , Set Snares and Fish , or simply venture out and risk their survival in the primal wilds.

When  experiencing  Solo  Play  and  embarking  on  any  of  these potential  adventures,  it  is  recommended  for  a  Traveler  to  roll on the Biome's Weather Table per  day of travel, and roll on the Scavengers Table per  biome  for  every  hour  in  the  wilds,  as  the roaming hungry predators are always on the look out for a meal and  the  randomness  of  nature  can  lend  a  level  of  danger  and variation to the session.

## Beastly Behavior

The  creatures  that  call  Sol'Kesh  home  are  more  than  mindless encounters  and  are  meant  to  naturally  coexist  within  their environment.  Not  all  creatures  are  out  to  rip  apart  a  Traveler savagely,  and  in  many  cases  fear  the  strange  intruder.  When encountering a creature in Solo Play, a  Traveler  can  roll  on  the Beast Behavior Table (pg. 256) to determine how the creature will react to their presence, as each creature has a listed behavior type in how they react to a Traveler.

## The Path Taken

While some may want to forge ahead entirely alone on this island of savage beasts, journeying through Sol'Kesh in Solo Play does not necessarily mean the Traveler must do so alone and unaided, and can involve Non Playable Characters (NPCs) along the way to aid a Traveler on their quest. Below is a list of potential narrative plots that could help add substance and story to your path. Yet the most important tool in your kit will always be your own creativity.

-  Add  NPC  characters  taken  from  5e's  Monster  Manual,  from Commoners, Scouts, Warriors to Cultists. Give these characters a name and consider a backstory of why and how they are on the island with the Traveler. If they're lucky, they may survive a whole season in these wilds.
-  Fast  forward  the  process  of  Taming  a  creature  and  add  a wayward  beast  to  the  Traveler's  side  immediately.  Perhaps  an orphan vinnirex chanced upon them when they washed ashore, or protective skyelarx spied them from afar and is guarding over them and their perilous journey.
- The Traveler is part of a crew that came across these uncharted coasts  in  a  vessel  fitting  exploration,  whether  from  across  the waves in a ship or across the stars in a spelljammer ship. Together they venture out on expeditions to discover the lay of the land.
- Sol'Kesh is merely a newly discovered island within an existing fantasy world, people have flocked to its shores to set up a colony of sorts. Through hardship and grit they have built a community to grow and call this island a new home.
- Eschewing all fantasy magic, the Traveler is a human from our modern Earth  and  has  skipped  forward  in  time  to  82  millions years to the distant future. Alone and completely cut off from the remains of humanity, they must survive in this new era far from the modern world they once knew.

<!-- image -->

## Experiencing a Living Island

Creature Behavior Modifi  cations

## Aggressive

Wary of all but their own species, an aggressive creature will use any  opportunity  to  prove  dominance  over  its  territory.  When not  in  combat  they  will  use  intimidation  to  aggrandizing  their prowess with shows of strength and size. Th is can take the form of standing tall to look imposing, stamping their feet, or making loud noises to display their might.

Behavior Modifi  cation - Aggressive creatures roll with Advantage if its size is larger than the Traveler(s).

- 1: Disadvantage on attack rolls
- 2-4: -10 ft. to its Movement Speed
- 5-8: No modifi  cation
- 9-11: Raise AC by 1
- 12: Advantage on attack rolls

## Defensive

Often  more  concerned  with  fi  nding  food,  or  simply  just  not intelligent enough to notice anything outside its natural instinctual triggers, a defensive creature often has little reaction besides protecting itself when met with any other species.

Behavior  Modifi  cation  -  Defensive  creatures  do  not  react  to  a Traveler's size.

- 1: Lower AC by 1
- 2-4: Disadvantage on Wisdom (Perception) checks
- 5-8: No modifi  cation
- 9-11: Advantage on Constitution checks
- 12: Raise AC by 1

## Wary

Naturally wary of all species not of their own, a wary creature's fi   rst instinct is to escape any encounter, whether the other species is friendly or not. While susceptible to Animal Handling or other forms of appeasement through offerings of food, they will often attempt to escape any attempt of being approached. Only through a calm persistence will they eventually feel at ease around a new species.

Behavior Modifi  cation - Wary creatures roll with Disadvantage if its size is smaller than the Traveler(s).

- 1: Disadvantage on Dexterity Saving Th rows
- 2-4: Disadvantage on Charisma (Intimidation) Saving Th rows
- 5-8: No modifi  cation
- 9-11: +10 Movement Speed
- 12: Advantage on Dexterity Saving Th rows

<!-- image -->

Brine Maiden Length - 25 ft.

The  jellyfish's  mantle  contains a glowing bubble of bacteria, the  methane  produced  by  them allows  the  jellyfish  to  regulate its buoyancy in the deepest underground lakes within the Umbrawells. Like most jellyfish the creature contains little to no nutritional value, and aquatic predators  use  them  more  for the gentle light they emit in the black water, than any  actual sustenance.

Location

## Job Board Quests

## Job Posting

Th ey call them mindless beasts but my korruk Pokku is family! Something has made him sick, his scales are all gray and I'm scared he's going to die. Please if there's anyone reading this, he needs some vigael and a Traveler to fi  nd out what's wrong! Job: Administer a vial of vigael to the ailing korruk and assess what has made it sick. Success: Heal and discover the medical issue.

Scenario: Travel to a small Farplains village to see to the sick korruk. Give the korruk one vial of vigael and perform a DC 16 Intelligence (Medicine) check. Success: Th e korruk heals 1d6 Hit Points and the Traveler fi  gures out the thistlespire roots that it has been eating are tainted with a fungus. Failure: Th e korruk reacts violently and attacks the Traveler. It is recommended that the Traver uses non-lethal force to subdue the beast.

Each dawn, herdsmen near the northern Wealdwoods of Last Bloom fi  nd another of their herd beasts slaughtered, leaving nothing but a half consumed carcass and large avian tracks into the woods. Please send help and hunt down this voracious predator!

Job: A single, uniquely large calitross is preying on herd animals in the dead of night. It is recommended to draw out the beast with live prey. Success: Kill or subdue the Grizzled Calitross.

Grizzled Calitross · Variant Additions: Size Medium, +1 AC, + 2 STR, +16 HP, Add 1 Damage Die to each of its attack types.

Lone Stalker Th e grizzled calitross stalker has Advantage on Dexterity (Stealth) checks made to Hide while within the Wealdwoods.

Numerous sightings of a pod of illithisk stalking the beach. Aggressively tearing up fi  shing nets, single manned vessels and attacking lone fi  shermen. Looking for a Traveler to either frighten them away or slay them for good.

Job: Six Brutal illithisk are seen at sundown, roaming around dangerously within 1 mile of a coastal village. Aggressively attacking any creatures on sight. Success: Slay or incapacitate all six Brutal Illithisk.

Variant Addition · Brutal Illithisk : +1 AC, +2 DEX, + 2 STR

Pod Aggressors Th e brutal illithisk has Advantage on an attack roll against a creature if at least one of its allies is within 5 ft. of the creature and the ally isn't Incapacitated.

Something's not right and us huntsmen need the studied eye of a Traveler to see what's goin on here. In the dead of the night, wild animals are being slaughtered by the cliffs, but not eaten. My hunters are calling the meat cursed and won't touch it. Either stop whats doin the killing or prove to my men it ain't no curse.

Job: A wayward acrynn is venturing out of the caves to slaughter whatever it can get its scythe-like arms on. Either wait for night or track it back to its lair. Success: Slay or incapacitate the Stalking Acrynn.

Stalking Acrynn · Variant Additions: + 4 INT, +10 Movement Speed

Multiattack Th e acrynn makes three attacks, using Scything Limbs and Mandibles in any combination. BONUS ACTION - Agile Killer Whenever the stalking acrynn successfully Attacks a creature, it may Dash. Th is movement does not provoke Opportunity attacks.

I heard tell of an oasis within the Grayfynn that shines like a sapphire in the sun. Our caravans can barely survive the trek with our stores of water and we're looking to chart a new trail. We'd be more than grateful if a Traveler could fi  nd and map out the location of this potential life saving oasis.

Job: Travel through the arid landscape to fi  nd the storied oasis. Success: Marking a map along the way to help tradesmen potentially use it.

Scenario: Search for the oasis in the wide open plains, DC 17 Wisdom (Survival) check. Th e Tracking roll can be attempted a total of three times. Success: Th e Traveler fi  nds the hidden oasis. Failure: Th e Traveler loses an hour of tracking and gains one level of Exhaustion. Upon discovery, the oasis is a wellspring of life of abundance, a Traveler attempting to fi  sh at the location can add ±2 to the range result on what fi  sh they catch. Th ough, this oasis is a safe haven for tartakka, a Traveler must be quiet and attempt a DC 10 Dexterity (Stealth) check or DC 10 Wisdom (Animal Handling) check every hour they spend at the oasis, otherwise 1d3 tartakka will become agitated and attack the Traveler.

My tough little vinnirex can't keep this up any longer, the khanith are getting bolder each day and I don't know if my bloomux herd will make it to harvest season. Looking for a Traveler to come help keep my herd alive and happy! Job: Defend a bloomux herd from an incoming khanith attack for a whole day. Success: At least 1 bloomux survives the day.

Scenario: Five bloomux graze on a fi  eld of flowers for a full day (12 hours). Two Tamed vinnirex accompany the Traveler and both the vinnirex and bloomux treat them as allies. Four separate waves of 1d6+3 khanith attack at random times throughout the length of the day.

I keep hearing the most mournful of cries coming from that cursed dark chasm, I can't imagine what could be making such a pitiful call for help. If there are any Travelers with a caring spirit to help those in pain, something out there needs you. Job: Track down and defend a wounded omaterssum against attacking skith. Success: Th e omaterssum survives the encounter.

Scenario: Track a wounded omaterssum into the caves, DC 15 Wisdom (Survival) check. Th e Tracking roll can be attempted a total of three times before the wounded omaterssum is slain. Success: Th e  Traveler fi  nds  the wounded omaterssum being attacked by 1d6+1 skith. Failure: Th e Traveler loses an hour of tracking and rolls on the Umbrawells Scavengers Table and fi  ghts any potential encounters.

Wounded Omaterssum : 30/75 HP remaining, will treat the Traveler as an ally if they attack the skith.

Our little village only survives because of our lexati. Something or someone has been taking them from their perches at night, please fi   nd them, I fear the worst!

Job: Search the whereabouts of the lost lexati within the Wealdwoods. Success: Free them before they are slain. Scenario: Follow the signs of struggle and listen for the cries of the distraught creatures, DC 12 Wisdom (Survival) check. Th e Tracking roll can be attempted a total of three times. Success: Th e Traveler fi  nds the lexati. Failure: Th   e Traveler loses an hour of tracking and rolls on the Wealdwoods Scavengers Table and fi  ghts any potential encounters. Th e lexati are found to be at the top of a massive tree deep in the Wealdwoods, 4d4 basilospri guard the lexati to feed to their young. Lexati will treat the Traveler as an ally and the basilospri will randomly attack either the Travelers or their captive lexati, when all basilospri are slain, the lexati will glide away back to the village.

No merchant caravans have visited our small isolated village in many moons. Worried, we traveled out along the trade roads and found a caravan waylaid and slaughtered. Long claw marks and dried blood are all the remained of the supplies.

Job: An especially aggressive grahknash is waylaying caravans with impunity. Success: Venture out and hunt down the beast before it strikes fi  rst.

Marauding Grahknash · Variant Additions: + 2 STR, +2 CON, +24 HP, +10 Movement Speed e marauding grahknash can move up to its speed toward a hostile creature that

BONUS ACTION - Voracious Th it can see.

A Traveler came through our town, speaking of a peaceful meadow with a mirror-like pond not far beyond our borders, a true safe haven from the dangerous wilds that surround us. We're requesting the help of another Traveler to journey out and create a map of this so called meadow.

Job: Travel through the ceaseless swamps to fi  nd the meadow. Success: Marking a map along the way to help the villagers know of its location.

Scenario: Search for the meadow among the marshes, DC 15 Wisdom (Survival) check. Th e Tracking roll can be attempted a total of three times. Success: Th e Traveler fi  nds the peaceful meadow. Failure: Th e Traveler loses an hour of tracking and is beset upon by 1d3 solmundis and fi  ghts these encounters. Upon discovery, the Traveler is overcome with a sense of serenity and peace as they step into a quiet clearing with a small group of gurubara basking in the water. Th e meadow's peace is soon shattered by an invading herd of 3d3 fennfi  stle. If the Traveler chooses to engage and combat the fennfi  stle, the gurubara will treat the Traveler as an ally in the encounter.

My tough little vinnirex can't keep this up any longer, the khanith are getting bolder each night and I don't know if my bloomux herd will make it to the season's harvest. Looking for a Traveler to come help keep my herd safe.

Job: Defend a bloomux herd from an incoming khanith attack for a full night. Success: At least 1 bloomux survives. Scenario: Five bloomux rest on a fi  eld of flowers for a full night. Two Tamed vinnirex accompany the Traveler, both the vinnirex and bloomux treat them as an ally. Four separate waves of 1d6+3 khanith attack throughout the length of the night.

Our waters have gone afoul, our fi  shermen bring home only rotten and decaying fi  sh, and a rank smell has sickened our folk. We need a Traveler to root out the source of this malady that is tainting our home.

Job: A strain of diseased ruske are poisoning the water. Success: Slay all 4d6 Pestilent Ruske.

Pestilent Ruske · Variant Additions: + 4 CON, +12 HP, Resistance: Poison

Infected Lance (replaces Chitin Lance) Melee Weapon Attack: +3 to hit, reach 5 ft., one target. Hit: 8 (2d6 + 1) Poison damage. If the ruske moved at least 10 ft. toward the target immediately before the hit, the target takes an extra 7 (2d6) Piercing damage.

Time and time again our caravans have been waylaid by a pack of voracious draffers. We need a strong arm and fi  erce guard to make our next trip.

Job: Stand atop a closed caravan and defend it from predatory draffers. Success: At least one pack beast must survive a full hour of travel.

Scenario: A pack beast drawn caravan is crossing the open plains of the Tundrus at 60 ft. per minute. Either use rutort or equivalent Beasts of Burden (horse, ox, etc.). When traveling begins, 1d6+2 draffers appear at the outskirts and run in to attack. Success: At least 1 Beast of Burden survives the full hour.

Umbrawells Splitspine Diffi   culty: Medium

Wealdwoods Arborne Diffi   culty: Medium

Strands Bastion Diffi   culty: Had

Wealdwoods Green Moot Diffi   culty: Hard

Strands Windward Diffi   culty: Hard

Wealdwoods Th ornden Diffi   culty: Hard

Someone has to fi  nd my daughter Daedriss! It has been three days since she wandered off from our village, she was last seen going west to the caverns at dawn and our best trackers have found nothing. Please we need someone before its too late. Job: Track down and bring home the child. Success: Daedriss survives the encounter and is brought back home to her village.

e

Scenario: Search the Umbrawells caves for signs of the missing child, DC 14 Wisdom (Survival) check. Th Tracking roll can be attempted a total of three times. Success: Th e Traveler fi  nds the girl within a cavern. Failure: Th e Traveler loses an hour of tracking and rolls on the Umbrawells Scavengers Table and fi  ghts any potential encounters. Upon discovery, the girl has been taken in by a family of 3d3 gorr, who are beset by an aggressive pack of 1d6+3 mollucite encroaching within their cave. Th e gorr and Daedriss will treat the Traveler as an ally if the Traveler combats the group of mollucite.

Th e ground has been rumbling something fi  erce this last fortnight and we loggers fear the worse. We need a Traveler to investigate what is happening within our glades.

Job: Barricade an opening in the earth with fallen logs. Success: Place eight logs over the hole to deter the invading tormull.

Scenario: Th e ground has been rumbling within a clearing in the woods. Eventually the ground gives way and tormull charge out of a 10 x 10 ft. round hole. Initially 1d4+1 tormull appear and roll a 1d4 every subsequent 6 minutes for more to escape the cave opening. Block the cave opening by placing 10 ft. long logs that lay in stacks around the clearing. DC 10 Strength test to pick up a log, while holding a log a Travelers speed is reduced to 5 ft. and they cannot use their arms while holding onto one.

Traveler needed. Every time we set sail those thrice-cursed crustaceans rip through our board and sail with their damnable stones. We can hardly feed our own if we cannot fi  sh beyond the surf.

Job: Defend a fi  shing vessel for a full hour on the open seas as they attempt to fi  sh. Success: At least 1 Fisherman survives the full hour.

Scenario: Every 10 minutes roll a 1d4, on a 1 an arkiopti appears to attack the fi  shing vessel. Six Fishermen are focused on casting their lines and nets. Arkiopti randomly attack Travelers and Fisherman on each turn of combat.

Fishermen

: Medium Humanoid, AC 10, Hp 4 (1d8), STR 10, DEX 10, CON 10, INT 10, WIS 10, CHA 10.

Never seen anything like it, ripped right through a family of aphistus before they could even squawk out a warning. I think it was a sacrissian from the look of those horns, but I've never seen one this deep in the Wealdwoods.

Job: An especially fi  erce sacrissian is upending the balance between predator and prey, either force it out from the Wealdwoods or slay it. Success: Slay or incapacitate the Savage Sacrissian.

Savage Sacrissian · Variant Additions: +2 AC, +3 WIS, +10 Movement Speed

Multiattack Th e sacrissian makes four attacks, it can use its Toothed Beak, Foreclaws or Crown of Horns in any combination.

We found a half starved and full mad sailor washed up ashore ranting about her priceless sextant, she was unsure of where her vessel crashed among the rocks and coral out at Windward. If a Traveler is looking to help, please bring back her precious tool. Job: Search for a wrecked scouting vessel. Success: Escape with the lost sextant.

Scenario: Search for the vessel out on the multitude of islands and coral outcroppings, DC 16 Wisdom (Survival) check.Th e Tracking roll can be attempted a total of three times. Success: Th e Traveler fi  nds the stranded scouting vessel. Failure: Th e Traveler loses an hour of tracking and rolls on the Strands Scavengers Table and fi  ghts any potential encounters. Upon discovery, the sextant can be found near the prow, but 1d3 nicalliath infest the vessel and will attack the Traveler on sight.

A sickening crimson taint has infected our grove, the trees bleed. All the animals, even our own pack beasts, have become wildly violent the moment they step into that bloody forest. Someone has to root out this disease before the whole of the Wealdwoods goes mad.

Job: A grove of trees has turned crimson and is weeping a blood colored sap. Creatures in the area are growing aggressive and will attack others on sight. Success: Investigate and remove what has caused this infection. Scenario: When entering the crimson grove, and every hour spent in the grove,  roll  on  the  Wealdwoods Scavengers Table and fi  ght any potential encounters. To discover what has caused the infection, roll a DC 16 Intelligence (Nature) check. Success: Th e Traveler discovers that a unique mushroom has infected one tree and once pulled from the trunk, the Wealdwoods will heal within a 1d4 days. Failure: Th e infection goes unchecked and all people leave the vicinity as the violent creatures are too lethal.

Crimson Fungus Infected Beasts ·  Variant  Additions:  Any  Beasts  within  100  ft.  of  the  infected  tree  will aggressively attack any species that is not their own.

Unreasoning Madness Advantage on Wisdom Saving Th rows.

Manic Rage Add their Profi  ciency Bonus to any Damage on melee attack rolls.

Senseless Anger Th ey gain Resistance to Bludgeoning, Piercing, and Slashing damage.

A thin rope loop, attached to a wooden support rod to capture prey.

Before crafting the Snare, determine which biome it is being set for: Strands, Farplains, Fenlands, Wealdwoods or Umbrawells.

Setting the Snare With a Utilize action you can set the snare onto a 5 ft. square Trap Space.

Snared Determining  what  was  snared  within  a  2  day  period  uses  the  Snare Expedition Table from the biome in which the snare was set in.

Disarming A Traveler can approach and disarm a snare, allowing it to be reused without the need of an ability check.

Bird Song With a Utilize Action you can attempt to mimic a bird song that you've heard with a DC 13 Charisma (Performance) check. The result of your success is the DC for other creatures to discern if the sound is authentic or not.
